# Derangiquarium — Architecture Doc

This file is the source of truth across phases. Each phase may be a fresh session — read this before writing code, and update the "Current Phase" section at the end of every phase.

## Vision

Insaniquarium, but the tank is one giant continuous world with a factory grid at the bottom. Top of the tank: classic feed-fish-watch-them-grow-and-drop-loot loop. Bottom of the tank: a player-built seabed grid (ramps, directional fans, collectors, an auto-feeder), every building anchored to a Platform, that automatically routes falling coins/waste/science into the right processor — a Fan can even push something back up into the water column instead of it being collected. Between the two: power (Electric Eels), tank toxicity/cleanliness, cybernetic gear crafted for individual fish, and — once Gene-Splicing is researched — dragging a utility fish onto another fish to breed a dual-role hybrid. Progression itself is diegetic: a Mound sitting on the seabed cracks open in stages as the player throws money at it, revealing each new tier of mechanics (see Tier Progression & The Mound below) rather than unlocking silently through a menu.

Arc of a level: manual and frantic → hands-off and humming.

**This is a campaign game, not a sandbox.** Levels are discrete and their state is wiped on transition. Meta progression (tech, unlocked buildings/species, completed levels) persists forever via LocalStorage. This split is architectural — see State Shape below — and exists from Phase 1 even though nothing persists yet until Phase 5 wires up save/load.

## State Shape & Field Ownership

Root state is one plain object, fully JSON-serializable — no class instances, no DOM refs, no closures, no `Map`/`Set` in any serialized path. Plain objects/arrays only.

```js
state = {
  meta: {                    // persists across levels, saved to LocalStorage (wired in Phase 5)
    scienceTotal,            // number — written by Systems.js (Phase 4)
    techUnlocked: [],        // string[] of tech ids — written by UI.js tech tree (Phase 4)
    buildingsUnlocked: [],   // string[] of grid tile ids — written by Levels.js on level completion
    speciesUnlocked: [],     // string[] of species ids — written by Levels.js on level completion
    levelsCompleted: [],     // string[] of level ids — written by Levels.js
    settings: {},            // sound toggle etc — written by UI.js (Phase 5)
  },
  level: {                   // wiped and rebuilt by Levels.js on every level load — nothing else may reset this
    levelId,                 // string — set once on load by Levels.js
    money,                   // number — written by Entities.js (coin bank) and UI.js (shop spend)
    cleanliness,             // number 0-100 — written by Systems.js (Phase 3), read by Entities.js for stress
    powerSupply, powerDemand,// numbers — written by Systems.js (Phase 3)
    entities: [],            // Fish/Alien instances (plain data) — written by Entities.js. A fish carries starTier (1-4, Economy Fish Combining — see that section) and dropValueOverride (null except on a hybrid produced by the T5 value-carry-over pipeline)
    items: [],                // Food/Coin/Waste/Science instances (plain data) — written by Entities.js, read/moved by Grid.js
    floatingTexts: [],        // transient "+$N" pickup readouts (plain data) — written by Entities.js, rendered by main.js; not physics items, Grid.js never touches these
    grid,                     // full WORLD_TILES_H x WORLD_TILES_W tile-id array (Phase 2) — written by Grid.js only; rows above SEABED_ROW_START always stay TILE_EMPTY
    buildingData: {},          // sparse "row,col" -> { type, angle, ... } map, written/read by Grid.js only — per-instance data a bare grid-cell type string can't hold (a Fan's aim angle, the Auto-Feeder's absorb/process state); see "Directional Fans"
    gridStats: { itemsRoutedTotal }, // lifetime count of items a Collector tile has consumed (Phase 2) — written by Entities.js on consumption, read by main.js/UI.js for the items-routed-per-minute debug stat
    tier,                     // number 1-5 (Phase 2) — Mound-driven progression, written by Mound.js's crackMound(); level-scoped like everything else here, resets to 1 on every level load/restart even though the meta-unlocks a previous crack granted stay permanent
    moundTeased,               // bool (Phase 2) — has the Mound's first (fake, no-op) crack attempt already happened this level? written by Mound.js's crackMound()
    notifications: [],        // rolling ticker log (Phase 2) — { id, text, elapsed }, written by any system with something to announce (Mound.js, Entities.js so far), read/rendered by UI.js, capped at NOTIFICATION_LOG_MAX
    tankPoints: { total, available }, // Phase 2 — earned by Entities.js on fish adult-growth transitions, spent in UI.js's Tank Upgrades panel; level-scoped, not meta — see CLAUDE.md's "Tank Points & Tank Upgrades"
    upgrades: { foodQuality, fishMovement, foodCapacity }, // Phase 2 — purchased Tank Upgrade levels (0 = not bought), written by UI.js's panel, read live by Entities.js
    waveTimer,                // number — written by Systems.js (Phase 5)
    elapsed,                  // number, ms since level start — written by Engine.js sim loop
  },
  camera: { x, y, zoom },     // written by Engine.js only; zoom is auto-fit to the water column height by main.js on load/resize
  ui: { selectedTool, shopCollapsed, tankPanelCollapsed, paused }, // selectedTool: which click-tool a canvas click performs ('food', 'demolish', or 'build:<tileId>'); shopCollapsed/tankPanelCollapsed/paused: toggled by UI.js (shop button/S key, tank button/P key, Escape/pause menu) — paused is read by main.js's update() to freeze the sim entirely; opening one of the two side panels auto-collapses the other
  debug: { ... },              // overlay toggles, time scale, selected species for G key — written by Engine.js/UI.js
}
```

**Rule:** any system that writes to `state.level` when it means `state.meta` (or vice versa) is a bug. This is called out with a comment at every level-load/reset site.

## SPECIES table schema & behavior tags

Species are data rows in `Config.js`, not subclasses. A single `Fish` entity in `Entities.js` is driven by a `SPECIES` table lookup keyed by species id.

Each `SPECIES` row provides:
- `id, name, tier, cost, description`
- `swimSpeed, hungerRate, growthStages[]` (each stage: `{ scale, dropInterval, dropValue, feedsRequired }`), `lifespan`
- `behavior: []` — array of behavior tags (composable)
- `dropType` — item type produced (e.g. `'coin'`, `'waste_cleared'`, `'power'`, `'science_blue'`)
- `unlockedByDefault: bool` — Tier 1 base species are `true` in Phase 1; every other row is scaffolded with `false` until its phase/mechanism grants it (see Species Roster below)
- `parents: [id, id]` — **hybrids only.** The two base species this one is spliced from; absent on all 6 base species. Phase 4's Gene-Splicing UI will use this to look up "what do these two produce," but nothing reads it before then — it's scaffolding, not behavior.

Behavior tags (composable, reused across species):
- `FEEDER` — eats food, drops items on a timer scaled by growth stage
- `SCAVENGER` — eats waste, drives tank cleanliness (Phase 3)
- `GENERATOR` — produces power (Phase 3)
- `RESEARCHER` — produces science near a Hub (Phase 4)
- `HUNTER` — attacks aliens *(not currently assigned to any species — held over from an early draft; Phase 5+ combat direction is undefined pending a future design pass, see Species Roster below)*
- `GRID_WALKER` — moves on seabed tiles rather than swimming *(also currently unassigned, same caveat)*

A hybrid's `behavior` is simply the union of its two parents' tags (e.g. Suckerfish `['SCAVENGER']` + Electric Eel `['GENERATOR']` → Scrub-Eel `['SCAVENGER', 'GENERATOR']`) — the Fish entity doesn't need to know a species is a hybrid at all, it just reads whatever tags are in the row.

Adding a species = adding one `SPECIES` row + at most one small behavior function. Never a new class.

## Species Roster & Progression

**6 base species, 12 hybrids, 18 total.** No standalone tier ladder beyond that — the old draft's separate Tier 2/3/4/exotic species (Voltminnow, Bristle Crab, Lumenjelly, Cogwhelk, Lancefish, Blasterpuff, Deepstalker, Prism Guppy, Magnetfish) are cut from the roster as of this revision. This section is the current source of truth for who exists; ignore any species list in the original planning prompt that conflicts with it. (Terminology note: this old "tier ladder" and each `SPECIES` row's numeric `tier` field predate, and are unrelated to, the Tier 1-4 Mound progression introduced below in Tier Progression & The Mound — that one is capitalized "Tier" throughout this doc to keep the two apart.)

**The 6 base species:**
| Species | Role | Behavior tag |
|---|---|---|
| Guppy | Baseline feeder | `FEEDER` |
| Dartfin | Fast, cheap feeder | `FEEDER` |
| Blimpfish | Slow, expensive feeder | `FEEDER` |
| Suckerfish | Scavenger / cleanliness | `SCAVENGER` |
| Electric Eel | Power generator | `GENERATOR` |
| Science Octopus | Researcher | `RESEARCHER` |

**Gene-Splicing:** once researched (Phase 4 Science Tech Tree), the 3 utility fish (Suckerfish, Electric Eel, Science Octopus) can be dragged onto any other base fish — including onto each other — to splice a dual-role hybrid. That's `3 × 5` ordered target pairs, but a Suckerfish+Eel splice is the same resulting hybrid regardless of which one gets dragged onto which, so the 3 utility-onto-utility pairs only count once each: `(3 utility × 3 feeders) + C(3,2) utility-utility = 9 + 3 = 12` unique hybrids. The 3 feeder-only pairs (Guppy+Dartfin, Guppy+Blimpfish, Dartfin+Blimpfish) aren't valid splices — neither side has a utility role to contribute.

**Splicing requires an adult target, and a feeder-based hybrid's coin value is derived from it:** a utility fish can only be dragged onto a base fish that has already reached its final (adult) growth stage — not a hatchling or juvenile. For the 9 feeder-based hybrids (a utility fish spliced onto Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish), the resulting hybrid's `dropValue` is fixed at 1.2x whichever feeder parent's *adult* `dropValue` was at the moment of splicing — e.g. Scrub Guppy/Volt Guppy/Scholar Guppy all carry `dropValue: 6` (1.2x Guppy's adult value of 5) regardless of which utility fish was used, since the utility parent only determines the secondary behavior tag, not the coin payout. This doesn't apply to the 3 utility-utility hybrids (Scrub-Eel, Scrub-Topus, Volt-Topus) — neither parent is a feeder, so there's no adult coin value to inherit from; their `dropValue` is unchanged from their original (mostly non-monetary) scaffold values. None of this gating is actually implemented yet (Phase 4 hasn't built the drag-to-splice interaction — see Phase rollout below), but the target `dropValue`s already reflect it in `Config.js` so the numbers don't need retuning once that interaction exists.

Hybrid naming/tag pattern (see `parents` field above): `Scrub` prefix = inherits `SCAVENGER`, `Volt` prefix = inherits `GENERATOR`, `Scholar` prefix = inherits `RESEARCHER`; a feeder-based hybrid is `[Prefix] [FeederName]`, a utility-utility hybrid is `[Prefix]-[OtherUtilityName]` (e.g. `Scrub-Eel`, the one name given directly in the design update).

**Phase rollout:** describes when Claude *codes* each species/mechanic. See the new Tier Progression & The Mound section below for when the *player* actually unlocks them within a level — Phase and Tier are different axes (Phase = build roadmap, Tier = in-level narrative progression) that now interlock: the Mound's Tier-crack action is what mechanically grants a meta unlock, even in a phase before that species'/building's full behavior is wired up.
- **Phase 2:** grid/routing work, plus the Mound itself and the Tier progression system (see below) — Suckerfish and the base buildings unlock via Mound cracks/the Tier 1.5 tease.
- **Phase 3 (current, partially built):** Suckerfish's real Scavenger behavior is done — it eats only Waste (never Food), at half Guppy's hunger rate, and each Waste item it (or an Auto-Feeder) cleans up restores real, live-tracked `state.level.cleanliness` (see "Cleanliness (Phase 3)" in `Config.js` and the Current Phase changelog entry). What's still NOT built: Electric Eel's Generator role/the power grid, and any actual gameplay *consequence* of low cleanliness (fish stress/toxicity) — cleanliness right now is a real, correctly-tracked number with HUD feedback, not yet a mechanic that affects anything else. Electric building variants (Electric Collector, Auto-Feeder-equivalent-for-power) that skip producing Waste in the first place also remain unbuilt.
- **Phase 4 (built):** Science Octopus (and every RESEARCHER-tagged species/hybrid) actually produces Science now, and the Mound's Tier 5 shatter reveals a real, clickable Science Lab whose one tech node — Gene-Splicing — is purchasable with Science and turns on the drag-a-utility-fish-onto-another-fish hybrid interaction. See "Science Lab & Gene-Splicing (Phase 4)" below for the full mechanism; this bullet is now historical context for when it was scoped, not a description of what's still missing.
- **Phase 5+:** undefined pending a future design pass. The original plan's alien-wave/combat content (Lancefish, Blasterpuff, Deepstalker, `HUNTER` tag) is not part of the current roster; don't build toward it without new direction.

**Building unlock rule:** superseded by the Mound below — see that section for the live mechanism. `state.meta.buildingsUnlocked`/`speciesUnlocked` are still the persistent unlock flags (`getAvailableBuildings`/`getAvailableSpecies` in `Levels.js` still gate the shop off both these and each level's `allowedBuildings`/`allowedSpecies` allow-list, unchanged), but they're now written by `Mound.js`'s `crackMound()` instead of "on level completion" (that old mechanism was never actually implemented, and is retired in favor of the Mound). Gene-Splicing still unlocks manually by spending Science in the Phase 4 tech tree (`state.meta.techUnlocked`, written by `UI.js`'s tech tree) once the Tier 4 Science Lab is reachable.

## Tier Progression & The Mound

Every level starts with a large, uncracked Mound sitting at the bottom-center of the seabed grid (`Mound.js`, `MOUND_X = WORLD_W / 2` in `Config.js`). The camera itself starts at world x=0 (the far-left edge) via `Levels.js`'s `loadLevel`, so without help the Mound would sit ~2000px off the right edge of the screen at level start — `Mound.js`'s `centerCameraOnMound(camera)` re-centers `camera.x` on `MOUND_X` (clamped to the world's bounds), called once by `main.js` right after the initial zoom/viewWidth computation and again by `UI.js`'s `restartLevel` after every restart (both paths go through `loadLevel`, which resets `camera.x` to 0). Deliberately not called from the window-resize handler, so resizing the browser mid-play doesn't yank the camera back to the Mound once the player has panned elsewhere. It's the diegetic replacement for "buildings unlock automatically" — instead, the player clicks the Mound and throws money at it, which advances `state.level.tier` (1-4, level-scoped like everything else in `state.level` — a restarted or new level always starts back at Tier 1, even though the meta-unlocks a previous crack already granted stay permanently available per the usual meta/level split). This is a different axis from *Phase* (Phase = how much of the game Claude has built so far; Tier = how far the player has progressed within the current level's arc) — see the note at the top of Phase rollout above.

**The five tiers:**
1. **Tier 1 (level start):** feeding, manual coin collection, and Platform (see BUILDING_TYPES' `unlockedByDefault` — Platform is NOT tier-gated at all, since every other building anchors to it; per direct request it's just always available). The Mound sits there uncracked, 10% bigger than its original size (`MOUND_WIDTH_TILES`/`MOUND_HEIGHT_PX` in `Config.js`) — clicking it opens a small popup right above it ("Throw money at it" / a `$`-labeled button showing `getMoundNextCost(state)`, disabled and grayed out like the shop's Buy button when unaffordable / a Cancel button) — see "The tease" below for what the first click at this popup actually does. This replaced an earlier version of the Mound that cracked immediately on click with a persistent on-canvas "Throw $X at it" label and no popup at all — the user explicitly asked for a real popup here, overriding this project's general no-extra-confirmation-modals preference (which still applies elsewhere, e.g. the shop) — and then, after seeing a first pass that centered/dimmed the whole screen pause-menu-style, asked for it un-dimmed, smaller, positioned right next to the Mound, and flying out of/back into it. `UI.js` owns it (`openMoundMenu`/`closeMoundMenu`/`isMoundMenuOpen`): `#mound-overlay` is a fully transparent, full-viewport click-catcher (so clicking outside the card still closes it, just with no visible tint); `#mound-menu-anchor`'s `left`/`top` are set every render frame it's open (`updateMoundMenuPosition`, via `worldToScreen(MOUND_X, ...)`) so the popup tracks the Mound's screen position even if the camera pans while it's open, with a static `translate(-50%, -100%)` anchoring the anchor's bottom-center there; `#mound-menu` itself carries a separate `transform: scale()` + opacity CSS transition, pivoting from that same bottom-center (`transform-origin: 50% 100%`), toggled by a `.mound-menu-closed` class — opening forces that class on then strips it next paint (the same forced-reflow trick `playFlash` uses) so the grow plays from a standing start, closing adds it back and defers actually hiding the overlay by `MOUND_MENU_TRANSITION_MS` so the shrink is visible first. The popup doesn't freeze the sim (`state.ui.paused`) — it's a lightweight decision popup, not a full pause state, same as the shop staying live while open. Escape closes it first if it's open, rather than opening the pause menu on top of it (see `main.js`'s keydown handler).
2. **Tier 2 (Economy Fish Combining):** grants the Rudimentary Fan (`TILE_FAN_T2`) — and, orthogonally to any building unlock, this is the tier `state.level.tier >= 2` gates the whole Economy Fish Combining/Splicing system on (dynamic feeder-fish pricing, drag-to-combine, star tiers — see that section below). No species unlock at this tier.
3. **Tier 3 (rudimentary factory, dirty):** grants Ramp Left/Right, Collector, and Auto-Feeder, plus Suckerfish, into `state.meta`. Downside: every basic building is unpowered and "dirty" — consuming an item also spawns a Waste item as a byproduct (Phase 2 scaffold: a basic Collector banking a coin also spawns waste at the same spot; see `Config.js`'s `WASTE_*` constants and `Entities.js`). Nothing currently *consumes* waste or turns it into a real cleanliness cost — that's Phase 3 (Suckerfish's real behavior + toxicity math), though the Auto-Feeder does now give waste a productive use (see "Auto-Feeder" below) even before real toxicity math exists. For now excess waste just piles up on the seabed as visible pressure to route it somewhere.
4. **Tier 4 (electrification, stronger automation):** grants Electric Eel and the Electric Fan (`TILE_FAN_T3`) — a straight upgrade over the Rudimentary Fan's reach/force, at a small power cost (see "Directional Fans" below). Electric Eel's own Generator behavior (and the real power grid it should draw from) is still Phase 3 scope, not yet built — the Electric Fan runs unconditionally today, same inert-power-gate situation every other not-yet-power-gated Electric building has been in.
5. **Tier 5 (the reveal) — Phase 4 alignment, not yet built for species/tech:** the Mound shatters completely instead of cracking further, revealing the Science Lab underneath. Clicking the Lab (not the Mound — it's a different clickable once revealed) opens the Tech Tree UI, granting Science Octopus and the Gene-Splicing research option. `Mound.js`'s render/click functions already no-op once `state.level.tier >= MOUND_MAX_TIER`, leaving a deliberate gap for Phase 4 to draw the Lab in its place. The Turbo Fan (`TILE_FAN_T4`) unlocks alongside this tier and IS fully functional the moment it does — its "requires Science Lab research" framing in the design spec doesn't have a real tech-tree gate to hang off yet (no Gene-Splicing-style purchase UI exists), so for now it's reachable the same way every other Tier 5 item is: the moment the tier itself is reached. See "T5 Hybridization Value Carry-Over Pipeline" below for what's actually built toward the real Gene-Splicing interaction today.

All five tiers shifted/reorganized from an earlier 4-tier scheme per direct request — see the Current Phase changelog entry for the exact before/after mapping.

**The tease ("Tier 1.5"):** the very first "throw money" attempt at the Mound, ever, is a distinct step from the real Tier 1 -> 2 crack — it costs `MOUND_TEASE_COST` ($150, unchanged across the tier reshuffle) and does **not** advance `state.level.tier` at all, but (per direct feedback, no longer a pure no-op joke) it DOES grant the Rudimentary Fan into `state.meta.buildingsUnlocked` and posts a real reveal line to the notification ticker — see "Story & Tutorial Notifications" above for the full text and rationale. `state.level.moundTeased` (set `true` the moment this fires) is what the game reads to tell "still need the first attempt" from "already teased, next attempt is the real Tier 1 -> 2 crack" — both are the same click/popup/button, the *cost and outcome* are just conditional on that flag (`Mound.js`'s `getMoundNextCost(state)`). It resets to `false` on every level load/restart same as everything else in `state.level`, so this replays each fresh playthrough.

**Mechanism (`Mound.js`):** `MOUND_CRACK_COST` in `Config.js` is a plain `{ tier: cost }` map — `{1: 1000, 2: 5000, 3: 25000, 4: 50000}`, the *real* cost to crack from that tier once teased (placeholder balance, tune once real playtesting exists, same as every other economy constant; the final crack, 4 -> 5 unlocking Hybridization, is priced at the explicitly requested 50000, steep on purpose — "they gotta make a whole system first," per the design note, even though nothing beyond money actually gates it yet). `getMoundNextCost(state)` is the single source of truth `UI.js`'s popup button reads for its label/afford-check — it returns `MOUND_TEASE_COST` while `tier === 1 && !moundTeased`, otherwise `MOUND_CRACK_COST[tier]`. `TIER_UNLOCKS` maps a tier to the `{ species: [...], buildings: [...] }` ids it grants; `crackMound()` spends whatever `getMoundNextCost` returned, and either (a) just sets `moundTeased` and posts the joke line, or (b) increments `state.level.tier`, pushes those ids into `state.meta.speciesUnlocked`/`buildingsUnlocked` (deduped), and writes the real crack's narrative line — see which branch based on the same `moundTeased` check. `Tier 4`/`5` already have their species listed in `TIER_UNLOCKS` even though their behavior isn't coded — reaching them today just makes Electric Eel/Science Octopus/the hybrids purchasable early with no real Generator/Researcher/splicing behavior behind them yet, same inert-scaffold situation every not-yet-behavior-wired species has always been in. Unlike those species, `TIER_UNLOCKS`' Tier 4/5 *buildings* (the Electric Fan and Turbo Fan) are NOT inert scaffolds — both are fully functional the moment their tier is reached, see "Directional Fans" below.

**Ramps ("funnels"):** a Ramp doesn't arrest vertical motion at all, in either direction — this replaced an earlier version where landing on a Ramp set an `item.slidingRamp` flag and rode a fixed-speed 45-degree diagonal slide (`RAMP_SLIDE_SPEED`) until it reached the next solid tile, which read as a sticky conveyor belt rather than something that just deflects what's already moving through it, per direct feedback ("it's not a sticky conveyor belt, its like a mini fan"). Ramps are deliberately excluded from `Grid.js`'s `SOLID_TILES` set — `sweepVertical` never "lands" an item on one, so an item falling *or rising* (e.g. off a Fan's push) through a Ramp's row keeps its `vy` completely untouched. Instead, `applyRampNudge` (called every sub-step of `sweepVertical`, not just once per tick, so a fast-moving item can't skip over an entire row within one tick) checks whether the item's current row is a Ramp tile and, if so, shifts its `x` by exactly one `RAMP_NUDGE_DISTANCE` (one tile width) in that Ramp's direction — once per row, tracked via `item.rampNudgedRow` so a slow-moving or lingering item doesn't get re-nudged every tick it happens to still be passing through the same row (that would read as stuttering, not "moved one tile"). `rampNudgedRow` clears the moment the item leaves that row, so a second Ramp of the same direction placed immediately below/above the first legitimately chains the push further — that's not specially blocked. Rendered as a triangle pointing the direction it nudges (`Grid.js`'s `renderTileShape`) rather than a plain square, so the effect reads at a glance: a left-pointing wedge for Ramp Left, right-pointing for Ramp Right. Same as every other non-Platform building, a Ramp must be placed adjacent to a Platform tile or directly on the world's bottom row — see "Platform Anchoring" below.

**Directional Fans (replaces the old Blaster):** the Blaster — a solid tile that instantly relaunched whatever landed on it straight back up — is gone entirely, along with the Funnel/Vacuum Pipe it had already replaced earlier in Phase 2. In its place: a Fan is a directional force *emitter*, not a landing-triggered launcher. It doesn't wait for something to land on it — every tick, `Grid.js`'s `computeFanForce` sums a force vector from every Fan whose triangular cone (`FAN_CONE_HALF_ANGLE_DEG`, ±25° off the Fan's aim = 50° total width — widened in several passes per direct request, most recently from ±20°/40°, alongside the range increase below) currently contains a given item, wherever that item is — open water or seabed band, since a Fan's whole point is routing things back up into the water column where fish are, not just seabed-local sorting. The cone blows in a *fixed* direction (uniformly along the Fan's own aim angle), not radiating outward from its position like an explosion, with force decaying linearly to 0 at `maxRange` (`force = maxForce * (1 - distance / maxRange)`, per-tier in `Config.js`). No occlusion is modeled — a Platform or another building between a Fan and an item doesn't block the cone; a deliberate simplification. Range per tier was extended across the board over several passes per direct request, most recently landing at: Rudimentary Fan 10 tiles / 320px, Electric Fan 15.5 tiles / 496px, Turbo Fan 20 tiles / 640px — force magnitudes unchanged throughout every pass, see the Current Phase changelog for the full history of increases. A Fan's cone is now rendered even when its own tile has scrolled off screen (`Grid.js`'s `renderFanIndicators`, called once per frame independently of the normal tile-culling loop) — see the Current Phase entry documenting that fix.

**Aiming — a two-click flow, not one:** per direct request, a Fan's placement and its aim are two separate deliberate decisions rather than one click doing both. Click 1 (while a Fan build tool is selected) arms aiming at whatever cell it landed on, IF that cell is a legal placement (`Grid.js`'s `canPlaceTile`) — no tile is placed yet, no money spent yet. From that point, the build ghost stays visually fixed at the armed cell but its aim rotates live with the cursor (`main.js`'s `isFanAimingActive()`/`fanAimingCell`, using the same `angleFromTileToPoint` math as before, just re-run every render frame against a fixed cell instead of a fixed click). Click 2, anywhere, confirms whatever angle the cursor is showing at that moment and actually calls `placeTile`. `fanAimingCell` is self-healing rather than needing explicit cross-module cancellation: it's only ever honored while `state.ui.selectedTool` still equals `build:<that fan's id>`, so switching tools, hotkeys, or Escape (checked explicitly, ahead of the pause menu, in `main.js`'s keydown handler) all implicitly cancel a pending aim just by changing that one piece of state. Only Fans get this two-step treatment — the Auto-Feeder, which also has an aim angle, was left as the original single-click flow (not explicitly requested to change, and drag-placing several in a row still works for it). The angle itself is still stored the same way as before: per-instance in `state.level.buildingData` (a sparse `"row,col"` -> `{ type, angle, ... }` map — Fans/the Auto-Feeder are the only buildings that need more than a bare type-id string, which is all `state.level.grid` cells hold). The placed tile renders both a small aim arrow and a translucent cone overlay (`Grid.js`'s `renderDirectionIndicator`) so the invisible force field is always visible.

**Force integration (`Grid.js`'s `integrateItemForces`, called from both `Entities.js`'s open-water branches and internally from `stepItemOnGrid`'s seabed branch, so a Fan behaves identically on either side of `SEABED_FLOOR_Y`):** gravity is `F_gravity = mass * g`, so `a_gravity = F_gravity / mass = g` — mass-independent, same as real gravity and same as this game's fall behavior always was. Fan thrust IS mass-dependent (`a_fan = F_fan / mass`), which is the whole point of the Mass Hierarchy: **Coins** are heavy (`mass = 3.0`, `ITEM_MASS_BY_TYPE.coin` in `Config.js`) and need strong or overlapping Fan coverage to actually clear a ledge; **Waste** is medium (`mass = 1.0`) and moves readily under a standard Fan; **Food** is super light (`mass = 0.3`) and readily launches/floats on even a Rudimentary Fan's modest force. Drag is linear (`a_drag = -drag * v`) and derived per item type from its own existing `gravity / maxFallSpeed` ratio (already in `Config.js` for coin/food/waste) rather than a separate new constant — this is what makes an item reach a stable terminal trajectory instead of accelerating forever under a sustained Fan force, and it also means an un-pushed item's ordinary fall converges to *exactly* the same terminal velocity this game always had, before Fans existed. Verified directly in-browser: a food pellet dropped dead-center into a Rudimentary Fan's straight-up cone reaches a stable hover almost immediately and holds position (fan thrust ≈ gravity at that mass/force/distance) rather than drifting or oscillating away.

**Tiers (`Config.js`'s `FAN_T2_*`/`FAN_T3_*`/`FAN_T4_*`, placeholder balance like every other economy/physics constant):** Rudimentary Fan (`TILE_FAN_T2`, Tier 2, free, 320px/10-tile range) -> Electric Fan (`TILE_FAN_T3`, Tier 4, small power cost, 496px/15.5-tile range) -> Turbo Fan (`TILE_FAN_T4`, Tier 5, moderate power cost, 640px/20-tile range, "extreme thrust" per spec — enough to clear a heavy coin on its own). Power cost is tracked (for a future power-grid system) but not yet gated on power availability — see the Tier 4 bullet above.

**Auto-Feeder:** placed and aimed exactly like a Fan used to be (single click, angle locked from the exact cursor position within the tile — NOT the two-click flow Fans now use, see "Aiming" above; not explicitly requested to change), same `buildingData` entry shape plus `absorbing`/`progressMs` runtime fields — its aim IS the *output* direction; the *intake* is the point directly opposite, `AUTO_FEEDER_PORT_OFFSET_FRACTION` of a tile outside the tile's center along that same axis. Ticked once per frame by `Grid.js`'s `updateBuildings` (called from `Entities.js`'s `updateEntities`, alongside `resolveItemCollisions`): while not already mid-process, it scans `state.level.items` for any Waste within `AUTO_FEEDER_INTAKE_RADIUS` of its intake point (typically pushed there by a Fan) **and genuinely on that side of the tile** (`isOnIntakeSide`, shared with the Collector's own intake scan below — added per direct request that neither building should pull an item in "through the building" or from the top; being merely within radius of the port coordinate used to be enough, which could otherwise still catch an item resting on top of the tile) and absorbs the first eligible one found (removed from the array directly — an intentional exception to the usual "Grid.js returns a status, the caller mutates the array" split, justified the same way `resolveItemCollisions` already mutates item positions in place). After `AUTO_FEEDER_PROCESS_DURATION_MS`, it dispenses exactly one Food item at its output point with zero velocity — a nearby Fan can then pick that Food back up and launch it into the water column, same as any other item. To avoid a circular import (`createFood` lives in `Entities.js`, which already imports from `Grid.js`), `updateBuildings` returns spawn points (`{ x, y }`) rather than constructing the Food itself; `Entities.js` does the actual `createFood`/push. Rendered with the same two-arrow input/output indicator the Collector now shares (`Grid.js`'s `renderDirectionIndicator`) — a dark arrow along the output side, a cyan arrow along the intake side. Unlocked at Tier 3, alongside Ramp/Collector/Suckerfish.

**Platform Anchoring:** nothing can float freely built in open water any more. Every building except Platform itself must be placed adjacent (up/down/left/right, not diagonal) to a Platform tile, or directly on the world's absolute bottom row (`WORLD_TILES_H - 1`, the true seabed floor, which needs no anchor). `Grid.js`'s `canPlaceTile` enforces this (`isAnchored`) alongside the existing bounds/occupancy/cost checks, returning a rejection reason the build-mode ghost preview tints red for, same as any other invalid placement. Platform is the sole exception — it has to be placeable on its own, since it's the thing everything else anchors to, which is also why it's the one building with `unlockedByDefault: true` (see the Tier 1 bullet above) rather than sitting behind any Mound crack.

**Dynamic building cost:** every building's shop price is live now, not a flat `BUILDING_TYPES[id].cost` — see `Grid.js`'s `getBuildingCost` and Config.js's comment above `BUILDING_TYPES` for the full rationale. Platform is a flat `PLATFORM_FLAT_COST` ($3) regardless of how many exist; every other building's cost is its base cost plus `BUILDING_COST_INCREMENT` ($1) for each tile of that exact type already placed on the grid, counted live (`countPlacedOfType`) the same "no separate counter to keep in sync" way the Economy Fish dynamic pricing already works — demolishing one brings the next one's price back down automatically. `canPlaceTile`/`placeTile` both read the live cost; `removeTile`'s refund is computed off the SAME live cost (evaluated while the tile being removed still counts toward it), not the static base cost, so a full-refund (`TILE_REFUND_FRACTION` = 1.0) place-then-immediately-demolish stays exactly cost-neutral even at a higher placed count.

**Demolish Tool:** removing a placed building now requires deliberately selecting the Demolish tool (🗑️, `UI.js`'s `#tool-demolish-btn`, right next to the Food tool in the shop's tool-bar) — per direct request, replacing the old always-on right-click-removes-anything shortcut. With it selected, hovering a built tile renders it in a translucent red "ghost mode" tint plus the exact refund amount it'll pay out (`main.js`'s render loop, reading `BUILDING_TYPES[tile].cost * TILE_REFUND_FRACTION`); a left click on that tile calls `Grid.js`'s `removeTile`. Right-click still works too, but ONLY while the Demolish tool is already selected (`input.rightClickHandlers` now checks `state.ui.selectedTool === 'demolish'` first) — it no longer removes tiles unconditionally regardless of tool. `TILE_REFUND_FRACTION` was raised from 0.5 to **1.0** (a full refund) at the same time, per direct request — safe to do now that removal is a deliberate two-step action (select tool, then click) rather than an always-available shortcut, so there's no free-conveyor exploit risk a partial refund was originally hedging against.

## Economy Fish Combining/Splicing (Tier 2)

A second progression system layered on top of the 3 base feeder species (Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish — called "economy fish" in the design spec, tracked by `Config.js`'s `ECONOMY_SPECIES_IDS`), gated on `state.level.tier >= 2` for its mechanics rather than any specific building unlock (Tier 2 itself grants no species/buildings besides the Rudimentary Fan — see the Tier 2 bullet above).

**Dynamic purchase cost (`Entities.js`'s `getFishPurchaseCost`):** an economy species' shop price is no longer flat — `Current_Cost = round(species.cost * ECONOMY_FISH_COST_GROWTH_RATE ^ N)`, where `N` is `countLivingFishOfSpecies(state, speciesId)` — every living fish of that exact species currently in `state.level.entities`, at ANY star tier (not just freshly-bought ones). `ECONOMY_FISH_COST_GROWTH_RATE` is 1.25 (cut from an original 1.4 per direct request — the steeper curve felt too punishing). `N` is always computed live off the current entity array, never tracked as a running counter, so a purchase (N+1, price up), a death/starvation (N-1, price down), or a combine (net N-1, since two fish become one) all reflect immediately with no separate bookkeeping to keep in sync. Non-economy species (utility fish, hybrids) are unaffected — always their flat `SPECIES.cost`. `UI.js`'s shop icons (`speciesPriceTags`, refreshed every frame the shop is open via `refreshShopPrices`) and the preview window's name/Buy button (`refreshPreviewBuyButton`, already running every frame) both read this live rather than the static `.cost`.

**Combining (drag one Adult economy fish onto another):** gated first on `state.level.upgrades.fishMergingUnlocked` — a one-time Tank Upgrade purchase (`FISH_MERGING_UNLOCK_COST`, 10 Tank Points, see Tank Points & Tank Upgrades below), not a Tier unlock; `isCombinableFish`/`canCombineFish` both now take `state` as their first argument specifically to check this flag before anything else. Once unlocked, the remaining prerequisites are unchanged: both fish must be the exact same species, the exact same `starTier` (1-3; a fish already at `FISH_STAR_TIER_MAX`, 4, can't be a combine source at all, since there's nothing to combine it into), and Adult (final growth stage — `fish.stage === def.growthStages.length - 1`). `combineFish` consumes both (spliced out of `state.level.entities`) and spawns one new Adult fish of `starTier + 1` at their midpoint, with a `"N★ Species!"` floating-text confirmation.

**Drag interaction (`main.js`):** built as a small parallel input system alongside the existing build-drag, using two new Engine.js handler registries (`input.mouseDownHandlers`/`mouseUpHandlers`, fired once per press/release with screen coords — mouseup always uses the last tracked in-canvas mouse position, since a release can land outside the canvas). A mousedown that lands on an eligible source fish (`isCombinableFish`) sets `draggedFishId` and a same-gesture `fishDragArmed` flag; every tick that follows, `main.js`'s `updateFishDrag` (called after `updateEntities` in `update()`, so it overrides whatever that tick's normal AI/physics did) snaps that fish's position to the cursor and zeroes its velocity — the "you're holding this fish" feedback, and why a dragged fish visibly stops swimming on its own for the duration. On mouseup, `findFishAt(state, releaseWorldX, releaseWorldY, draggedFishId)` (the `excludeId` param exists specifically so the dragged fish — now sitting exactly at the cursor — doesn't match itself) looks for a valid target at the release point and combines if one qualifies. `fishDragArmed` exists purely to suppress the native browser `click` event that ALWAYS fires after mouseup on the same element regardless of drag distance — without it, starting a drag on a fish would also trigger the click handler's bank-coin/spawn-food/open-Mound logic at the release point. Every render frame a drag is active, the fish currently under the cursor (again excluding the dragged one) gets a live green/red ring (`canCombineFish` re-checked fresh each frame) — the dragged fish itself gets a plain white ring. `updateBuildDrag` explicitly bails out while a fish-drag is active, so the two systems can't fight over the same mousedown.

**Coin value scaling — 1.8x per tier, not a flat doubling:** a Tier-`X` fish's adult coin `dropValue` is `species' own adult dropValue * FISH_STAR_TIER_VALUE_MULTIPLIER ^ (X - 1)` (multiplier 1.8, raised from an original 1.5 per direct request) — computed live in `Entities.js`'s `updateFish` at the moment of each coin drop (`fish.starTier`, defaulting to 1 for every fish that's never been combined, so the formula is a no-op `^0 = 1x` for the overwhelming majority of fish and needed no changes to `updateFish`'s existing per-stage dropValue lookup otherwise). For a Guppy (adult dropValue 5): T2 $9 -> T3 $17 -> T4 $30. The result is wrapped in `Math.ceil(...)` before being used (both here and in `getEconomyAdultDropValue`, the T5 carry-over helper below), per an earlier direct request that a fractional value like $11.25 read as sloppy — a higher-tier fish always drops a whole-number coin, rounded up rather than down so the tier bonus is never fully erased by truncation.

**Each combine step also makes the fish 10% less hungry — compounding, same pattern as the value multiplier:** `FISH_STAR_TIER_HUNGER_MULTIPLIER` (0.9, `Config.js`) is applied the same way — `def.hungerRate * FISH_STAR_TIER_HUNGER_MULTIPLIER ^ (starTier - 1)` — computed live in `updateFish` right before the per-tick hunger accumulation, so a Tier-2 fish's hunger rises 10% slower than Tier-1, Tier-3 19% slower, Tier-4 27.1% slower. Per direct request, this is the only thing scaling down with tier — **Waste production does NOT scale with star tier at all**, on either path: the direct fish-poop timer (`WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS`) fires and spawns exactly one Waste item regardless of `starTier` (that code never reads the field), and the Collector-byproduct path is likewise per-item-consumed, not per-dollar. A Tier-4 fish is explicitly meant to be a strict upgrade on the "money in, waste out" ratio — same waste as a Tier-1 adult, much more money, and it needs feeding less often to sustain that.

**Visual stars, not new sprites (`FishRenderer.js`):** `drawFish` takes an extra `starTier` param (default 1, so every existing call site that doesn't know about this system — the shop preview, most of all — needs no changes) and, only on the Adult-stage sprite, overlays a small row of gold 5-point stars (`drawStar`, a standard polygon helper) above the body. Count is NOT tier-1 (per the exact design spec table): `FISH_STAR_COUNT_BY_TIER = {1: 0, 2: 2, 3: 3, 4: 4}` — Tier 1 has none, Tier 2 jumps straight to 2.

## T5 Hybridization Value Carry-Over Pipeline

`Entities.js` has the value-carry-over math the actual splicing interaction (see "Science Lab & Gene-Splicing" below, which now calls into this) is built on: `getEconomyAdultDropValue(speciesId, starTier)` resolves exactly what a specific economy fish instance's coin is worth right now (same formula `updateFish` uses live, exposed standalone), `getHybridSpeciesId(economySpeciesId, utilitySpeciesId)` reverse-looks-up a hybrid SPECIES row from its `parents: [utilityId, economyId]` field (no hardcoded second id map to keep in sync as hybrids are added), and `createHybridFish(state, economyFish, utilitySpeciesId)` spends those to build the correct hybrid with that carried-over value written into a new `dropValueOverride` field. `updateFish` checks `dropValueOverride` first, before the star-tier formula — a spliced hybrid's value is already fully resolved at creation time (its own `starTier` stays the unused default, 1), so the two mechanisms never stack.

## Science Lab & Gene-Splicing (Phase 4)

Built on top of the pipeline above. `Mound.js`'s `isPointOnScienceLab`/`renderScienceLab` occupy the exact same footprint the Mound itself used, revealed the instant `state.level.tier >= MOUND_MAX_TIER` (the same condition that makes `isPointOnMound`/`renderMound` go inert) — a small dome structure with its own cached speckle texture (see the Mound's own texture note below), clicked to open `UI.js`'s Lab popup (`openLabMenu`/`closeLabMenu`/`isLabMenuOpen`, a second copy of the Mound's own fly-out-of-its-anchor popup pattern, separate DOM tree). The popup shows the live `state.meta.scienceTotal` and one button: Research Gene-Splicing, `GENE_SPLICING_COST` (100 Science, flat) — buying it pushes `GENE_SPLICING_TECH_ID` (`'gene_splicing'`) into `state.meta.techUnlocked`, a permanent meta unlock like a building or species.

**Science (and Power) production:** any species whose `behavior` includes `RESEARCHER` (or `GENERATOR`) *without* also `FEEDER` — Science Octopus, Electric Eel, and the utility-utility hybrids that carry one tag without the other (Scrub-Topus, Scrub-Eel; Volt-Topus checks RESEARCHER first and only ever produces Science, a deliberate one-resource-per-fish simplification) — produces Science/Power directly on its existing coin-drop timer instead of a coin (`Entities.js`'s `updateFish`). A Scholar/Volt hybrid (RESEARCHER/GENERATOR *and* FEEDER) keeps producing its carried-over coin value instead; it doesn't also produce Science/Power on top, same simplification. **Speed-scaled, per direct request** ("science and electricity are based on a fish's movement speed... even when it's moving towards food"): the produced amount is multiplied by `fish's actual current speed / effectiveSwimSpeed(def, state)` (floored at 0.3x so a fish mid-wander-turn doesn't produce a flat zero) — `fish.vx`/`vy`'s magnitude already reflects the Fish Movement Tank Upgrade and a seek-chase's speed boost alike, so both naturally increase output with no extra wiring. Several rows (Electric Eel, Scrub-Eel) have `dropValue: 0` — that used to mean "skip the coin drop entirely"; repurposed as a resource amount it's floored at 1 instead, so it doesn't mean "never produces anything."

**Gene-Splicing drag interaction:** `Entities.js`'s `isSpliceSource(state, fish)` (any of the 3 utility species, any growth stage, gated on the tech being researched) is the splice equivalent of `isCombinableFish`, checked on mousedown alongside it in `main.js` — a fish can be picked up as EITHER a combine source or a splice source, never both (the two species sets never overlap). `canSpliceFish(state, utilityFish, targetFish)` additionally requires the target be Adult (same "an adult target is what has a coin value to carry over" reasoning as the design spec) and that `getHybridSpeciesId` actually resolves a hybrid for that pair. `spliceFish` consumes both fish and spawns the hybrid at their midpoint, same shape as `combineFish`. `main.js`'s mouseup tries `canCombineFish` first, then `canSpliceFish` — mutually exclusive in practice since a fish can only ever qualify as one or the other. The green/red hover ring during a drag checks both the same way.

**The Collector:** aimed at placement exactly like the Auto-Feeder now (single click, angle from the cursor's sub-tile position — see `placeTile`), and no longer accepts a plain top-landing as an entry point at all — per direct request that items shouldn't be pulled in "through the building" or vacuumed up just by landing on top. `Grid.js`'s `handleLanding` now just returns `'resting'` for every solid tile including a Collector (identical to a Platform); instead, `updateBuildings` runs a per-tick intake scan (the same pattern the Auto-Feeder already used) that looks for an eligible item within `COLLECTOR_INTAKE_RADIUS` of the Collector's intake point AND genuinely approaching from that side (`isOnIntakeSide`, a normalized dot-product check against the tile's aim/output direction — see that function's own comment) — a coin resting on top, or drifting in from the output side, doesn't qualify. A match starts the same hold as before (`beginCollectorProcessing`, extracted from the old landing-event code unchanged): `stepCollectorProcessing` eases the item toward the tile's stored center (`COLLECTOR_PULL_STRENGTH`) and counts up toward `COLLECTOR_PROCESS_DURATION_MS` (3000ms) before reporting `'consumed'`, with `item.mass` temporarily overridden to `COLLECTOR_PROCESSING_MASS` so it barely budges under `resolveItemCollisions` mid-hold. Only one item processes per Collector at a time (`updateBuildings` skips the intake scan entirely while one's already mid-hold on that tile). If the Collector tile itself gets torn down mid-process, `stepCollectorProcessing` still notices and hands the item back to normal physics, unchanged. Rendered with a circle in the tile's center (`COLLECTOR_CIRCLE_RADIUS_FRACTION`) plus the same two-arrow input/output indicator the Auto-Feeder gets (see that section below) — a dark arrow pointing out along the aim/output side, a cyan arrow pointing in along the intake side, so which side is which reads at a glance instead of one generic arrow implying a single direction.

**Waste:** a third item type alongside food/coin (`Entities.js`'s `createWaste`), reusing the exact same `Grid.js` tile physics as a coin (falls, lands on tiles, can be routed) but isn't click-bankable. Now consumed two ways (Phase 3): a Scavenger fish (Suckerfish) eating it directly, or an Auto-Feeder absorbing it — both restore `CLEANLINESS_PER_WASTE_EVENT` of cleanliness, mirroring the penalty paid when it spawned; see "Cleanliness (Phase 3)" in `Config.js`.

**Two independent sources of Waste, not one:** (1) the Collector byproduct described below — a plain `TILE_COLLECTOR` finishing its processing hold spawns one Waste at the same spot as a "dirty automation" downside; and (2) direct fish poop — every non-Scavenger fish (`Entities.js`'s `updateFish`, gated on `!isScavenger` so Suckerfish never poops, since it's the one consuming Waste, not producing it) accumulates its own `poopTimer` every tick and, once it crosses `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` (25000ms), spawns a Waste item at its own position and costs `CLEANLINESS_PER_WASTE_EVENT` of cleanliness, resetting the timer to 0. This second source was added per direct correction — an earlier state of the game had fish never producing Waste on their own at all (only the Collector byproduct existed), which the design intent was clear didn't match "fish poop" as a mental model; a fish in open water with zero buildings anywhere near it now still dirties the tank over time, same as the real Insaniquarium games this is patterned on. `createWaste`/`stepItemOnGrid`'s Collector-byproduct call site (spawned by `Entities.js` whenever `updateCoin`/`updateFood` see `'consumed'` come back from `Grid.js`'s `stepItemOnGrid`) is unrelated code, untouched by this addition. Once an Electric Collector exists (Tier 4+) that call site will need to branch on which tile actually consumed the item before deciding whether to spawn Waste — the poop mechanic has no such gap since it doesn't depend on any tile at all.

## Rolling Notification Log

`state.level.notifications` — a capped array (`NOTIFICATION_LOG_MAX` in `Config.js`, oldest entries drop off past that) of `{ id, text, elapsed }`, rendered by `UI.js` as a collapsible ticker in the HUD (top-left, collapsed by default to its latest line; click to expand a scrollback of recent messages). `Levels.js`'s `loadLevel` seeds it with one real entry at level start (`WELCOME_MESSAGE`) rather than `UI.js` showing a hardcoded fallback string for an empty array the way it originally did — per direct feedback that "Welcome to the tank" should stay visible in the scrollback log once other notifications start arriving, not just vanish the instant anything else happens. Every other writer follows the same push-then-cap pattern (`notifications.push({ id: notifications.length + 1, text, elapsed: state.level.elapsed }); if (notifications.length > NOTIFICATION_LOG_MAX) notifications.shift();`), duplicated inline at each call site rather than factored into one shared cross-module export — see the paragraph below. The intent per the design updates is for this to also carry gameplay alerts (cleanliness warnings, wave warnings) and system status (power grid) once Phase 3+ systems exist to write them; any system can push a `{ text }` onto `state.level.notifications` (capped the same way, see any writer for the pattern) and the ticker picks it up with no changes needed on its end.

**Story & Tutorial Notifications:** per direct request, every player-facing notification string in the game (the Mound's tease/crack narration, the Tank Points tutorial line, the welcome message, and the newer one-time story beats below) is written in a consistent sarcastic, fish-pun-laden voice rather than plain mechanical description — this is the intended tutorial/story delivery mechanism for the whole game going forward, not just flavor text. `state.level.tutorialFlags` (`Levels.js`, level-scoped, reset on restart same as `moundTeased`) is a small set of one-shot gates — `firstFishBought`/`firstFishDied`/`firstBuildingPlaced`/`firstCombine`/`firstFanPlaced`/`moneyMilestone1k`/`escapePressed`/`escapeDareShown`/`firstChatClosed` — each checked-and-set at its trigger site the same way `awardTankPoint`'s existing `state.level.tankPoints.total === 0` check already worked, just generalized into named flags instead of inferring "first" from another system's counter being zero. The bankruptcy bailout/game-over pair below uses its own separate counter (`bankruptciesTriggered`, since it needs to distinguish "1st time" from "2nd time," not just "has this ever happened") rather than one of these boolean flags:
- **First fish bought** (`UI.js`, the shop's Buy button handler) — "You bought your first fish! Please remember to feed it occasionally. It's not a decoration. Probably."
- **First fish death** (`Entities.js`'s `updateFish`, the starvation branch) — per the user's own dictated line (revised once after the first pass): "Your fish is now swimming with the fishes. Oh wait...it just starved. You might want to try feeding your fish."
- **First building placed** (`Grid.js`'s `placeTile`) — "You just placed your first piece of seabed hardware. Welcome to factory brain — there's no swimming back from this now."
- **First Economy Fish combine** (`Entities.js`'s `combineFish`) — "You just smooshed two fish into one bigger, shinier fish. They're fine. Probably. It's basically fusion, and fusion is science, and science is great."
- **First Fan placed** (`Grid.js`'s `placeTile`, any of the three Fan tiers) — "fancy fan....oooo you fancy"
- **1k money milestone** (`Entities.js`'s `bankMoney`, a small wrapper both coin-banking paths — click-bank and Collector auto-bank — now route through) — fires once `state.level.lifetimeMoneyEarned` (a separate, monotonically-increasing counter alongside the real spendable `state.level.money` balance; NOT the starting endowment or a bankruptcy bailout gift, see below) crosses `MONEY_MILESTONE_1K` (1000): "1k money? Bruh save some for the fishes"
- **The 2-minute Escape dare, and its "gotcha" follow-up** (`Systems.js`'s `updateEscapeDare`, called every tick from `main.js`'s `update()`; the follow-up itself lives in `main.js`'s Escape keydown handler) — if `state.level.tutorialFlags.escapePressed` is still false once `state.level.elapsed` passes `ESCAPE_DARE_DELAY_MS` (2 minutes), posts "yoo, press escape. I dare you" and sets `escapeDareShown`. `escapePressed` is set the moment Escape is EVER pressed for the first time, ahead of every other Escape-handling branch (closing the Mound popup, cancelling a Fan aim, opening the pause menu) so all of those still count; if `escapeDareShown` was already true at that moment, it also posts "Made ya look. tehe" right then.
- **"You found the chat" — the first notification-log CLOSE, not open** (`UI.js`'s `#notification-latest` click handler, only on the expanded->collapsed transition — per direct request, so it fires once the player's actually read what's in there and closed it again, not the instant they open it; `firstChatClosed` can obviously only go true after at least one open, so no extra guard is needed) — posts "You found the chat. Curiosity kills the fish." and starts `state.level.fishVanishTimer` at `FISH_VANISH_DURATION_MS` (2.5s). While that timer is running, EVERY fish freezes exactly as it was (`Entities.js`'s `updateFishVanish`, called first thing in `updateEntities`, simply skips calling `updateFish` for any fish entity while the timer is `> 0` — position, hunger, and coin-drop timer all stay frozen, not just visually) and `main.js`'s render loop skips the whole fish-drawing loop for the same duration, so they're genuinely invisible, not just paused-looking. The tick the timer reaches 0, fish resume on their own and a "JK! You should have seen your face tho" notification posts.
- **Bankruptcy: a one-time bailout, then game over** (`Systems.js`, its first real code — see that module's own header) — `updateBankruptcy`, called every tick alongside `updateEscapeDare` from the same `updateStoryTriggers(state)` entry point, detects "no fish alive AND `state.level.money` is less than the cheapest thing currently purchasable" (`cheapestAvailablePurchase` — the minimum of Food's flat cost and every available species'/building's live cost, species read through the same dynamic economy pricing the shop itself uses). `state.level.bankruptcyActive` gates this to the rising edge of that condition (set once handled, cleared once the condition stops being true) so it fires once per fresh occurrence, not every tick the condition holds. First occurrence (`state.level.bankruptciesTriggered` going 0 -> 1): grants `BANKRUPTCY_BAILOUT_AMOUNT` ($100) and posts "Oopah, looks like someone got their CDL so they could drive the struggle bus! Here's 100 gold to get you back on your feet. I'll be expecting that back (I'm lying)." Second occurrence (1 -> 2): sets `state.level.gameOver = true` and posts 'My mama always said "Shooting a fish out of water in a barrel with bigger fish to fry" and I always took that to heart. Better luck next time! (Restart in the menu)'. `main.js`'s `update()` stops simulating entirely while `gameOver` is true — checked as a second, separate guard alongside the existing `state.ui.paused` check, deliberately NOT using `state.ui.paused` itself, so Escape still reaches the pause menu (and its Restart button, which rebuilds `state.level` from scratch via `loadLevel` and so clears `gameOver` naturally) without also silently un-freezing a lost game the way toggling `paused` would.

`Entities.js` factors its own writers (Tank Points, first fish death, first combine, the fish-vanish reappear line) through one local `pushStoryNotification(state, text)` helper, and `Grid.js`/`Systems.js`/`UI.js`/`main.js` each have their own equivalent local helper — still not exported/shared across modules, per the Rolling Notification Log section's stated convention above ("any system can push... see any writer for the pattern"), just de-duplicated within whichever one file happens to own more than one of these triggers.

**Tier 1.5 — the Mound's tease is a real reward now, not a joke:** the very first "throw money" attempt at the Mound used to cost `MOUND_TEASE_COST` and do nothing but post a punchline. Per direct feedback it now grants the Rudimentary Fan (`state.meta.buildingsUnlocked`, same as any other building unlock) while still NOT advancing `state.level.tier` past 1 — mechanically unchanged (`Mound.js`'s `crackMound` still branches on `tier === 1 && !moundTeased` exactly as before), just with a real payoff instead of an empty one. `TIER_UNLOCKS[2]` no longer lists the Rudimentary Fan as a result (it's already been granted by this point) — the real Tier 1 -> 2 crack now exists purely to flip `state.level.tier` to 2, which is what gates the whole Economy Fish Combining system on (see that section). The `N` debug cheat key still always skips the tease/grants nothing extra and goes straight to a real crack (`main.js` forces `moundTeased = true` before calling `crackMound`) — unchanged behavior, verified during this pass to still work correctly, just worth noting it does NOT exercise the Tier 1.5 grant path; testing that requires going through the actual Mound-click popup flow.

## Tank Points & Tank Upgrades

A second, separate progression currency alongside money and Tier — earned automatically, not spent on the shop's fish/buildings, only on a dedicated upgrade panel. `state.level.tankPoints = { total, available }` — level-scoped like Tier and everything else in `state.level` (a restart wipes both back to 0, same as purchased upgrade levels), not `state.meta`: this is deliberately a per-playthrough resource, not a permanent unlock currency like species/buildings. `total` never decreases (lifetime-earned this level, in case a later phase wants it for stats); `available` is what purchases spend.

**Earning:** `Entities.js`'s `awardTankPoint()` fires exactly once per fish — the moment its growth `stage` transitions into that species' final `growthStages` index (checked as `wasAdult`/`stage === growthStages.length - 1` around the existing feed-driven stage-advance in `updateFish`, the only place growth already happens). Cheat-spawning an already-grown fish (`Shift+G`, or `grown: true` generally) never triggers this — it starts at the adult stage rather than transitioning into it, so there's no exploit. Every award spawns a small `+1 Tank Point!` floating text over the fish (`TANK_POINT_COLOR` in `Config.js`); the very first one *also* posts a one-time explanatory line to the notification ticker ("...that's your first Tank Point! Spend Tank Points in the Tank Upgrades panel...") so a player who's never seen the mechanic before understands what just happened and where to spend it — gated on `state.level.tankPoints.total === 0` at the moment of the award, so it can only ever fire once per level.

**The panel:** `UI.js` owns a Tank Upgrades side panel, structurally the shop's twin — same collapse/expand behavior, same card styling — opened via a dedicated button (🏆) directly below the Shop's own collapse button in a shared always-on-top `#panel-toggle-cluster` (both toggle buttons live there now, outside either panel's own box, so neither panel's expand/collapse state can ever visually cover the other's button — see the CSS comment on `#panel-toggle-cluster`). Opening either panel auto-closes the other (`UI.js`'s `toggleShopCollapse`/`toggleTankPanel` each collapse the other before expanding themselves) — the two side panels share the same on-screen slot (`top:14px;right:14px;bottom:14px`), so at most one is ever expanded. `P` is the Tank panel's hotkey, mirroring `S` for the shop. Whichever of the two toggle buttons corresponds to the currently-open panel gets a `.panel-toggle-active` class (a filled ring + darker background, toggled in `updateShopCollapse`/`updateTankPanelCollapse`) — both buttons stay visible and, without this, identical-looking regardless of which panel is actually open, which per direct feedback wasn't obvious enough at a glance.

Each card's description line is deliberately pure stats, current level in red → next level in green (e.g. "Food fills 55 hunger → 65 hunger, sinks 0% slower → 10% slower") — no framing about design intent ("restores original speed," "deliberately reduced," etc.) leaks into player-facing panel text, even though that framing is exactly how the mechanic is described below and in `Config.js`'s comments for developers. `UI.js`'s `describeFoodQualityLevel`/`describeFishMovementLevel`/`describeFoodCapacityLevel` return small HTML strings (`.stat-current`/`.stat-next` spans) set via `innerHTML`, not `textContent` — safe here since every value going in is a computed number, never user input.

**Costs:** Food Quality keeps its own 5-level ladder, `FOOD_QUALITY_UPGRADE_COSTS = [2, 5, 15, 30, 50]` in `Config.js` — a much steeper curve than the original flat `[1,2,3,4]`, per earlier direct feedback that upgrades felt too cheap/quick to max out. Going from 4 levels to 5 meant `FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_BY_LEVEL` needed a 6th entry (index 0 = unupgraded through index 5 = level 5, now `[55, 65, 75, 85, 100, 115]`). Fish Movement and Food Capacity later split off onto their OWN shared 9-level ladder, `FISH_MOVEMENT_FOOD_CAPACITY_UPGRADE_COSTS = [1, 3, 6, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30, 35]` — per direct request, several more levels than Food Quality but far cheaper per level, so these two read as a steady trickle of small wins. `FISH_MOVEMENT_UPGRADE_COSTS`/`FOOD_CAPACITY_UPGRADE_COSTS` are just aliases of it. Both cost arrays' `.length` drives their own `_MAX_LEVEL` constant, so `UI.js`'s level-count/max-level display logic needed no changes to support the split (it was already generic over each tree's own array/max-level pair).

**Upgrade scope (four cards, the three purchasable ones level-gated — must own level N-1 to buy level N):**
- **Food Quality** (`state.level.upgrades.foodQuality`, 0-5, costs `FOOD_QUALITY_UPGRADE_COSTS`): each level (a) looks up a flatter, larger hunger-relief amount from `FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_BY_LEVEL`, replacing the old dynamic `FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_FLAT + FRACTION*hunger` formula entirely — applied in `updateFish`'s eat branch with the hunger-floor clamp removed, so a higher-quality feed than the fish actually needed pushes hunger *negative* (an "overfed" state Entities.js leaves inert for now — no bonus reads it yet, a hook for a later phase), and (b) reduces food's fall speed by `FOOD_QUALITY_SINK_SPEED_REDUCTION_PER_LEVEL` (10%, doubled from an original 5% as part of a general slower-pacing pass) per level, scaling both `FOOD_GRAVITY` and `FOOD_MAX_FALL_SPEED` together, applied live in `updateFood` (reads `state.level.upgrades.foodQuality` every tick, not baked into a pellet at spawn time).
- **Fish Movement** (`state.level.upgrades.fishMovement`, 0-9, costs `FISH_MOVEMENT_UPGRADE_COSTS`): adds `FISH_MOVEMENT_UPGRADE_SPEED_BONUS` (5 px/sec — left as a flat per-level bonus rather than converted to a percentage, per direct request to leave this formula alone even while restructuring the cost curve around it) per level on top of a species' `swimSpeed`. Every `SPECIES` row in `Config.js` already has this exact amount subtracted from its originally-tuned speed, so buying Level 1 restores the "real" speed and every level after pushes past it — un-upgraded, every species swims 5px/sec slower than its balance-tuned value, which is intentional (see each row's inline comment). Applied live via `Entities.js`'s `effectiveSwimSpeed(def, state)`, read everywhere movement is computed (`createFish`'s initial velocity, `wander`, and the hunger-seek speed) rather than baked in once, so a mid-playthrough purchase speeds up every fish already in the tank on their very next wander/seek recompute, not just future spawns.
- **Food Capacity** (`state.level.upgrades.foodCapacity`, 0-9, costs `FOOD_CAPACITY_UPGRADE_COSTS`): caps how many food pellets can exist *in the open-water tank* at once — `FOOD_MAX_ON_SCREEN_BASE` (2) plus `FOOD_CAPACITY_UPGRADE_INCREMENT` (1 — cut from 2 now that there are 9 levels instead of 5, so the cap still tops out somewhere comparable: 2 → 11 at max level instead of nearly doubling) per level. Enforced in `Entities.js`'s `trySpawnFood` (`effectiveFoodCapacity(state)` computes the live cap; spawning is refused past it regardless of money, the same kind of hard gate as insufficient funds) — deliberately tight at base level so early feeding is a real logistical constraint (wait for pellets to be eaten/despawn before dropping more) rather than something the player can spam freely. Per direct request, the cap only ever counts Food actually above `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` — `Entities.js`'s `countTankFood(state)` filters `state.level.items` on `item.y < SEABED_FLOOR_Y`, a live position check with no separate bookkeeping, so a pellet that's fallen (or been dispensed by an Auto-Feeder) into the seabed city stops counting against the cap the instant it crosses the line, and starts counting again the instant a Fan blows it back up — see "Food doesn't count toward its cap while in the city" in the Current Phase changelog for the full rationale and a worked verification. The current count vs. cap is also shown live in the main HUD (`🍽️ N/cap`, `#hud-food`, updated every frame in `UI.js`'s `updateHUD`, and duplicated into the shop panel's own `#shop-food` — see "The full main HUD now duplicates into the shop panel" above) — not just inside this panel.
- **Defensive Capabilities** (click damage/offense vs. alien waves): shown as a fourth card in the panel shell (Phase 2 UI scope, per the design update) but locked/non-interactive — there's no alien system to upgrade yet (Phase 5). Wiring this up is explicitly Phase 5+ scope, tied to whatever combat direction that future design pass settles on (see Species Roster & Progression's Phase 5+ note).

All three trees are simple linear ladders (`*_UPGRADE_COSTS` arrays in `Config.js`, index 0 = cost of level 1) — placeholder balance, tune once real playtesting exists, same as every other economy constant in this file.

## Items can't stack, and can fall off the bottom

Two design changes to seabed item physics, both aimed at the same problem: without them, a single Collector tile could just absorb an unlimited stream of coins forever, and any coin that missed one just sat at the world's bottom waiting to be clicked by hand — neither made routing infrastructure (ramps, multiple Collectors, spreading a stream out) actually necessary. Both are pure `Grid.js`/`Entities.js` physics; nothing about tile placement or the Mound/Tank Points systems above changed.

**No floor at the world's bottom (`Grid.js`'s `tileAt`):** out-of-bounds reads off the *left/right/top* edges of the world still read as solid (a `BOUNDARY_WALL` sentinel — nothing needs to fall off the sides), but reads below `WORLD_TILES_H` now return `TILE_EMPTY` instead — there's deliberately no implicit floor down there any more. An item that reaches it just keeps falling; `stepItemOnGrid` checks for this before anything else and returns a new `'lost'` status once the item has fallen `ITEM_LOST_BELOW_WORLD_MARGIN_PX` past `WORLD_H` (falls a little past the bottom edge first, so the loss reads as "fell off the bottom of the screen" rather than popping out of existence at an invisible line). Entities.js's callers all remove the item on `'lost'` with no payout; coins additionally get a small "Lost!" floating text (`ITEM_LOST_COLOR`) planted at `(item.x, WORLD_H)` — not the item's actual, long-since-off-screen position — so a player watching the bottom edge sees *why* it vanished. Food/waste vanish silently (food already had no such feedback; waste disappearing into the void is arguably a convenient way to get rid of it).

**UPDATE — the Rocky Shelf now catches every item type, so `'lost'` is effectively dead in normal play:** `Grid.js`'s `sweepVertical` has a second, earlier stop — `ROCK_SHELF_Y` (`Config.js`, 4 tiles above the world's absolute bottom) — that now applies unconditionally to every item type (coin/Science/Food/Waste alike), not just Waste as it originally did. Per direct request ("the rocky shelf is now a hard barrier for all objects... don't pass to the bottom of the tank"), nothing the player produces can fall through an unbuilt gap and get permanently lost any more; it just comes to rest on the shelf, well above where the `'lost'`/`ITEM_LOST_BELOW_WORLD_MARGIN_PX` check even triggers. That check (and its whole code path above) is kept purely as a defensive fallback now, not something normal gravity can reach.

**Items can't overlap, and nothing is ever permanently anchored (`Grid.js`'s `resolveItemCollisions`):** the first version of this made a pile "settle and freeze" — once an item was marked resting it was never simulated again, so a pile just sat there immune to gravity and immune to being pushed, which read as items snapping into a fixed structure rather than behaving like coins. Per direct playtest feedback ("they aren't affected by gravity when settled... they need to behave more like particles of liquid pushing each other around, rather than stickers that get stuck in weird structures"), this was replaced with a model where **every seabed item is stepped every tick for as long as it exists, whether it's currently resting or not** — there's no one-way "stop simulating this" state anywhere in the pipeline:
- `resting`/`restingOnFloor` are purely informational booleans, re-evaluated fresh from `stepItemOnGrid`'s return value every single tick (`Entities.js`'s `updateCoin`/`updateWaste`/`updateFood` — `item.resting = status === 'resting'`, not a one-way flip). An item that was resting last tick but got shoved off its support this tick immediately reports `'falling'` again and gravity picks it right back up, the same as if the tile under it had been removed.
- `resolveItemCollisions` (`Grid.js`) runs once per tick from `Entities.js`'s `updateEntities`, after every item's individual tile-physics step — comparing every item in `state.level.items` against every other one, not just against tiles. Originally scoped to the seabed band only (`item.y >= SEABED_FLOOR_Y`), since open water had nothing to collide with before Fans existed; widened to cover open water too once a Fan could hold items suspended there indefinitely (see "Directional Fans") — without this, a stream of coins held at the same point in a Fan's cone just overlapped infinitely instead of spreading out, since nothing above the seabed band ever pushed them apart (per direct bug report). It runs `ITEM_COLLISION_ITERATIONS` (4) sub-passes per tick so a push at the top of a stack can propagate down through several layers within a single tick, rather than one layer per tick.
- Resolution combines two things applied symmetrically to *both* items in an overlapping pair (no "anchor" side any more): a direct positional correction along the center-to-center line (guarantees no overlap remains immediately after — what keeps this stable no matter how deep a pile gets) plus a small velocity kick (`ITEM_PUSH_IMPULSE_SPEED`) so a shoved item keeps visibly drifting for a moment instead of snapping into place and stopping dead. `ITEM_HORIZONTAL_DAMPING` (0.82/tick, applied in `stepItemOnGrid`) is what brings that drift back to a natural stop rather than oscillating or running away forever. `ITEM_PUSH_IMPULSE_SPEED` is deliberately small (3, down from an initial 20) — at the higher value, a steady stream of coins landing near the same spot could each add enough sideways (and, since the push direction always carries some vertical component too — see the vertical-fraction cap below — upward) velocity that gravity couldn't pull a freshly-arrived coin back down to the pile's actual resting height before the next one landed, producing a visibly unnatural diagonal "staircase" of coins marching up and off the edge of whatever they were piled on instead of settling flush in a row. The positional correction alone (bounded by actual overlap depth, so it can't run away) is what actually guarantees non-overlap; the impulse is now just enough residual motion to read as a gentle settle.
- Both the positional correction and the velocity kick are weighted by relative mass (`item.mass`, an explicit field set at creation — see `ITEM_MASS_BY_TYPE` below — not derived from radius): the heavier item's fraction of the push is the *lighter* item's mass over the total, so a heavy coin barely moves while a light food pellet gets shoved well clear. Both halves have to be weighted, not just the position — the velocity kick dominates a multi-tick shove far more than the one-shot positional correction does, so an early version that mass-weighted only the position still let a coin drift sideways almost as far as the food pellet that hit it.
- `pushDirection` uses the *true* center-to-center angle whenever it already has a meaningful horizontal component, so an item rolls toward whichever side it's actually leaning, proportional to how far off-center it landed — a 2%-off-center landing and a 40%-off-center landing resolve differently, not identically. Only a landing close enough to dead-center to be a genuine unstable-equilibrium case (no real lean at all, which would otherwise balance on the peak forever — a real coin wouldn't actually hold that, but the simulation has no physical noise to break the tie with) gets a small fixed nudge substituted in via `ITEM_MIN_HORIZONTAL_PUSH_FRACTION` (0.15), just enough that nothing perfectly stacks. An earlier version instead *clamped* every landing whose vertical component exceeded a threshold down to one fixed diagonal regardless of true offset, which read as items sliding along a fixed-angle "flat ceiling" instead of rolling proportionally to where they actually landed.
- The positional correction is additionally capped per pairwise resolution by `ITEM_MAX_PUSH_PER_STEP` (3px), regardless of how deep the true overlap is. Two coins spawned at (almost) the exact same spot — a tight fish swarm dropping coins on top of each other in open water, where nothing separates them until they cross into the seabed band together — can arrive already overlapping by nearly their full diameter; resolving that in one uncapped shot could reposition a coin far enough to cross back above `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` into open water, where it then free-falls under the open-water gravity branch with whatever velocity it had, reading as a coin launching itself out of the pile into the water for no reason. Clamping the per-call correction means a severe overlap just takes a few extra ticks (still resolved within well under a second, since `resolveItemCollisions` reruns every tick) instead of teleporting.
- Landing on top of *another item* (as opposed to a tile) clamps that item's `vy` down to `ITEM_ON_ITEM_LANDING_VY_CAP` (24px/s) instead of leaving it alone. `sweepVertical` already zeroes `vy` the instant an item lands on a solid *tile*, but nothing did the equivalent for item-on-item contact — and `stepItemOnGrid`'s gravity integration runs unconditionally every tick regardless of resting status (see its module comment), so a coin resting on top of another coin had its `vy` climb under gravity every single tick, each time caught by a full-strength positional correction: a genuine "impact" every tick, forever. That was the dominant cause of coins visibly bumping/sliding along the top of a pile instead of settling — worse than the angle issue above. The fix isn't a hard zero, though: a first attempt at exactly that made re-penetration per tick (and thus the corrective roll-off) so tiny — under 0.03px — that an off-center landing took upward of 20 simulated seconds to visibly finish rolling off, reading as "stuck" rather than settled. A capped-but-nonzero residual fall speed keeps the roll-off brisk (under ~2 seconds) while staying far enough below `MAX_FALL_SPEED` that it can't regenerate a large, `ITEM_MAX_PUSH_PER_STEP`-clamped overlap the way an unclamped full-speed fall did.

`ITEM_MASS_BY_TYPE` (`Config.js`) is `{ food: 0.3, coin: 3, waste: 1 }`, with a coin's mass additionally scaled by its value tier's `sizeMultiplier` (a diamond coin is heavier than a bronze one, same scale as its size).
