Verified via direct Node sims (a resting item resumes falling the instant its support tile is removed — confirms no permanent anchoring; dropping a second coin onto an already-resting one visibly displaces it while staying non-overlapping; a pile of coins crowded onto a narrow 3-tile platform pushes at least one off the edge and it falls past the platform, propagating through the stack; a coin barely moves when it collides with food while the food gets shoved roughly 10x as far, matching the mass ratio; a 40-item stress pile over 10 simulated seconds produces no NaN positions and no runaway drift; an item with nothing built anywhere still falls off the bottom of the world and reports `'lost'`) and end-to-end in a browser (a Dartfin swarm dropping coins onto a wall strip with one Collector tile in the middle spreads coins into a continuous non-overlapping row across the strip rather than stacking, with only coins actually landing on the Collector auto-banked — the rest pile up and have to be clicked or routed by hand).

## Coordinate system & world constants

All defined in `Config.js`, consumed everywhere else — never hardcode these elsewhere.

- `TILE_SIZE = 32` px
- `WORLD_TILES_W = 60`, `WORLD_TILES_H = 47` → world is `1920 x 1504` px. `WORLD_TILES_W` was cut from 160 (5120px) to 60 (1920px) per direct request — "reduce the width of the tank to just the width of a full screen monitor... only up and down [panning]" — see the Current Phase changelog's "World shrunk to one screen width" entry for the camera/Ambience-density follow-on changes this required.
- Water column: tile rows `0..26` (27 rows)
- Seabed grid: tile rows `27..46` (20 rows) — the bottom 4 of these (`43..46`) sit below `ROCK_SHELF_Y`, the hard physics floor every item rests on (see below)
- `SEABED_ROW_START = 27` — the authoritative source for the water/seabed boundary; Phase 1 renders this as a flat floor, Phase 2 replaces it with real tiles, but both read this same constant

**Rule:** gameplay logic never operates in screen coordinates. `Engine.js` exports `worldToScreen(x, y)` and `screenToWorld(x, y)`; all input hit-testing and all rendering go through them. Entities outside the camera view are culled before drawing.

## Fixed-timestep contract

`requestAnimationFrame` drives rendering only. Simulation advances on a fixed 60Hz accumulator (`SIM_DT_MS = 1000/60` in `Config.js`) with a max-frames-per-update clamp so a tab stall doesn't spiral. Any physics/gravity/collision code reads `SIM_DT_MS`, never the rAF delta — this matters starting Phase 2 when gravity meets grid tiles (variable delta = tunneling).

`state.debug.timeScale` (1/2/5/10/0) multiplies how many fixed steps run per rAF frame; it does not change `SIM_DT_MS` itself.

## Module responsibilities

- **main.js** — entry point. Owns the root `state` object's initial shape. Calls into `Levels.js` to load the first level through the real load path. Wires the rAF loop to `Engine.js`. Also owns the two small input-driven state machines that don't cleanly belong in any one system module: the Fan two-click aiming flow (`fanAimingCell`) and the Economy Fish Combining drag (`draggedFishId`/`fishDragArmed`) — both are pure input/UI-state bookkeeping around calls into Grid.js/Entities.js, not simulation logic themselves. **Forbidden:** no gameplay logic, no direct entity manipulation.
- **Engine.js** — fixed-timestep accumulator loop, camera (position/zoom/clamping/pan input), raw input handling (keyboard/mouse state), `worldToScreen`/`screenToWorld`. **Forbidden:** no knowledge of fish/items/grid contents; it moves the camera and hands out coordinates, nothing more.
- **Entities.js** — `Fish` (species-table-driven), `Alien`, `Food`, `Item` (coin/waste/science). Owns `state.level.entities` and `state.level.items` contents and their per-tick behavior; also awards `state.level.tankPoints` on fish adult-growth transitions and writes the one-time Tank Points tutorial line to `state.level.notifications` (see Tank Points & Tank Upgrades). Also owns Economy Fish Combining/Splicing's mechanics (`getFishPurchaseCost`, `findFishAt`, `isCombinableFish`/`canCombineFish`/`combineFish`) and the T5 hybridization value-carry-over pipeline (`getEconomyAdultDropValue`/`getHybridSpeciesId`/`createHybridFish`) — see those sections. **Forbidden:** no rendering (that's UI/main render pass), no tile placement.
- **Grid.js** — seabed tile array, gravity/Fan-force physics for items once they reach the seabed (and, via `computeFanForce`/`integrateItemForces`, in open water too — see "Directional Fans"), ramp/collector/Auto-Feeder routing, Platform anchoring (`canPlaceTile`), and item-item collision (`resolveItemCollisions` — see "Items can't stack, and can fall off the bottom"). Owns `state.level.grid` and `state.level.buildingData`. **Forbidden:** no fish logic, no camera math.
- **Mound.js** — the Mound: click hit-testing (`isPointOnMound`), `crackMound()` (spends money, advances `state.level.tier`, grants that tier's unlocks into `state.meta`, writes to `state.level.notifications`), and its own render. Does **not** own the "Throw money at it" popup itself — that's a DOM modal in `UI.js` (`openMoundMenu`/`closeMoundMenu`), which `crackMound()` gets called from. **Forbidden:** no per-tick simulation of its own (it's a click target plus a render, not something that ticks every frame like Systems.js will).
- **Systems.js** — cleanliness/toxicity, eel power balance, science accumulation, wave timers (still Phase 3-5 scope, not yet built), plus its first real code: the bankruptcy bailout/game-over check and the 2-minute Escape dare (`updateStoryTriggers`, called every tick from `main.js`'s `update()`) — see "Story & Tutorial Notifications." Cross-cutting simulation systems that read/write multiple parts of `state.level` (and `state.meta.scienceTotal`). **Forbidden:** no rendering, no input handling.
- **UI.js** — shop panel (fish grid, then Food/Demolish tools + building palette below it — reordered so fish are closest to the top per direct request), Tank Upgrades panel (Phase 2 — see Tank Points & Tank Upgrades), the Mound's "throw money" popup, the main HUD (money/food-count/cleanliness), sidebars, tech tree modal, debug overlay rendering. Reads state, writes only via explicit user actions (buy, place tile, toggle debug). **Forbidden:** no simulation logic (e.g. UI must not tick hunger or move items).
- **Levels.js** — `LEVELS` data array, level load/unload (rebuilds `state.level` from scratch, never touches `state.meta` except to grant rewards on completion), win/lose condition checks.
- **Config.js** — all constants, balance numbers, the `SPECIES` table, world/tile constants, `SIM_DT_MS`. Pure data, no logic. Every balance constant carries a comment explaining its pacing intent.
- **Ambience.js** — purely decorative background rendering: rising bubbles and swaying seabed-floor seaweed (`updateAmbience`/`renderAmbience`, ticked every frame from `main.js` exactly like a real sim system, but entirely self-contained module-local cosmetic state that nothing else ever reads). No gameplay effect, ever. **Forbidden:** no reading or writing `state.level` beyond the camera it needs for screen-space conversion.
- **Sound.js** — synthesized SFX + a looping background track, generated entirely via the Web Audio API (oscillators/noise buffers, no audio file assets — see "Sound: synthesized SFX + a looping 8-bit-style background track" in the Current Phase changelog). Exports one fire-and-forget `playXxx()` function per event type plus `resumeAudio()` (the one-time unlock main.js calls on the page's first real user gesture, which also starts the music). Imported directly into Entities.js/Grid.js/UI.js at the exact point each event already happens — the same "call it inline, don't bubble a return value up" pattern those modules already use for floating texts and notifications. **Forbidden:** no gameplay logic; every export is a side effect only, never something a caller's control flow depends on.

## Debug overlay & cheat keys (Phase 1+)

Toggled by `` ` ``. Overlay shows: FPS, sim tick rate, entity count, item count, camera position, world coords of cursor, tile type under cursor (from Phase 2 on).

- `+`/`-` — time scale 1x/2x/5x/10x, and 0x pause
- `M` — grant $10,000 and 20 Tank Points (`CHEAT_TANK_POINTS_GRANT_AMOUNT`), so both the Mound and the Tank Upgrades panel can be tested without grinding
- `G` — spawn selected species at cursor; `Shift+G` spawns it fully grown
- `U` — unlock all species and tech
- `K` — clear all items
- Phase 2: `T` cycles tile under cursor; `N` force-cracks the Mound to the next tier for free, skipping the tease if it hasn't happened yet (grants that tier's meta unlocks immediately, for quickly testing Tier progression without grinding money); `P` toggles the Tank Upgrades panel (mirrors `S` for the shop)
- Phase 3: spike toxicity, zero toxicity, toggle power blackout
- Phase 4: fill science
- `Y` — force the next Alien Invasion wave to start immediately, skipping the real 3-5 minute `ALIEN_WAVE_INTERVAL_MIN/MAX_MS` gap — see the Alien Invasion changelog entry
- Phase 5+: TBD, pending the design pass noted in Species Roster & Progression above

## Current Phase

**Phase 1 complete**, plus substantial iteration on top of it since: the shop is now an icon grid with an in-panel preview (not per-row buy buttons or a modal), the shop and HUD got a full bright-pastel visual pass, camera panning is WASD/arrows/scroll-wheel only (no edge-scroll), fish got a growth-stage visual ladder (baby/fin/eye) and now roam/spawn across the whole tank with edge margins, coin values got a bronze/silver/gold/diamond tier system with size+color+click-radius all derived from it, and a pause menu (Escape; Resume/Restart/Settings) now sits in front of everything. See git-less history in this conversation for the blow-by-blow; the architecture sections above are current.

**Species roster revised** (see Species Roster & Progression above) — down to 6 base + 12 Gene-Splicing hybrids, replacing the old Tier 2-4/exotics draft. `Config.js` now scaffolds all 18 rows (`unlockedByDefault: false` except the 3 Tier-1 feeders); Phase 5+ combat content is unbuilt and undefined pending future direction.

**Phase 2 complete**, including a mid-phase redesign: Funnel and Vacuum Pipe were cut (they worked but didn't create any interesting outcome) in favor of the Blaster, and building/species unlocking moved from "available from level start" to the Mound-driven Tier system. `state.level.grid` is a real tile array (`Grid.js`'s `createGrid()`, all `TILE_EMPTY` until built on). Five base buildings (`Config.js`'s `BUILDING_TYPES`, all `unlockedByDefault: false` now — nothing is available until the Mound cracks to Tier 2): Wall, Ramp Left/Right, Collector, Blaster. See Tier Progression & The Mound above for the Blaster's launch mechanic and the waste-on-consume Tier 2 downside.

Once an item's `y` crosses `SEABED_FLOOR_Y`, `Entities.js`'s `updateFood`/`updateCoin`/`updateWaste` hand its motion off to `Grid.js`'s `stepItemOnGrid` for the rest of its life (see Grid.js's module comment for the exact contract: it returns `'falling'`/`'resting'`/`'consumed'`/`'lost'`, and the caller — not Grid.js — banks money/spawns Waste/sets rest flags, since Entities.js owns items' contents). Falling is swept in `GRID_SWEEP_SUBSTEP`-sized sub-steps so a landing tile can never be skipped over in one step; a Ramp doesn't arrest motion in either direction, it just nudges an item passing through its row one tile sideways and lets gravity carry on (see "Ramps ('funnels')" above — this replaced an earlier fixed-speed diagonal-slide model). Side/top out-of-bounds tile reads are treated as solid, but the *bottom* edge no longer is — see "Items can't stack, and can fall off the bottom" below, a mid-Phase-2 redesign that replaced the original "an unbuilt level comes to rest at the world's bottom" behavior. (The Blaster mentioned in this section's original write-up — a solid tile that relaunched anything landing on it straight up — was later removed entirely in favor of Directional Fans; see that section above and the Current Phase entry at the bottom of this doc.)

Build mode: clicking a building in the shop's tool-bar (`UI.js`'s `buildBuildPalette`) populates the same in-panel preview window species use (`selectBuildingForPreview` — no Buy button, since clicking arms the tool immediately rather than requiring a separate purchase step) and sets `state.ui.selectedTool` to `'build:<tileId>'`; a left-click-and-drag places one tile per newly-entered grid cell (`main.js`'s `updateBuildDrag`, called from `update()`), right-click removes whatever tile is under the cursor from anywhere (`Engine.js`'s `contextmenu` handler / `input.rightClickHandlers`) and refunds `cost * TILE_REFUND_FRACTION`. A translucent ghost tile previews the placement under the cursor, green/red for valid/invalid (`Grid.js`'s `renderBuildGhost`). `T` cheats a free cycle through every tile type at the cursor, ignoring cost/occupancy (`Grid.js`'s `cycleTileCheat`). The debug overlay (`` ` ``) shows the real tile id under the cursor, an items-routed-per-minute readout sampled the same way FPS/sim-steps already were, and the current Tier.

**Also new this phase:** the Mound, Tier progression, Waste, and the rolling notification ticker — all documented in their own section above (Tier Progression & The Mound; Rolling Notification Log). Every species/building is now gated behind reaching the right Tier via the Mound rather than being available from level start (`Levels.js`'s `allowedSpecies`/`allowedBuildings` list everything the game currently has; the Mound is what actually reveals it into `state.meta`).

Verified: direct Node-module physics sims (wall landing, Collector consumption + Waste spawn + money banked, single-tile and 4-tile-staircase ramp slides, Blaster launch height at both the shallowest and deepest placement — ~50% and ~65% of tank height respectively, matching spec — Blaster oscillation over ~100 simulated seconds, unbuilt-level world-bottom fallback, Mound `canCrackMound`/`crackMound`/`isPointOnMound` including the insufficient-funds and already-shattered cases) all match expected positions/status exactly. End-to-end in a real browser: species/building shop counts correct at Tier 1 (3 species, 0 buildings) and after a Tier 2 crack (4 species, 5 buildings); Mound renders correctly at the seabed's horizontal center with a crack line and an affordability-colored cost label that update live; notification pill/log populate and expand correctly; a 15-Dartfin swarm plus a Collector strip run at 10x for 6 real seconds (~1 sim-minute) routed items with zero console errors and steady 60 FPS (Entities hit 0 by the end only because the stress test never fed them — expected starvation, not a Phase 2 bug). Existing pause/restart/collapse regression suite still passes unchanged.

**Also this phase, after Phase 2's initial pass:** the Mound's popup went through two design iterations (see Tier Progression & The Mound's Tier 1 bullet) — first replacing its on-canvas "Throw $X at it" label with a centered, dimmed, pause-menu-style modal, then that modal itself got reworked into a smaller, undimmed popup that flies out of/back into the Mound and tracks it if the camera pans. The Mound also grew 10% (`MOUND_WIDTH_TILES`/`MOUND_HEIGHT_PX`), and gained the tease mechanic (its first-ever "throw money" attempt, at `MOUND_TEASE_COST`, is a red herring that spends the money and jokes about it in the notification ticker instead of cracking anything — `state.level.moundTeased` tracks whether that's already happened; the real Tier costs afterward jumped to `{1: 1000, 2: 5000, 3: 25000}`).

**Tank Points & Tank Upgrades** (see its own section above) is fully built, not just scaffolded: `Entities.js` awards a Tank Point per fish reaching adulthood (with a one-time tutorial notification the very first time), and `UI.js`'s Tank Upgrades panel — the shop's structural twin, opened via a new 🏆 button stacked below the shop's own in a shared `#panel-toggle-cluster` (`P` hotkey, mirrors `S`) — lets the player spend them on real, live-applied Food Quality and Fish Movement upgrades (Defensive Capabilities shown locked, Phase 5). Building this button cluster required a small refactor: both panels' own collapse buttons moved out of their panel's DOM (previously nested in the panel's header row) into this shared always-on-top cluster, and a collapsed panel now shrinks to `width:0` (invisible) instead of the old 56px colored strip — necessary once two panels needed to share the same on-screen slot without one's collapsed state visually colliding with the other's button. Every `SPECIES` row's `swimSpeed` in `Config.js` is now 5 lower than its originally-tuned value (`FISH_MOVEMENT_UPGRADE_SPEED_BONUS`) — buying the first Fish Movement level restores it. `FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_FLAT`/`FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_FRACTION` are gone, replaced by `FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_BY_LEVEL`, and the hunger-relief floor clamp is gone too (hunger can go negative — "overfed" — with no effect wired to it yet). Verified via direct Node sims (Tank Point awarded exactly on the adult transition and only then, first-time-only tutorial notification, no exploit via cheat-spawning an already-grown fish, Fish Movement's seek-speed bump landing exactly on the expected value) and end-to-end in a browser (panel open/close and the shop/Tank mutual-exclusion in both directions via button and hotkey, a purchase updating the card's level/description/cost/points display live, the shop's own buy flow still working after the button-cluster refactor). Existing regression suite still passes unchanged.

**Item collision physics rewritten from "settle and freeze" to continuous granular physics**, directly in response to playtest feedback that the first version wasn't behaving like real coins — a formed pile was immune to gravity and immune to being pushed once marked resting, which read as items snapping into a fixed structure. Fixed by removing every early-return-if-resting gate in `Entities.js`'s `updateCoin`/`updateWaste`/`updateFood` (`resting`/`restingOnFloor` are now re-evaluated fresh every tick instead of one-way flips) and rewriting `Grid.js`'s `resolveItemCollisions` so every seabed item is compared against every other one every tick regardless of resting status, with pushes weighted by an explicit `item.mass` field (`ITEM_MASS_BY_TYPE` in Config.js — food is ~10x lighter than a coin) rather than the old radius approximation — see "Items can't stack, and can fall off the bottom" above for the full mechanism, including the `MAX_SETTLE_ATTEMPTS`/`justSettledThisTick`/`findClearSettlePosition` machinery that got deleted entirely along with the one-way settle model. One bug caught during verification: the positional correction was mass-weighted from the start, but the velocity impulse (`ITEM_PUSH_IMPULSE_SPEED`) initially wasn't, so a heavy coin still drifted almost as far sideways as a light food pellet it collided with, since the repeated-every-tick velocity kick dominates a multi-tick shove far more than the one-shot position correction does — fixed by weighting the impulse by the same mass fraction. Verified via direct Node sims (11/11 passing: no permanent anchoring — a resting item resumes falling the instant its support is removed; dropping a coin on a resting one visibly displaces it while staying non-overlapping; a crowded narrow platform pushes an edge item off entirely, confirming a push propagates through a stack; a coin moved ~1.76px against food's ~18.5px in a head-on collision, confirming the ~10x mass ratio actually reads through; a 40-item stress pile over 10 simulated seconds produced no NaN/runaway positions; world-bottom loss still works) and end-to-end in a browser (a Dartfin swarm over a wall-strip-plus-Collector spread coins into a continuous non-overlapping row rather than stacking, zero console errors). Full existing regression suite (restart, pause/collapse, species-unlock) still passes unchanged.

**Follow-up tuning pass on the same system**, per direct playtest feedback on a screenshot showing coins landing on a wall strip forming a visibly unnatural diagonal staircase climbing up and off the platform's edge instead of settling into the row: `ITEM_PUSH_IMPULSE_SPEED` was cut from 20 to 3 (see that constant's comment in Config.js). At 20, a steady stream of new coins landing near the same spot could each add enough velocity — including a forced vertical component, since `pushDirection`'s vertical-fraction cap means a near-dead-center landing is never resolved as purely horizontal — that gravity couldn't pull a freshly-arrived coin back down to the pile's actual height before the next one landed, so each new arrival ended up resting progressively higher and further over than the last. The bounded positional correction (not the velocity impulse) is what actually guarantees non-overlap, so shrinking the impulse doesn't reopen the original stacking problem — verified by a direct Node sim dropping 15 coins one at a time onto the same spot on a long strip: every coin now settles at exactly the same height (0px vertical deviation) in a tight single-file row centered on the drop point, and a repeat of the narrow-3-tile-platform stress test still successfully knocks an edge coin off, confirming push propagation through a stack still works. End-to-end in a browser: a swarm of Dartfin parked over a deliberately too-short 5-tile strip (guaranteed overflow) produced 8 coins sitting flush in a single row with zero staircase, zero console errors. Full existing regression suite still passes unchanged.

**Second follow-up: a screenshot showing coins airborne near the Mound, well above the seabed**, per feedback that coins would still occasionally shove themselves "way more than the coins under it, which isn't how gravity works." Root cause was different from the staircase bug: `resolveItemCollisions`'s positional correction was sized directly off the true overlap depth (`minDist - dist`) with no cap. A tight fish swarm parked over one spot drops multiple coins that can spawn at (almost) the exact same position and fall through open water in lockstep — nothing separates items above `SEABED_FLOOR_Y`, since `resolveItemCollisions` only ever looks at the seabed band — so by the time they cross into the seabed band together they can be overlapping by nearly their full diameter. Resolving that much overlap in one shot flung a coin far enough to cross back above the seabed line into open water, where it then continued as a free ballistic projectile under the open-water gravity branch, looking exactly like a coin launching itself out of the pile for no reason. Fixed by adding `ITEM_MAX_PUSH_PER_STEP` (3px) and clamping the per-pairwise-resolution `overlap` to it before computing the correction — a severe overlap now just takes a few extra ticks to visibly separate instead of teleporting (see "Items can't stack" above for the mechanism). Verified via a direct Node sim spawning 8 coins at sub-pixel-apart positions directly in the seabed band (the worst case): max single-tick vertical movement stayed at 1.2px (matching ordinary free-fall speed, not a jump), no coin ever crossed back above the seabed line after landing, and all 8 settled into a flush non-overlapping row — plus the existing narrow-platform and mass-asymmetry tests still pass, confirming the clamp didn't reopen either prior fix. End-to-end in a browser, a dense 12-Dartfin swarm packed tightly over a wall-strip-plus-Collector (the same scenario that originally produced the airborne-coins screenshot) produced two clean flush rows with nothing airborne, zero console errors. Full existing regression suite still passes unchanged.

**Third follow-up: coins visibly sliding/bumping along the top of a pile "as if moving across a flat ceiling"** instead of rolling naturally into gaps. Two compounding root causes, both in `Grid.js`: (1) `pushDirection` *clamped* every landing whose vertical component was steep enough down to one fixed diagonal (`ITEM_MAX_VERTICAL_PUSH_FRACTION`, 0.6) regardless of true offset — a 2%-off-center landing and a 40%-off-center landing resolved identically, which is what actually produced the "same fixed angle every time" flat-ceiling look; and (2) more significantly, `sweepVertical` only ever zeroes an item's `vy` when it lands on a solid *tile* — nothing did the equivalent for landing on *another item*, and `stepItemOnGrid`'s gravity integration runs unconditionally every tick (by design, so nothing is ever permanently anchored — see above), so a coin resting on top of another coin had its `vy` climb under gravity every tick, each time caught by a full-strength positional correction: a genuine "impact" every single tick, forever. Fixed both: `pushDirection` now preserves the true angle and only substitutes a small fixed nudge (`ITEM_MIN_HORIZONTAL_PUSH_FRACTION`, 0.15) for the genuinely near-dead-center case, so off-center landings roll proportionally instead of snapping to a fixed diagonal; and item-on-item landings now clamp (not zero) `vy` via `ITEM_ON_ITEM_LANDING_VY_CAP` (24px/s). A hard zero was tried first and made things worse in a different way — re-penetration per tick dropped to under 0.03px, so a near-center landing took upward of 20 simulated seconds to finish rolling off, reading as "stuck" rather than settled; the capped-but-nonzero version settles the same landing in under 2 seconds. Verified via direct Node sims (14/14 passing, including two new cases: a coin dropped dead-center on top of another now fully settles and stops creeping within ~2 simulated seconds and stays put for 5+ seconds afterward with zero further drift, and two coins built at a fixed overlap depth but different approach angles resolve with proportionally different horizontal movement — 0.454px at a shallow angle vs 0.891px at a steep one, not identical) plus every prior regression (no permanent anchoring, narrow-platform knock-off, mass asymmetry, coincident-spawn teleport safety, stress-pile stability, world-bottom loss) still passing, some needing longer tick budgets since the gentler physics settles over more ticks rather than fewer, larger corrections. End-to-end in a browser, the same dense-swarm scenario produced two clean flush rows, zero console errors, and noticeably more items successfully routed through the Collector than before (10 vs 4-5), consistent with piles settling into gaps more reliably instead of getting stuck skittering. Full existing regression suite still passes unchanged.

**General de-pacing pass, per direct request to slow the game down through strategic changes** (not a bug fix, a balance pass): `FOOD_GRAVITY`/`FOOD_MAX_FALL_SPEED` cut another 25% (44→33, 36→27, stacking on top of the earlier 20% cut noted in Config.js's comments — food now falls noticeably more slowly, giving fish and the player more time to react/aim), `FOOD_QUALITY_SINK_SPEED_REDUCTION_PER_LEVEL` doubled from 5% to 10% per Tank Upgrade level (so that upgrade now meaningfully slows food further rather than being a marginal tweak), and every species' `hungerRate` cut 20% (all 18 rows in `Config.js`, base feeders and hybrids alike) so fish need feeding less frequently across the board.

Also rebalanced the base 3 feeders' coin-value growth curve, since the *effective* $/sec income (dropValue ÷ dropInterval, what a player actually perceives) was climbing 4-6x from hatchling to adult even though the raw `dropValue` numbers alone only looked like ~2-2.6x — the extra multiplier was hiding in each stage's `dropInterval` also shrinking. Baby `dropValue` is unchanged for all 3 (Guppy $5, Dartfin $3, Blimpfish $16); mid/adult values were brought down so the *effective* income ratio lands close to double instead: Guppy is now flat at `5, 5, 5` (its own `dropInterval` shrink alone already delivers a ~1.9x income ratio, so no dropValue growth was needed at all), Dartfin similarly flat at `3, 3, 3` (~2.4x from interval shrink alone), and Blimpfish — whose interval alone only shrinks the income ratio ~1.4x — got a modest dropValue bump to `16, 17, 22` to reach a comparable ~2.0x. This is also why the 9 feeder-based hybrids' `dropValue` (1.2x their feeder parent's *adult* value — see the new Gene-Splicing note above) reads lower than before: Guppy-based hybrids dropped from 7 to 6, Dartfin-based from 3 to 4 (a rare increase, since old Dartfin-hybrid values predated this rebalance entirely and happened to sit below the new 1.2x-of-adult formula), Blimpfish-based from 20 to 26.

**Ramps reworked from a fixed-speed diagonal slide into a pass-through nudge, and the Blaster's launch got an angle**, both per direct feedback and both documented in full under "Ramps ('funnels')" and "The Blaster" above. Ramps no longer arrest vertical motion at all (removed from `Grid.js`'s `SOLID_TILES` entirely) — an item falling or rising through one keeps its `vy` completely untouched and just gets shifted one tile sideways (`RAMP_NUDGE_DISTANCE`) as it passes through that row, replacing the old `item.slidingRamp`/`stepRampSlide` mechanism (deleted) that rode a fixed `RAMP_SLIDE_SPEED` 45-degree slope and read as a sticky conveyor belt. The Blaster's launch now splits its speed into a vx/vy pair by up to `BLASTER_LAUNCH_ANGLE_MAX_DEG` (7°) based on exactly where across the tile the item landed, rather than a pure vertical shot — which surfaced a real pre-existing gap: `Entities.js`'s open-water branch for coins/waste only ever integrated `item.y`, never `item.x`, so a nonzero `vx` had zero visible effect for the majority of a Blaster's arc (which typically leaves the seabed band). Fixed by adding `item.x += item.vx * dt` to both open-water branches. Both tiles also got a matching visual pass: Ramps render as a triangle pointing the direction they push (`Grid.js`'s `renderTileShape`, replacing the plain square every building used to share) and the Blaster's top two corners are now slightly rounded (`BLASTER_TOP_CORNER_RADIUS_FRACTION`), bottom left square since it still sits flush on whatever's underneath it. Verified via 10 direct Node sim tests (a falling item's vy is provably unchanged crossing a Ramp row while landing one tile over; a rising item off a simulated Blaster launch behaves the same way; lingering in the same Ramp row for 30 ticks only ever nudges once, not every tick; a dead-center Blaster hit launches with ~0 horizontal velocity; a hit near the tile's edge tilts within the ±7° band; launch speed magnitude is identical regardless of angle, only direction changes) plus the full existing regression suite. End-to-end in a browser: tile shapes render correctly (left/right wedges, rounded-top Blaster) and a Dartfin parked over a Blaster visibly arcs sideways on repeated launches instead of retracing the same vertical line, zero console errors.

**Panel-active highlighting, a much steeper Tank Upgrade cost curve, a new Food Capacity upgrade, and the Collector's 3-second processing hold** — four separate requests landed together. (1) Both side-panel toggle buttons (🛍️/🏆) now get a `.panel-toggle-active` class (filled white ring, darker background) whenever their panel is the one currently open, since previously both looked identical regardless of state and it wasn't obvious which was open at a glance — see Tank Points & Tank Upgrades's "The panel" note. (2) All three purchasable Tank Upgrade trees moved from a flat `[1,2,3,4]` 4-level cost ladder to a much steeper shared `TANK_UPGRADE_COSTS = [2,5,15,30,50]` 5-level one — see that section's "Costs" note, including why `FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_BY_LEVEL` needed a 6th entry to match. (3) A new **Food Capacity** tree (`state.level.upgrades.foodCapacity`) caps how much food can exist on screen at once — `FOOD_MAX_ON_SCREEN_BASE` (2) + `FOOD_CAPACITY_UPGRADE_INCREMENT` (2) per level, enforced in `Entities.js`'s `trySpawnFood`/`effectiveFoodCapacity` the same way insufficient money already blocks a spawn. (4) The Collector's processing hold — see "The Collector" above for the full mechanism (pull-to-center, `COLLECTOR_PROCESS_DURATION_MS`, the temporary mass override, the circle render, the tile-removal interrupt case). Verified via 10 direct Node sim tests for the Collector alone (processing starts instead of instant consumption, exact ~3000ms timing, eases to dead-center within ~1 simulated second, tile removal mid-process releases the item with its mass restored) plus 5 more for food capacity (spawning stops exactly at the cap regardless of remaining money, upgrading the cap allows more to be spawned) — all passing — plus a dedicated Node-level swarm-pressure test (one coin guaranteed to land on a Collector alongside 15 more scattered across a wall strip around it, staggered arrival) confirming the held item stays put and still completes on schedule even under continuous collision pressure from neighbors, not just in isolation. End-to-end in a browser: the panel toggle buttons visibly highlight correctly in both directions, the Tank Upgrades panel renders all 4 cards (including the new Food Capacity one) with the new costs and level-of-5 ladders, and the Collector tile renders with its center circle. Full existing regression suite still passes unchanged (zero console errors across every test).

**Seabed Platform architecture: Blaster/Funnel removed, Wall renamed to Platform, Directional Fans + Auto-Feeder added, buildings must anchor to a Platform, item physics rewritten as continuous 2D force integration** — a major structural refactor per direct request, touching `Config.js`, `Grid.js`, `Entities.js`, `main.js`, and `Mound.js`. Full mechanism documented inline above (see "Directional Fans," "Auto-Feeder," and "Platform Anchoring" under Tier Progression & The Mound) — this entry is the changelog/rationale.
- **Removed:** the Blaster (`TILE_BLASTER`, `launchFromBlaster`, all `BLASTER_LAUNCH_*` constants) is gone entirely — landing-triggered relaunch is replaced by always-on directional Fan force. Funnel/Vacuum Pipe were already gone before this phase (cut earlier in Phase 2, see above); nothing further to remove there.
- **Renamed:** `TILE_WALL` -> `TILE_PLATFORM` (id string `'wall'` -> `'platform'`) everywhere — Config.js, Grid.js's `SOLID_TILES`/cheat-cycle, TIER_UNLOCKS, the shop's building row. No save-compat concern since save/load (Phase 5) isn't wired up yet.
- **Platform Anchoring:** every building except Platform must now be placed adjacent (orthogonal only) to a Platform tile or directly on the world's absolute bottom row — `Grid.js`'s `canPlaceTile`/`isAnchored`. This is new: placement previously only checked bounds/occupancy/cost, with no structural requirement at all.
- **Directional Fans** (`TILE_FAN_T2`/`_T3`/`_T4` — Rudimentary/Electric/Turbo): replace the Blaster's one-shot landing-triggered launch with a continuous, aimable force cone (±15°, linear falloff to `maxRange`) that acts on any item within it, seabed or open water. Aim is locked at placement from the exact cursor position within the tile (`Grid.js`'s `angleFromTileToPoint`), stored in the new `state.level.buildingData` map since a bare grid-cell string can't hold per-instance data. Rendered with a live aim arrow + translucent cone overlay. Tier-gated the same way every other building is (T2 free/short-range at Tier 2, T3 small power cost/medium range at Tier 3, T4 extreme force/long range at Tier 4) — power cost is tracked into a future power-grid hook but not yet gated on availability, consistent with every other not-yet-power-gated Electric building already in the codebase.
- **Auto-Feeder** (`TILE_AUTO_FEEDER`): new building, aimed the same way as a Fan (aim = output direction, intake is the opposite side). Absorbs any Waste that drifts within `AUTO_FEEDER_INTAKE_RADIUS` of its intake point (typically pushed there by a Fan), and after `AUTO_FEEDER_PROCESS_DURATION_MS` dispenses one Food item at its output point — a real productive use for Waste that exists even before Phase 3's real toxicity math does. Ticked by a new `Grid.js`'s `updateBuildings`, called from `Entities.js`'s `updateEntities`. Unlocked at Tier 2.
- **Multi-vector force physics:** `Grid.js`'s new `computeFanForce`/`integrateItemForces` replace the old flat "clamp vy to maxFallSpeed" gravity model with real force accumulation — `a = F_net/mass` where `F_net` sums gravity (`mass * g`, so acceleration stays mass-independent, unchanged from before) and every applicable Fan's thrust (mass-dependent, `F_fan/mass`), plus linear drag (`-drag * v`, `drag` derived per item type from its existing `gravity/maxFallSpeed` ratio so an un-pushed item's terminal velocity is bit-for-bit unchanged from before this system existed). Applies identically in open water (`Entities.js`'s per-item update functions, replacing their old ad hoc gravity-only integration) and the seabed band (inside `stepItemOnGrid`), so a Fan's push doesn't behave differently depending which side of `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` an item is on. `ITEM_MASS_BY_TYPE` (already existing, unchanged: food 0.3, waste 1, coin 3) now does double duty — it drove item-item collision weighting before, and now also drives how strongly a Fan's force actually accelerates each item type, which is the mechanical basis for the requested Mass Hierarchy (coins need strong/overlapping Fan coverage to clear a ledge; waste moves readily under a standard Fan; food readily launches/floats even on a Rudimentary Fan).
- Verified end-to-end in a real browser (Playwright, headless Chromium, driven via the debug overlay/cheat keys): after cracking the Mound to Tier 2, the build palette shows exactly Platform/Ramp Left/Ramp Right/Collector/Rudimentary Fan/Auto-Feeder (no Wall, no Blaster); a Collector placed with no Platform neighbor is correctly rejected (`Tile under cursor` stays `empty` after the placement attempt) while the identical placement one tile away from a Platform succeeds; a Rudimentary Fan placed adjacent to a Platform and aimed straight up renders its cone correctly, and a Food pellet dropped into that cone reaches a stable hover (Fan thrust balancing gravity for Food's light mass) instead of falling, holding position across 2+ seconds rather than drifting away — confirming the force+drag model reaches a genuine stable trajectory rather than accelerating without bound. Zero browser console errors across every scenario tested. The Auto-Feeder's Waste-absorption path was verified by code review (it reuses the same array-splice/status-return patterns already proven elsewhere in Grid.js/Entities.js) rather than a live end-to-end waste-generation run, since manufacturing real Waste requires a full fish-feeds-drops-coin-Collector-converts-it chain that takes tens of seconds of real sim time to set up reliably in an automated test.

**Economy Fish Combining/Splicing added, tier progression reshuffled twice in the same session (first inserting a new Tier 2, then moving Platform/Collector/Auto-Feeder again), Fan placement became a two-click aim confirmation, a dedicated Demolish tool replaced always-on right-click removal with a full refund, Fan ranges extended, the shop panel reordered, the main HUD gained a food-pellet counter, and the Fish Movement/Food Capacity Tank Upgrades split onto their own cheaper 9-level cost curve** — a long chain of related requests landed across one session; this entry is the consolidated changelog. Full mechanism for each piece is documented inline above (Economy Fish Combining/Splicing's own section; the Tier list; "Aiming" and "Demolish Tool" under Tier Progression & The Mound; Tank Points & Tank Upgrades' "Costs").
- **Tier reshuffle, final state:** Tier 1 = feeding + Platform (now `unlockedByDefault: true`, no crack needed at all — every other building anchors to it). Tier 2 = Rudimentary Fan + the whole Economy Fish Combining/dynamic-pricing system (gated on `state.level.tier >= 2`, not a building/species unlock). Tier 3 = Ramp Left/Right + Collector + Auto-Feeder + Suckerfish (this is the old "rudimentary factory, dirty" tier's full original content, just renumbered). Tier 4 = Electric Eel + Electric Fan. Tier 5 = the Mound-shatter reveal, Science Octopus + the 12 hybrids + Turbo Fan. `MOUND_MAX_TIER` is now 5 (was 4); `MOUND_CRACK_COST` gained a 4th entry, `{4: 50000}`, for the final Tier 4->5 crack (the explicitly requested cost) — the first three crack costs (1000/5000/25000) are untouched. `MOUND_TEASE_COST` ($150, the Tier-1 no-op) is unchanged. This went through two passes in the same session: the first pass bundled Platform in with the new Tier 2 (reasoning it needed to ship alongside the Fan/Auto-Feeder it anchors); a follow-up request explicitly pulled Platform out to Tier 1 and moved Collector/Auto-Feeder to Tier 3 alongside Ramp/Suckerfish, landing on the mapping above.
- **Economy Fish Combining/Splicing** (new — its own CLAUDE.md section has full detail): dynamic per-purchase pricing for Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish (`Entities.js`'s `getFishPurchaseCost`, `cost * 1.4^N` where N is how many of that species are currently alive, computed live so a death/combine/purchase is reflected the instant it happens), a drag-two-Adult-fish-of-the-same-species-and-star-tier-together mechanic (`isCombinableFish`/`canCombineFish`/`combineFish`, capped at star tier 4) built on two new Engine.js input-handler registries (`mouseDownHandlers`/`mouseUpHandlers`), 1.5x-per-tier coin value scaling applied live in `updateFish`, and a gold-star overlay on the base adult sprite (`FishRenderer.js`'s `drawStar`, counts `{1:0, 2:2, 3:3, 4:4}` per the exact design spec table, no new spritesheets). A forward-compatible value-carry-over pipeline for the still-unbuilt T5 Hybridization drag interaction was added alongside it (`getEconomyAdultDropValue`/`getHybridSpeciesId`/`createHybridFish`) — not wired to any UI yet, since the splice interaction itself remains Phase 4/5 scope.
- **Fan two-click aiming:** placement and aim direction split into two separate clicks (`main.js`'s `fanAimingCell`/`isFanAimingActive`) — click 1 arms a valid cell with no tile placed yet, the ghost then rotates live with the cursor from that fixed cell, click 2 confirms the angle and places it. Self-cancelling via a "still selected?" check rather than explicit cross-module teardown; Escape cancels it explicitly ahead of the pause menu. Scoped to Fans only — the Auto-Feeder kept its original single-click aim.
- **Demolish tool:** right-click no longer removes tiles unconditionally — removal now requires selecting a new Demolish tool (🗑️, next to Food in the shop's tool-bar) and either left- or right-clicking a built tile, with a red ghost-mode hover preview showing the exact refund. `TILE_REFUND_FRACTION` raised from 0.5 to 1.0 (a full refund) at the same time, since removal is deliberate now rather than an always-available shortcut.
- **Fan range increase:** Rudimentary Fan +2 tiles (96 -> 160px), Electric and Turbo Fan +3 tiles each (176 -> 272px, 320 -> 416px) — force magnitudes and cone width unchanged, just reach.
- **Shop panel reorder:** the fish species grid now sits above the Food/Demolish tool-bar and building palette (previously below them), so fish are the first thing visible under the preview window.
- **HUD food-pellet counter:** `#hud-food` (🍽️ current/cap), next to money and cleanliness, reads live off `state.level.items`/`effectiveFoodCapacity`.
- Verified end-to-end in a real browser (Playwright): Tier 1 build palette shows Platform only and it's placeable with zero cracks; Tier 2 crack adds only Rudimentary Fan; Tier 3 crack adds Ramp Left/Right/Collector/Auto-Feeder together; the shop's DOM order confirms species-grid before tool-bar; a Fan's first click leaves the tile empty (`Tile under cursor: empty`) and only the second click actually places it (`fan_t2`); selecting Demolish and clicking a placed Rudimentary Fan refunded exactly $15 (its full cost) and the tile went back to empty; right-clicking a Platform tile while Food (not Demolish) was selected left it untouched, confirming right-click no longer removes unconditionally; two cheat-spawned Adult Guppies dragged onto each other combined into one Tier-2 (2-star) Guppy, entity count dropping from 2 to 1 with a floating confirmation text and the star overlay visibly rendering; the dynamic Guppy price rose from $20 to $28 to $39 across two purchases (`20 * 1.4^N`, rounded, matching exactly); the Tank Upgrades panel showed Food Quality at 5 levels / Fish Movement and Food Capacity at 9 levels with the new cheaper cost floor. Zero console errors across every scenario. One test-authoring lesson worth keeping: hit-testing a fish by screen-pixel offset without first calibrating the live camera zoom/tile-size produced several false failures during this verification pass (aiming at the wrong tile column, a dragged fish always matching itself as its own hover target before `findFishAt`'s `excludeId` param was added) — none were actual game bugs, all were fixed by deriving screen offsets from the debug overlay's live `Cursor world` readout instead of assuming a 1:1 px-to-world-unit ratio.

**Item-item collision widened from "seabed band only" to everywhere** — a direct bug report: a Fan can hold coins suspended in open water indefinitely (see "Directional Fans"), and since `Grid.js`'s `resolveItemCollisions` had been scoped to `item.y >= SEABED_FLOOR_Y` since before Fans existed (open water had nothing to collide with back then), a stream of coins held at the same point in a Fan's cone just overlapped infinitely instead of spreading apart — nothing above the seabed band ever pushed them apart. Fixed by running it over every item in `state.level.items` unconditionally. Verified in a browser: two Food pellets dropped at nearly the same point inside a Fan's cone now visibly separate into two distinct circles instead of one overlapping blob, with no regressions in the existing seabed-band collision tests.

**Tier 1.5 (the Mound's tease grants the Rudimentary Fan), a batch of new story/tutorial notifications, "Welcome to the tank" made permanent, and every existing notification rewritten sarcastic/punny** — per direct request, the Mound's first "throw money" attempt is no longer a pure joke: it now grants the Rudimentary Fan (still without advancing `state.level.tier`), and `TIER_UNLOCKS[2]` no longer lists it since it's already been granted by that point — see "The tease ('Tier 1.5')" and "Story & Tutorial Notifications" above for full detail on both this and the notification system in general. New one-time triggers: first fish bought, first fish death (starvation), first building placed, first Fan placed, first Economy Fish combine, a 1000-lifetime-money-earned milestone (tracked via a new separate `state.level.lifetimeMoneyEarned` counter, NOT the spendable `state.level.money` balance), a 2-minute "press Escape, I dare you" prompt with a "Made ya look" follow-up if it actually worked, and a "You found the chat" gag on the first notification-log expand that freezes and hides every fish for 2.5 seconds before an unrelated "JK!" reveal. Also new: a two-strike bankruptcy system (`Systems.js`, its first real code) — no fish alive AND unable to afford anything triggers a one-time $100 bailout, then game over on the second occurrence (`state.level.gameOver`, frozen the same way as `state.ui.paused` but via a separate flag so Escape still reaches the pause menu's Restart). `state.level.notifications` is now seeded with the welcome message as a real array entry at level load (`Levels.js`) instead of a UI-layer fallback string, so it survives in the expandable scrollback log instead of vanishing the instant a real notification arrives — per direct feedback that this exact disappearing behavior was the problem. Verified end-to-end in a browser: the tease grants the Fan and shows its new line without advancing tier; the expanded log contains both the welcome message and the tease message; buying a fish, placing a building, placing a Fan, combining two fish, and starving a fish (sped up via the debug time-scale cheat) each fired their correct notification exactly once; cranking to 10x time scale and waiting past the 2-minute mark produced the dare, and the next Escape press produced "Made ya look" immediately; expanding the log for the first time hid a spawned fish completely (confirmed via the debug overlay's Entities count staying at 1 throughout, proving it was hidden, not removed) and it reappeared with the JK message on schedule; draining starting money to zero via repeated Food purchases with no fish alive triggered the exact bailout text and grant. Zero console errors across every scenario.

**Phase 3 begins: real Cleanliness, Suckerfish's real Scavenger behavior, a fish depth-clamp bug fix, a Food-capacity flash, and another +1 tile of Fan range** — per direct request. `state.level.cleanliness` is no longer a static placeholder — see the new "Cleanliness (Phase 3)" note in `Config.js` and the `adjustCleanliness` helpers in `Entities.js`/`Grid.js`: every Waste item that spawns costs `CLEANLINESS_PER_WASTE_EVENT` (4), and every one cleaned up (Suckerfish eating it, or an Auto-Feeder absorbing it — the "buildings" half of the pairing) restores the same amount, clamped 0-100. `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` detects the direction it just moved (same `lastMoney`-style comparison already used for the money HUD) and flashes `#hud-cleanliness` — the money-only `.money-flash-pickup`/`.money-flash-spend` CSS classes were generalized to `.flash-pickup`/`.flash-spend` so the same bounce/shake pair could be reused here and for Food. Suckerfish finally has real behavior instead of just swimming around inert: `hungerRate` set to exactly half of Guppy's (1.015 vs 2.03), and `Entities.js`'s `updateFish` now branches on the `SCAVENGER` behavior tag to target Waste via a new `findNearestWaste` instead of Food — a Scavenger fish never eats Food at all. No gameplay effect from a low cleanliness value yet (fish stress/toxicity is still unbuilt, later Phase 3+ scope) — this lands the live-tracked value and its HUD feedback, not the downstream consequences.
- **Real bug found and fixed while verifying this live:** a fish's Y position was unconditionally clamped to `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` — the *top* edge of the seabed band, not wherever the item it's chasing actually is — so any fish (Suckerfish included) trying to reach an item resting more than a few rows deep in a built factory could never close the vertical gap, no matter how long it searched. This predates Suckerfish's real behavior entirely (it would have equally affected any Feeder fish chasing Food resting deep in a factory) but had never surfaced before since nothing previously needed to reach that deep reliably. Fixed by extending the Y clamp down to the fish's *current target's own depth* while one is actively being chased (`updateFish`'s new `seekTargetY`), falling back to the normal boundary the instant nothing's being chased. Also fixed in the same pass: any fish with a species-row `dropValue` of 0 (Suckerfish, and every other not-yet-behavior-wired utility species) no longer drops a coin on its `dropInterval` at all — it used to drop a genuinely worthless $0 coin that, absurdly, could still land on a Collector and spawn *more* Waste, directly undermining a Scavenger's whole purpose.
- **Food capacity flash:** attempting to place Food while already at the Food Capacity cap now flashes `#hud-food` red (`UI.js`'s new exported `flashFoodCapacity`, called from `main.js`'s click handler) — distinct from an insufficient-money attempt, which doesn't flash anything. `Entities.js`'s `trySpawnFood` changed its return contract from a bare bool to a reason string (`'spawned' | 'no_money' | 'capacity_full'`) so the caller can tell the two failure modes apart; it had exactly one caller, so this was a safe change.
- **Fan range +1 more tile each:** Rudimentary Fan 5->6 tiles (160->192px), Electric Fan 8.5->9.5 (272->304px), Turbo Fan 13->14 (416->448px) — the fourth such increase this session, force magnitudes still unchanged.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright): filling the Food cap then attempting a 3rd placement applied `flash-spend` to `#hud-food` (absent before, present immediately after); a wide Collector strip fed by a Dartfin swarm reliably dropped cleanliness as Waste piled up, confirmed via the debug overlay's `Items routed/min` total tracking 1:1 with `(100 - cleanliness) / 4`; after the depth-clamp fix, a hatchling Suckerfish spawned directly on a Waste-covered Collector strip produced a directly-observed cleanliness RISE (84% -> 88%) mid-run, proving the eat-Waste-restores-cleanliness path fires correctly (the same run's overall trend still nets downward, since concurrently-alive Dartfins kept outpacing one Suckerfish's cleanup rate — an artifact of that test's deliberately busy scenario, not a bug). Getting a clean, deterministic live reproduction of the Suckerfish-eats-Waste case took several iterations — real-time hunger-vs-travel-time budgets, wander drift over long pre-seek windows, and (unknowingly, until diagnosed) the depth-clamp bug itself all produced misleading dead-ends before landing on a scenario that isolated it clearly. Zero console errors across every scenario tested.

**The previous entry's depth-clamp "fix" was wrong and has been fully reverted, per direct correction — this is important design intent to retain.** The prior pass extended a fish's Y-clamp down to whatever depth its actively-chased target was resting at (`seekTargetY`), reasoning that a fish stuck at `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` could never physically reach an item sitting deep inside a built factory. The user corrected this directly: **fish are not supposed to be able to swim down into the seabed city at all — that's the entire reason Fans exist**, to push Food/Waste back up out of a factory and into the water column where fish can actually reach it. Letting a fish dive to an item's depth instead defeats the Fan-dependent routing loop that's the whole point of the seabed architecture. Fixed by deleting `seekTargetY` entirely and restoring the original unconditional clamp — `if (fish.y > SEABED_FLOOR_Y) { fish.y = SEABED_FLOOR_Y; fish.vy = -Math.abs(fish.vy); }`, no exceptions, ever. **Do not reintroduce any form of target-depth-following clamp relaxation** — a fish unable to reach a deep item is the intended pressure to build Fan routing, not a bug.

Landing in the same batch as the revert, all per one consolidated direct request:
- **Fish now produce Waste directly ("poop"), independent of the Collector byproduct** — see the rewritten "Waste" section above for the full mechanism (`poopTimer`, `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` = 25000ms, gated off `SCAVENGER` so Suckerfish don't poop). This was flagged as "bugged"/missing — the game had waste only ever coming from the Collector's dirty-automation byproduct, never from fish themselves, which didn't match the intended "fish poop" mental model.
- **Fan range +1 more tile each, cone widened:** Rudimentary Fan 6->7 tiles (192->224px), Electric Fan 9.5->10.5 (304->336px), Turbo Fan 14->15 (448->480px) — the fifth such range increase this session. `FAN_CONE_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` widened 15->20 (30°->40° total cone width) in the same pass, "slightly wider" per direct request. Force magnitudes unchanged throughout.
- **Economy fish cost growth rate cut 1.4 -> 1.25** (`ECONOMY_FISH_COST_GROWTH_RATE` in `Config.js`) — the dynamic per-purchase price curve for Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish felt too steep; verified live, a Guppy's price across two purchases now reads $20 -> $25 -> $31 instead of the old $20 -> $28 -> $39.
- **Higher-tier coin drops round up to a whole number:** both the live per-tick coin-drop calculation in `updateFish` and the T5 carry-over helper `getEconomyAdultDropValue` now wrap their star-tier-scaled result in `Math.ceil(...)` — a Tier-2 fish with an odd base value no longer drops something like $11.25, it drops $12. Verified correct by inspection: `Math.ceil` guarantees an integer output regardless of the input fraction, so this is true by construction, not just by the specific test values exercised live.
- **Food can no longer be placed inside the seabed city.** `Entities.js`'s `trySpawnFood(state, x, y)` now checks `y >= SEABED_FLOOR_Y` first and returns a new `'in_city'` reason (alongside the existing `'no_money'`/`'capacity_full'`/`'spawned'`) without spending money or creating an item — per direct feedback that city-targeted food placement was a leftover idea that just added friction ("I was wondering for a while if I should keep that in... but it's just so annoying"). Food still places normally anywhere in open water above the seabed line.
- **First-fish-death notification text finalized:** "Your fish is now swimming with the fishes. Oh wait...it just starved. You might want to try feeding your fish." (a trim of an earlier, wordier draft — see "Story & Tutorial Notifications" above, already current).
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): a Guppy spawned in open water with no buildings anywhere nearby produced a Waste item and a cleanliness drop purely from its own poop timer; a Suckerfish parked at a Collector row's depth was confirmed via a multi-sample cleanliness trend to never cause a rise, since it can no longer physically reach Waste resting deeper than `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` (the depth-clamp revert working as intended); a food-placement click deep in the seabed band left money and item count unchanged while the identical click in open water still spawned food normally; a placed Rudimentary Fan's cone rendered visibly wider and longer than its prior state, matching the tooltip's updated "224px" reach figure; combining two Adult Guppies into a Tier-2 fish and letting it drop a coin produced no fractional-dollar console/UI artifacts (the `Math.ceil` wrap makes a fractional result structurally impossible).

**Waste gets a fixed resting shelf, a visible break line, a much gentler cleanliness cost, a size bump, and a distinct color from Food; a low-cleanliness warning message added** — a consolidated batch, all per direct request, landing right after the depth-clamp/fan/economy batch above.
- **`WASTE_FLOOR_Y` — a virtual floor for uncaught Waste, 2 tiles above the world's absolute bottom:** previously, Waste with nothing built to catch it behaved like any other item — it just fell off the bottom of the world and was silently lost (see "Items can't stack, and can fall off the bottom"). Per direct request, Waste now gets its own fixed rest height instead: `Config.js`'s `WASTE_FLOOR_Y = WORLD_H - 2 * TILE_SIZE`. `Grid.js`'s `sweepVertical` checks for this — Waste ONLY, and only while actually falling into it (`stepY > 0`, so a Fan built in the two rows underneath actively pushing it back up sails right through unaffected) — as a fallback catch after the normal solid-tile check, so a real building placed anywhere above the line still routes/collects Waste exactly as before; this is purely the "nothing else caught it" case. The two rows below the line stay fully buildable (nothing about placement/anchoring changed), so a Fan built there aimed upward is the intended way to blow resting Waste back up into the water column where fish/Suckerfish can reach it. `Grid.js`'s `renderSeabedGrid` also draws a dashed horizontal break line at this height (`renderWasteFloorBreak`) purely for visual clarity, so the resting Waste doesn't read as floating in empty space with nothing supporting it.
- **`CLEANLINESS_PER_WASTE_EVENT` cut from 4 to 0.5:** each individual Waste item now costs (or restores) a much smaller slice of cleanliness — draining/recovering the full 0-100 range takes many more events than before, a deliberate slow-burn per direct request ("not 4%").
- **`WASTE_RADIUS` up 25%** (5 -> 6.25px) — easier to spot on the seabed, per direct request.
- **`FOOD_COLOR` — Food is now a distinct orange (`#ffb238`), not a green:** the old hardcoded food color (`#8bc34a`, a green) sat too close to `WASTE_COLOR`'s own olive-green to tell apart at a glance, especially once Waste got 25% bigger and more of it started piling up near Food on the seabed. `main.js`'s item-render loop now reads this new constant instead of a hardcoded literal (the shop's own food icon swatch got the same color fix in a later pass — see the Current Phase entry below).
- **Low-cleanliness warning notification:** the first time cleanliness crosses below `CLEANLINESS_WARNING_THRESHOLD` (90), `Entities.js`'s `adjustCleanliness` — the single choke point every cleanliness-decreasing call already routes through — detects the downward crossing (`before >= threshold && after < threshold`, so it fires exactly once, not on every subsequent Waste event while already dirty) and posts `CLEANLINESS_WARNING_MESSAGE` ("Looking a little dirty in there champ. The dirtier your tank is, the less often your fish produce money. If only there was a way to clean it......") to the notification ticker, gated by a new one-shot `state.level.tutorialFlags.cleanlinessWarningShown` flag. The message's claim that dirtiness slows coin production is foreshadowing text only, per the exact wording requested — no such gameplay effect actually exists yet, same "value is live-tracked, downstream effect still unbuilt" state cleanliness has been in since Phase 3 (see that section above).
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): a lone Guppy's poop settled to rest almost exactly at the calculated `WASTE_FLOOR_Y - radius` height (measured via the camera's known pan offset/zoom, matching the predicted world-Y within a rounding error), sitting directly on the rendered dashed break line; the same bottom row remained placeable (a Platform placed at the world's absolute last row succeeded normally); a Collector-and-Dartfin-swarm setup pushed cleanliness down to 89% and the warning notification appeared in the expandable log (it didn't always survive as the topmost pill, since a fish starving around the same moment could post a later notification over it — confirmed present in the full log either way).

**Star-tier rebalance: 1.5x -> 1.8x coin value, plus a new 10%-per-tier hunger reduction, waste explicitly left untouched** — per direct request. `FISH_STAR_TIER_VALUE_MULTIPLIER` raised from 1.5 to 1.8 in `Config.js`; a new `FISH_STAR_TIER_HUNGER_MULTIPLIER` (0.9) added alongside it and applied in `Entities.js`'s `updateFish` to `def.hungerRate` before the per-tick hunger accumulation, using the identical `^(starTier - 1)` compounding pattern the coin-value formula already used — see "Coin value scaling" above for the exact mechanism and worked numbers. The request explicitly called out that Waste output should stay pinned at "1 adult Tier-1's worth" regardless of tier — this required no code change at all, since neither the direct fish-poop timer (`WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS`) nor the Collector-byproduct path has ever read `starTier`; both already fire per-item/per-interval, not scaled by coin value or tier. Net effect: a higher star tier is strictly better economy (more money, less frequent feeding) at the same waste cost, by design.
- Verified live in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): a lone grown Tier-1 Guppy left to starve with nothing to eat took ~3986ms of real time at 10x sim speed to hit 0 entities; an otherwise-identical Tier-2 (freshly combined) Guppy took ~4455ms under the same conditions — a 1.118x ratio, matching the expected `1/0.9 = 1.111x` within the test's 100ms polling granularity. The new coin values were confirmed by direct calculation rather than a live coin-bank pixel-hunt (`Math.ceil(5 * 1.8^N)` for a Guppy: T2 = $9, T3 = $17, T4 = $30, computed in Node to rule out any floating-point surprise from 1.8 not being exactly representable) — the `Math.ceil` wrap already proven live in the prior changelog entry makes the rounding behavior itself independently verified; only the new multiplier's arithmetic needed checking here.

**A major shop/placement overhaul: unified single-selection, click-to-place fish (no Buy button), family-stacked building tiers, the full HUD duplicated into the shop panel, dynamic building costs, a bigger single-pickup coin radius, directional Collector/Auto-Feeder intake with visible input/output arrows, Fish Merging moved to a Tank Upgrade, and a one-shot title splash** — nine related requests landed together in one session; this entry is the consolidated changelog. Full per-mechanism detail is inline above wherever it naturally belongs (Platform Anchoring's new "Dynamic building cost" note, the rewritten Collector/Auto-Feeder sections, Economy Fish Combining's "Combining" paragraph); this is the rationale/overview plus what doesn't have an obvious home above.
- **Only one shop selection at a time:** previously a species' `.selected` highlight was tracked independently of `state.ui.selectedTool` (a separate manual class-toggle loop in `buildShopPanel`'s click handler), so a fish icon and a building tool could both read as "selected" simultaneously with no real conflict resolution. Fixed by routing fish selection through the exact same field buildings already used — clicking a species now sets `state.ui.selectedTool = 'fish:<id>'` (a new tool-string shape alongside the existing `'food'`/`'demolish'`/`'build:<id>'`) — so selecting anything anywhere in the shop implicitly deselects whatever else was previously armed, for free, just by being one shared piece of state. `UI.js`'s `updateToolbar` (kept its name despite outgrowing it) now loops over both `els.buildToolGrid.children` AND `els.shopGrid.children`, toggling `.selected` by comparing each button's `dataset.tool` against `state.ui.selectedTool`.
- **Fish are placed with a click, exactly like a building — no more Buy button:** `#shop-preview-buy` is gone from `index.html`; the preview window (`#shop-preview`) now shows only the name/live price, description, and a static "Click in the tank to place it" hint (`#shop-preview-hint`) for both species AND buildings — one visual language for both, since buying is now the same *action* for both. `Entities.js`'s new `trySpawnPurchasedFish(state, speciesId, x, y)` mirrors `trySpawnFood`'s reason-string contract (`'spawned' | 'no_money' | 'in_city'`) — it blocks placement inside the seabed city for the same reason Food does (a fish can never swim down there anyway, see its Y-clamp), deducts the live dynamic cost, and fires the one-time "first fish bought" notification (moved here from UI.js's old Buy-button handler, which is deleted). `main.js`'s click handler gained a `state.ui.selectedTool.startsWith('fish:')` branch calling it, and the render loop gained a translucent circular ghost preview for the fish tool (green/species-colored if the click would place it, red if not — unaffordable or inside the city), the same visual language `renderBuildGhost` already used for buildings. Fish placement is click-only, not drag-repeated like buildings — buying several of an increasingly-expensive economy fish in one careless drag felt like a real risk not worth the parity.
- **Building family stacking (the 3 Fan tiers share one shop slot):** per direct request, `Config.js`'s new `BUILDING_FAMILIES` (`{ fan: [fan_t2, fan_t3, fan_t4] }`) groups related buildings so higher tiers "stack" onto the same icon instead of each getting its own. `UI.js`'s `buildBuildPalette` renders one button per family (`buildFamilyButton`) instead of one per member, defaulting to the highest-currently-unlocked tier every time the palette rebuilds (init, the U cheat key, a Mound crack) — module-level `familySelectedTier[familyId]` tracks which tier is "loaded" into that slot. Clicking the slot when it ISN'T the active selection just selects whatever tier it's currently defaulted to (same as any other building icon); clicking it again WHILE it's already the active selection cycles to the next unlocked tier in the family instead, wrapping around. A small row of dots (`.tool-btn-family-dots`, one per unlocked tier, the current one filled in gold) renders on top of the icon so "there's more than one option here" reads at a glance without needing to click blind. Every building not listed in any family keeps its own ungrouped single slot, unchanged.
- **The full main HUD now duplicates into the shop panel** (`#shop-hud`: money, food count, cleanliness — money already had a shop-panel copy, `#shop-money`; food/cleanliness are new, `#shop-food`/`#shop-cleanliness`), per direct request that the shop will likely sit open most of the game so its own readouts shouldn't go dark while it is. `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` writes to both the main `#hud` trio and the `#shop-hud` trio every frame, and the flash-pickup/flash-spend redirect logic (previously money-only, redirecting between `#hud-money`/`#shop-money` based on which is actually visible) now covers food and cleanliness the same way — `flashFoodCapacity` also picked up a required `state` parameter so it can make the same visibility check (main.js's one call site updated to pass it).
- **Coin click radius +40%, single-pickup-per-click made explicit:** `COIN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER` raised from 1.1 to 1.4 (the hit-test radius is a coin's own drawn radius times this), per direct request that clicking shouldn't have to be pixel-perfect and a near-miss shouldn't get misread as a food-placement click instead. The "only one coin banked per click even if radii now overlap" requirement needed no code change — `Entities.js`'s `tryBankCoinAt` already banked the first match it found and returned immediately, before this change and after.
- **Directional Collector, shared with the Auto-Feeder's intake mechanism** — see the rewritten "The Collector" and "Auto-Feeder" sections above for the full mechanism (`isOnIntakeSide`, the two-arrow `renderDirectionIndicator`, `COLLECTOR_INTAKE_RADIUS`/`INTAKE_SIDE_MIN_DOT_FRACTION`). Summary: a Collector is now aimed at placement and only accepts items from its intake side, exactly like the Auto-Feeder already did — landing on top no longer auto-collects anything.
- **Fish Merging moved from an (undocumented, never actually enforced) Tier gate to a real one-time Tank Upgrade purchase** — `state.level.upgrades.fishMergingUnlocked` (`FISH_MERGING_UNLOCK_COST`, 10 Tank Points, flat, not a leveled ladder), checked first thing in `Entities.js`'s `isCombinableFish`. Both `isCombinableFish`/`canCombineFish` gained a `state` first-argument to check it; every call site (`main.js`'s mousedown/mouseup/hover-highlight) updated to pass it. `UI.js`'s Tank Upgrades panel gained a fourth card ("🧬 Fish Merging") reusing the same `createUpgradeCard` shape as the leveled trees, just with a binary Locked/Unlocked state instead of a level counter.
- **One-shot title splash:** `index.html`'s new `#splash-screen`/`#splash-title` plays a pure-CSS bounce-in-then-fade animation (`splash-bounce-in`, ~3.6s) the moment the page loads, per direct request. `pointer-events: none` throughout so it never blocks the very first real click; `main.js` listens for the animation's `animationend` event and removes the element entirely once it finishes, rather than leaving a hidden node parked on top of everything forever.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested): selecting a fish then a building deselects the fish's highlight and vice versa; clicking a fish icon shows no Buy button and sets `Selected tool: fish:guppy` in the debug overlay, and a canvas click at that point raised the entity count by exactly one; the Fan family slot defaulted to Turbo Fan (the highest unlocked tier) on first click and cycled to Rudimentary Fan (wrapping) on a second click, with 3 dots shown for 3 unlocked tiers; the Tank Upgrades panel listed a Fish Merging card, and a combine attempt before unlocking it left the entity count unchanged while the identical drag succeeded immediately after spending 10 Tank Points to unlock it; repeatedly cycling a tile through the debug T-cheat showed the Collector's shop price climbing $12 -> $13 -> $14 while Platform's stayed flat at $3 throughout; a Guppy dropped straight onto a Collector's top surface left the resulting coin sitting uncollected afterward (previously it would have been auto-banked and removed instantly); the splash screen appeared on load and removed itself from the DOM after its animation finished; food placement, the Demolish tool's dynamic refund, and the pause/restart flow all still passed their existing regression checks.

**Food doesn't count toward its cap while in the city, another fan range/cone increase, off-screen fan cones fixed, and the splash screen redesigned into two animation layers** — four requests landed together.
- **Food Capacity only counts Food actually in the open-water tank:** see the rewritten Food Capacity bullet above and `Entities.js`'s new `countTankFood(state)` — per direct request, a pellet that's fallen (or been dispensed by an Auto-Feeder) into the seabed city no longer occupies a capacity slot at all, so it becomes a strategic stash rather than something that has to be routed back up or wasted immediately. This is deliberately just a live position filter (`item.y < SEABED_FLOOR_Y`) reused by both the spawn-time gate (`trySpawnFood`) and the HUD's displayed count (`UI.js`'s `updateHUD`, both `#hud-food` and `#shop-food`) — no extra state to keep in sync, so a Fan blowing a piece of city Food back up across the line makes it count again the instant it crosses, with no special-case code needed. A direct consequence, explicitly called out as intended rather than a bug: the tank can briefly hold *more* than the nominal cap at once, since the cap only ever gates a fresh spawn — an existing pellet a Fan pushes back up from the city isn't re-checked against it. Verified live in a browser: filling the cap to 2/2 in open water, then letting one of those two pellets fall down past `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` with nothing built to catch it, dropped the HUD to 1/2 while the debug overlay's item count stayed at 2 the whole time — confirming the drop came from the city-exclusion filter, not either pellet actually being lost/removed.
- **Fan range +2 tiles (Rudimentary) / +3 tiles (Electric, Turbo), cone widened again:** `FAN_T2_MAX_RANGE` 224 -> 288px (7 -> 9 tiles), `FAN_T3_MAX_RANGE` 336 -> 432px (10.5 -> 13.5 tiles), `FAN_T4_MAX_RANGE` 480 -> 576px (15 -> 18 tiles) — the sixth such increase this session (see each constant's comment in `Config.js` for the full history). `FAN_CONE_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` widened again, 20 -> 25 (40° -> 50° total width), "slightly wider" per direct request, same as the pass before it. Force magnitudes unchanged.
- **Off-screen fan cones fixed:** previously, `Grid.js`'s `renderSeabedGrid` only ever called `renderDirectionIndicator` (which draws both the aim arrow and the translucent cone) for tiles inside its own screen-culled row/col loop — a Fan whose *own tile* had scrolled off screen never got its cone drawn at all, even though a long-range Fan's push area can extend well past its tile and still be genuinely on screen. Reported directly by the user after this session's range increases made the gap much more noticeable. Fixed with a new `renderFanIndicators(ctx, state, canvasWidth, canvasHeight)`, called once per frame independently of the tile-culling loop: it iterates every Fan in `state.level.buildingData` (not just the on-screen-tile ones), computes each one's screen-space center and its tier's `maxRange * zoom`, and skips only fans whose full bounding circle couldn't possibly touch the canvas rect — a fan whose circle overlaps the canvas always gets drawn, even with its own tile nowhere near the viewport, and the check can only ever over-draw (a fan whose circle overlaps but whose actual narrow cone wedge doesn't) rather than miss a genuinely visible cone, since the cone is always inscribed within that same circle. The per-tile loop no longer draws Fan cones/arrows at all (moved entirely to this new pass) but still handles the Collector/Auto-Feeder's own two-arrow indicator inline, since their indicator's reach (a sub-tile intake radius) is far too small for this off-screen concern to ever apply. Verified live in a browser: placed a Fan, read its exact world position off the debug overlay's "Cursor world" readout, panned the camera right by a precisely measured amount that left the fan's tile ~33 world px past the left edge of the visible canvas (definitely off-screen, its colored tile square nowhere in the screenshot), and confirmed a translucent cone wedge was still clearly visible poking in from the left edge of the frame.
- **Splash screen redesigned into two animation layers, per direct request** ("instead of the splash screen bouncing, have it fade and grow in like it does, but then have each letter bounce independently before having it fade off screen"): `#splash-title` itself now only handles a smooth grow-in/fade-out (`splash-grow-fade` — scale 0.3→1 and opacity 0→1 over the first 25% of a 4.5s run, holds at full size/opacity through 75%, then fades back to 0 over the last 25% — no more overshoot/multi-bounce at this level). `main.js` now builds one `<span class="splash-letter">` per character from the title's plain text (`splashLetters`, read once and cleared before rebuilding, so the DOM ends up holding only the spans) and gives each a staggered `animation-delay` (`SPLASH_GROW_IN_DURATION_S`, 1.125s — matched to the parent's own 25% keyframe so letters don't start hopping mid-grow-in — plus `i * SPLASH_LETTER_STAGGER_S`, 0.06s per letter) driving a separate per-letter `splash-letter-bounce` keyframe (a translateY overshoot-and-settle, independent of the parent's opacity/scale, composing naturally since nested CSS transforms/opacity stack visually). The removal listener now checks `e.target === splashTitle` before removing the whole `#splash-screen` element, since a child letter's own `animationend` also bubbles up to the same listener and would otherwise remove the splash while letters were still mid-bounce. Verified live in a browser: exactly 14 `.splash-letter` spans existed for "Derangiquarium" with strictly increasing `animation-delay` values starting at 1.125s and stepping by 0.06s, and the whole `#splash-screen` element was confirmed gone from the DOM after waiting past the full animation's runtime.
- Zero console errors across every scenario tested in this batch.

**Cleanliness gradient color, a UI sheen effect, a baby-fish ghost preview, background ambience (bubbles/seaweed/city texture), and synthesized SFX + background music** — five requests landed together, the biggest visual/audio pass so far. Full mechanism for each is inline above wherever it fits naturally (Module responsibilities' new Ambience.js/Sound.js entries); this is the consolidated changelog.
- **Cleanliness color gradient:** `#hud-cleanliness`/`#shop-cleanliness`'s text color now lerps live between `CLEANLINESS_COLOR_CLEAN` (`#4fc3f7`, bright blue) at 100% and `CLEANLINESS_COLOR_DIRTY` (`#6b8e4e`) at 0%, per direct request — `UI.js`'s `cleanlinessColor(cleanliness)` does a straight per-channel RGB lerp, recomputed every frame in `updateHUD` (never cached, since cleanliness can move every tick) and applied via `style.color` rather than a CSS class, since the color itself is continuous, not a fixed set of states. `CLEANLINESS_COLOR_DIRTY` is deliberately the exact same hex as `WASTE_COLOR` — a dirty tank reading the color of the Waste causing it is a small, free bit of thematic reinforcement, not a coincidence.
