This composes fine with the existing `flash-pickup`/`flash-spend` animations (which briefly override `color` via their own keyframes) since those classes get removed shortly after firing, letting the inline gradient color reassert itself.
- **UI sheen/shine sweep, on a random 5-30s cycle per element:** `style.css`'s new `.sheen-target`/`.sheen-play`/`@keyframes sheen-sweep` — a diagonal light band animated purely via `background-position` on a `::after` pseudo-element (`background-size: 250% 250%`), not by moving/translating the element itself. `border-radius: inherit` on the pseudo means it automatically matches whatever shape the host has (circle, pill, rounded-rect) with zero per-element configuration, and critically needs no `overflow: hidden` on the host at all — which matters because the species/building icon buttons have a price-tag badge that intentionally sits partly outside their own box (`.species-icon-price`/`.building-icon-price`, `bottom: -8px`), and `overflow: hidden` on the button would have clipped it. `UI.js`'s `scheduleSheen(el)`/`scheduleSheenAll()` give every `.sheen-target` element its own independent `setTimeout` chain with a fresh random 5-30s delay each cycle (not one shared ticker — per direct request that this "runs across it" per element, so they visibly glimmer out of sync with each other), guarded by a `dataset.sheenScheduled` flag against double-scheduling and self-terminating via `el.isConnected` once a shop/palette/panel rebuild disconnects the element. Applied broadly per the request's own list — `#hud-money`/`#hud-food`/`#hud-cleanliness`, `#shop-money`/`#shop-food`/`#shop-cleanliness`, `#shop-preview`, every species/building icon button, `#tool-food-btn`/`#tool-demolish-btn`, `#notification-latest` (the chat pill), all 4 `.pause-btn`s, every `.tank-upgrade-card`, and `#tank-points-display`.
- **Fish placement ghost is a real baby fish, not a circle:** `main.js`'s render loop now calls `drawFish(ctx, screen.x, screen.y, speciesId, 0, ...)` (stage 0 = hatchling, per `SPECIES[id].growthStages[0]`) at reduced opacity for the fish-tool ghost, with a small continuous idle tail-wag driven off `performance.now()` (no extra state needed — it's already discarded and redrawn every frame). Validity (afford + not inside the city) is still shown the same way a building ghost's red/green tint works, just as a soft colored ring drawn behind the fish instead of a solid circle standing in for it.
- **Background ambience — bubbles, seaweed, and city texture, per direct request:** new `Ambience.js` (see Module responsibilities above). `BUBBLE_COUNT` (45) bubbles recycle in a fixed pool — each rises from near `SEABED_FLOOR_Y` up past the top of the water column with a sine-wobble drift, then respawns lower down once it's off-screen above; rendered as a stroked ring plus a small highlight dot, at low opacity, called once per frame from `main.js`'s `render()` right after `renderMound` (in front of the city backdrop, behind fish/items which render after). `SEAWEED_COUNT` (16) fixed strands anchor along the seabed floor line and sway via a sine-bent quadratic curve; a real canvas `ctx.filter: blur()` was tried first for the "blurry, unobtrusive" look and **tanked frame rate hard** (measured 60fps → ~11fps with just 16 filtered strokes a frame — Chromium re-rasterizes each filtered draw call individually rather than batching a whole filtered region, so setting the filter once outside the loop didn't help), so it was replaced with a cheap fake: a wide, very transparent stroke underneath a narrower, slightly more opaque one on the same path, which reads as soft-edged at this opacity/scale for a fraction of the cost — restored to a solid 60fps. **City texture:** `Grid.js`'s `getCityTexturePattern` pre-renders a small 48×48 speckle-noise tile once (random light/dark dots, cached as a `CanvasPattern`) and overlays it on the seabed's base fill at low opacity, giving the flat brown ground some grain instead of reading as one solid color — deliberately tiled in plain screen space rather than world-locked to the camera (camera zoom is fixed for the whole session, only panning moves, and a perfectly world-locked version would need to track the pattern's own transform against `camera.x` every frame for what's ultimately subtle, low-opacity background noise nobody is meant to consciously track while panning).
- **Sound: synthesized SFX + a looping 8-bit-style background track — no audio file assets at all.** New `Sound.js` (see Module responsibilities above) builds every sound from Web Audio oscillators/noise buffers on the fly — genuinely how real NES/Game Boy-era audio worked in the first place (square/triangle/noise wave synthesis), not a placeholder standing in for "real" sampled audio. `ensureContext()` lazily creates the `AudioContext` plus two gain nodes (`musicGain` at 0.05, `sfxGain` at 0.22 — music stays soft/background per direct request, SFX a bit more present); `resumeAudio()`, called from `main.js`'s one-time `pointerdown`/`keydown` listeners (browsers refuse to produce sound before a real user gesture), resumes a suspended context and starts the music loop the first time it's ever called. `playTone(freq, duration, opts)` is the shared building block — one oscillator plus a linear attack/release gain envelope; `playNoise(duration, opts)` is the same idea with a fades-out white-noise buffer instead of a pitch, for the percussive SFX (demolish's crunch). Per-event SFX, hooked in directly at the exact point each event already fires (the same "call it inline" pattern Entities.js/Grid.js already use for floating texts/notifications): `playPurchase` (a cheerful two-note ascending blip — buying a fish, `Entities.js`'s `trySpawnPurchasedFish`), `playFoodPlace` (a tiny soft plink, `trySpawnFood`), `playEat` (deliberately very quiet per direct request — any fish eating Food or Waste, `updateFish`'s eat branch), `playFishDeath` (a short descending sad phrase — starvation, `updateFish`), `playCoinBank` (a bright Mario-coin-style double-blip — both click-banking, `tryBankCoinAt`, and a Collector auto-banking, `updateCoin`'s `'consumed'` branch), `playBuildPlace` (a low two-note thunk, `Grid.js`'s `placeTile`), `playDemolish` (a short noise crunch, `removeTile`), `playUpgrade` (a rising 4-note arpeggio — any of the 4 Tank Upgrade purchase buttons, `UI.js`), and `playTankPoint` (a small sparkle — a fish reaching adulthood, `awardTankPoint`). The background track is a short 28-note pentatonic melody (C major pentatonic — no interval in that scale can sound "wrong" regardless of note order, a reliable way to guarantee something harmonious/cute without hand-composing a real tune) over a sparse walking bass note every 2 bars, both triangle/square wave; `scheduleMusicLoop` converts the whole phrase into absolute `when` offsets up front and reschedules the next loop via a `setTimeout` timed to the phrase's own total duration, so any small timer drift only ever delays the next loop's first note by a few ms rather than causing an audible glitch mid-phrase.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario, including the full sequence of purchase/food/build/demolish/upgrade/eat/starve actions that exercise every SFX hook): `#shop-cleanliness`'s computed color read back as exactly `rgb(79, 195, 247)` at 100% cleanliness; 44 `.sheen-target` elements were found in the DOM with `dataset.sheenScheduled` correctly set; the fish-tool ghost visibly rendered as a small animated baby fish (with its validity ring) instead of a plain circle; bubbles and swaying seaweed were both clearly visible in a full-scene screenshot, and a cropped close-up of the seabed strip showed the speckled city texture clearly breaking up the flat brown fill; FPS held at a steady ~60-61 after the blur-filter performance fix (confirmed by an explicit before/after measurement — 10-11fps with the filter, 60-61fps with the two-stroke fake-blur replacement).

**Phase 4 built, a rotate hotkey, another fan range increase, and a large visual/audio-adjacent polish pass — everything landed together to "see how it all acts together" per direct request.** This is the consolidated changelog for a very large batch; per-mechanism detail for Phase 4 is in its own new section above ("Science Lab & Gene-Splicing (Phase 4)"), the Collector/Auto-Feeder rotate note is inline in Grid.js.
- **Rotate hotkey (`R`):** `Grid.js`'s `rotateBuilding(state, worldX, worldY)` adds 90° to whatever Collector/Auto-Feeder's `data.angle` is under the cursor — Fans are excluded (they already have their own two-click aiming flow). No new logic needed anywhere else: every consumer of `data.angle` (the intake-side scan, the output spawn point, the two-arrow render) already reads it fresh every tick/frame, so rotating "just works" the instant the one stored number changes.
- **Fan range +1 tile (Rudimentary) / +2 tiles (Electric, Turbo), 8th such increase this session:** `FAN_T2_MAX_RANGE` 288→320px (9→10 tiles), `FAN_T3_MAX_RANGE` 432→496px (13.5→15.5), `FAN_T4_MAX_RANGE` 576→640px (18→20). `FAN_CONE_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` 25→28 (50°→56° total).
- **Fish are 10% faster across the board:** a new `FISH_SPEED_MULTIPLIER` (1.1) applied once in `Entities.js`'s `effectiveSwimSpeed` — the single choke point every fish speed calc already reads through — rather than editing all 18 `SPECIES` rows' `swimSpeed` individually. Stacks with the Fish Movement Tank Upgrade (the whole sum gets the 10%, not just the base stat).
- **Seaweed: 3x as many, much bigger, size-scaled blur:** `SEAWEED_COUNT` 16→48. The old height range (55-130px) becomes the new MINIMUM; the new maximum is 4x that (520px) tall and 3x the old fixed stroke width (5px→15px) wide, each strand's height/width/blur now driven together by one random `sizeT` per strand. Blur (the layered-translucent-stroke fake-blur from the earlier perf fix) scales from 0.6x the old fixed amount at the smallest size up to 1.15x at the biggest — "the old amount was already toward the blurry end," per direct request, not the midpoint.
- **Food/Waste sway reworked from scheduled bursts to a continuous sine wobble, matching the bubbles:** the old scheme (`estimateFoodFallTime`/`createSwayEvents`/a pre-scheduled list of sway "events" timed against an *estimated* total fall duration) went stale the moment a Fan actually touched an item's real trajectory, since the schedule was computed once at spawn and never revisited — "the way I coded you into a corner on that one." Replaced with the same idea Ambience.js's bubbles already use: `currentSwayVx(item, amplitude, frequency)` recomputes a sine wobble fresh every tick from `item.fallTime` (still just accumulates, unchanged) and a per-item random `swayPhase` — self-correcting no matter what actually happens to the item, no schedule to go stale. Waste gets the same treatment "to a less degree" (`WASTE_SWAY_AMPLITUDE` 9 vs Food's 24).
- **Mound: city-style texture + more interesting cracks:** `Mound.js`'s `getMoundTexturePattern` is the same cached-speckle-tile-as-CanvasPattern technique `Grid.js`'s `getCityTexturePattern` already used, clipped to the dome's own silhouette path (`ctx.clip()`) so it never spills past the rounded shape. Cracks are now jagged 5-6-segment fractures (`CRACK_SHAPES`, generated once at module load — not per-render, which would make them visibly jitter) with a short forking branch partway down and a 1px offset light "highlight" stroke alongside the dark line for a carved/engraved look, instead of the original plain 3-point zigzag.
- **Items and buildings get a highlight/shadow pass, "so they pop more":** `main.js`'s item-render loop now adds a thin dark rim stroke plus a small glossy highlight circle to every coin/food/waste; `Grid.js`'s new `renderSquareBevel` adds a diagonal lighter-top-left/darker-bottom-right bevel to every square building tile (Collector, and the generic square every other non-Ramp building uses). Cheap — a couple of extra fills/strokes per item/tile, no gradients or filters, so it's safe at "every item and building, every frame."
- **Fish get the same highlight/shadow treatment, PLUS a tail-swish rework and a hunger hue-shift:** `FishRenderer.js`'s `drawFish` adds a darker underside ellipse and a small glossy highlight to the body. The tail (previously a straight-edged triangle whose only moving part was the apex sliding vertically, reading as an up-down flap) is now a curved `quadraticCurveTo` shape with a small counter-lean at the base attachment opposite the tip's own swing — reads as one continuous side-to-side swish instead of a rigid hinge-flap, per direct request. `drawFish` also takes a new optional `sickness` (0-1) parameter, blended into the fish's color toward a sick green (`mixColor`, a plain per-channel RGB lerp — a real `ctx.filter` hue-rotate was rejected outright without even benchmarking it, given the seaweed blur filter's own measured 60fps→11fps crash on far fewer draw calls than "every fish every frame" would be); `main.js` computes it from `fish.hunger` — 0.18 past `HUNGER_SEEK_THRESHOLD` (the same threshold that already shows the "!" indicator — "the second stage of hunger"), 0.35 past `HUNGER_CRITICAL_THRESHOLD`.
- **Tank background: a lightened vertical gradient that darkens with dirtiness:** `main.js`'s `waterBackgroundGradient` replaces the old flat `#1c5f8a` fill with a `createLinearGradient` (lighter blue at the top, deeper at the bottom, both stops brighter overall than the old single color) — and both stops scale down to 82% brightness at 0% cleanliness, un-scaled at 100%, recomputed fresh every frame since cleanliness moves continuously.
- **A font, a scrollbar, an icon, bigger shop icons in a 3-per-row grid, and a couple of bounce/shimmer triggers:** `index.html` now loads Google Fonts' "Baloo 2" (a rounded, playful display face) with the old system-font stack kept as a fallback; base font size bumped 13-14px→15px throughout. `#notification-log`'s scrollbar is restyled pink/rounded via `::-webkit-scrollbar`/`scrollbar-color` instead of the browser default. The Demolish tool's icon is a 🔨 emoji now, not a plain red circle. `.tool-btn` (Food/Demolish/every building slot) grew from 40px to 56px — the species icons' old size — and `.species-icon-btn` grew again to 64px; `#tool-bar` is a `display: grid` 3-columns-per-row layout now, with `#build-tool-grid` set to `display: contents` so its dynamically-built building buttons participate as direct grid items alongside Food/Demolish instead of wrapping as their own separate nested group. A new shared `.bounce-play` class (a springy scale keyframe, same forced-reflow retrigger trick every other one-shot animation class here already uses) plays on the pause menu opening and — paired with a forced `.sheen-play` retrigger for "bounce AND shimmer" — on the notification pill whenever a genuinely new message arrives (tracked by a separate `lastPillNotificationCount`, distinct from the existing log-rebuild counter, so it fires regardless of whether the log happens to be expanded).
- **Cleanliness color gradient:** `#hud-cleanliness`/`#shop-cleanliness`'s text color now lerps live between a bright blue (`CLEANLINESS_COLOR_CLEAN`, `#4fc3f7`) at 100% and an olive green (`CLEANLINESS_COLOR_DIRTY`, `#6b8e4e` — deliberately the same hex as `WASTE_COLOR`) at 0%, via `UI.js`'s `cleanlinessColor(cleanliness)`, a straight per-channel RGB lerp applied via `style.color` every frame.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario, steady 60-61fps with fish/items/seaweed/bubbles/mound-texture all active simultaneously): cracking the Mound to Tier 5 revealed a clickable Science Lab whose popup showed a live Science readout; a screenshot confirmed the lightened/gradient water background, the 3x-bigger swaying seaweed, the mound's speckled texture and forked cracks, the 3-per-row bigger shop icons (including the new hammer icon), and fish rendering with a visible highlight/shading and curved tail shape all together in one frame.

**Ramps removed entirely, bubbles grow in over 3s instead of popping in, food/waste sway made sporadic via an amplitude envelope, the 3 utility species got real distinct body shapes/colors, hybrids got a genuine parent-color blend and were pulled from the purchasable shop grid, tank upgrade buttons trimmed to just their cost, and a "poppy," non-flat gradient/glossy-highlight pass landed across the HUD/chat/shop/pause menu/Tank Upgrades panel plus custom scrollbars everywhere** — a large batch, all per direct request.
- **Ramp Left/Right removed from the game entirely** — the tile constants, their `BUILDING_TYPES` rows, the `applyRampNudge` nudge-while-falling function and its `sweepVertical` call site, the `RAMP_TILES` set, the `CHEAT_CYCLE`/T-key entries, and the triangle-wedge rendering branch in `Grid.js`'s `renderTileShape` are all gone — see the "Ramps ('funnels')" section's own history for what they used to do; that section itself, and every other place ramps were described (`Config.js`'s `TIER_UNLOCKS`/module comments, `Entities.js`'s module comment, `Grid.js`'s own header comment, and — the one piece of actual player-facing copy — the Tier 3 Mound-crack notification line in `Mound.js`, which used to read "A Ramp, a Collector, and an Auto-Feeder tumble out...") were all updated to stop mentioning them. `TIER_UNLOCKS[3].buildings` is now just `[TILE_COLLECTOR, TILE_AUTO_FEEDER]`. Verified live: the build palette's tool-bar renders exactly 6 slots (Food, Demolish, Platform, the Fan family, Collector, Auto-Feeder) with no Ramp icon anywhere, and cracking the Mound to Tier 4 via the debug `N` cheat produced zero console errors.
- **Bubbles grow in over `BUBBLE_GROW_DURATION_S` (3s) instead of appearing at full size** (`Ambience.js`): a per-bubble `age` field drives `renderBubbles`' drawn radius (`radius * min(1, age / 3) `, floored at 1px so the very first instant isn't literally invisible) — only for bubbles recycled after page load; the initial on-load population is seeded with `age` already at the full duration so the tank doesn't read as "everything pops in at once" the moment the page loads.
- **Food/waste sway reworked from a plain continuous sine wobble into sporadic swell-and-fade bursts** (`Entities.js`'s `currentSwayVx`, `Config.js`'s new `FOOD_SWAY_ENVELOPE_FREQUENCY`): the sway amplitude is now multiplied by a slow secondary envelope (`sin(fallTime * envelopeFrequency * 2π + swayPhase * 0.3)`, squared and floored at 0) shared by both food and waste — the squaring makes each burst fade in/out smoothly rather than snapping on/off, and reusing `swayPhase` (already existing, per-item random) as an offset keeps every item's burst timing out of sync with every other item's. Still fully self-correcting with no pre-scheduled state, same principle as the sway rework from the prior batch — this is a refinement of that, not a re-litigation of it. `FOOD_SWAY_FREQUENCY` itself was also lowered (0.35→0.22) per "sway less frequently."
- **Suckerfish, Electric Eel, and Science Octopus got real distinct body shapes and their requested colors** (`FishRenderer.js`, `Config.js`'s `FISH_COLORS`): Suckerfish is teal/green (`#2dd4a5`) and renders via a new `drawSuckerfishBody` — flatter/wider than the standard oval with 3 small bump circles along its top edge for a lumpy silhouette, and it keeps a (simplified) tail fin. Electric Eel is yellow (`#ffd93d`) and renders via a new `drawEelBody` — no fin at all, a thin 7-segment tapered path whose segments undulate sideways with easing toward the head, explicitly modeled on the same technique `Ambience.js`'s swaying seaweed already uses, just oriented horizontally instead of vertically. Science Octopus is purple (`#a663ff`, the old Blimpfish color, freed up by Blimpfish's own recolor below) and renders via a new `drawOctopusBody` — no fin, a round head positioned *above* the body's center (`y - size*0.22`) with 4 wavy `quadraticCurveTo` tentacles hanging below, phase-offset per tentacle for an independent wiggle. Blimpfish moved from purple to pinkish-orange (`#ff8a65`) so its color doesn't collide with the Octopus's new purple — its shop icon updates automatically since both read the same `FISH_COLORS` entry. Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish (the 3 base feeders) and every Gene-Splicing hybrid still render via the pre-existing `drawStandardBody` (extracted, unchanged in shape) — a hybrid's id never matches the 3 special-cased utility ids, so it falls through to this branch automatically with no extra logic needed. Since the shop preview canvas and the real game canvas both call the same `drawFish`, "make sure the preview matches" required no separate code path — verified live via screenshots of each species' shop preview showing the exact same shape/color the tank itself renders.
- **Hybrids get a genuine 50/50 color blend of their two parent species, and were pulled out of the purchasable shop grid:** `FishRenderer.js`'s new `blendHexColors(hexA, hexB)` (built on a new `hexToRgb` helper, also used to generalize the existing `mixColor` sickness-tint lerp) does a straight per-channel average; `drawFish`'s body-color resolution now checks `def.parents` first — a hybrid row's color is `blendHexColors(FISH_COLORS[parents[0]], FISH_COLORS[parents[1]])` instead of its own (mostly unused) `FISH_COLORS` entry, while its *shape* still comes from whichever `drawXxxBody` branch its `speciesId` falls into (a feeder-based hybrid's id is never `electric_eel`/`octopus`/`suckerfish`, so it always renders via `drawStandardBody` — "look like the guppy/dartfin/blimpfish fish that was used"). Separately, `UI.js`'s `buildShopPanel` now filters `getAvailableSpecies(state)` down to `!s.parents` before rendering the species grid — hybrids stay unlocked in `state.meta.speciesUnlocked` (the splice interaction still needs that to look them up) but no longer show up as a purchasable icon; they can only ever be obtained by actually splicing. Verified live with the sim frozen (`0x` time scale, so a spawned pair can't drift before the drag lands): the shop's species grid shows exactly 6 icons (`fish:guppy/dartfin/blimpfish/suckerfish/electric_eel/octopus`, confirmed via each button's own `data-tool`) regardless of how many hybrids are unlocked; splicing a grown Suckerfish onto a grown Adult Guppy dropped the entity count from 2 to 1 and rendered the resulting Scrub Guppy in the standard guppy body shape, colored a genuine olive/yellow-green blend of the two parents' colors (matching the expected per-channel average by direct calculation) — not the old scaffold color. (Testing this surfaced and fixed a small pre-existing bug: the debug `U` cheat's own comment claimed it unlocks "species, buildings, and tech," but the code only ever unlocked species/buildings — `state.meta.techUnlocked` was never touched, so Gene-Splicing's drag interaction could never actually be cheat-tested. Fixed in `main.js` to match its own stated intent, importing `GENE_SPLICING_TECH_ID` from Config.js.)
- **Tank Upgrade buy buttons show just the cost**, not `Buy Level X — cost`: `UI.js`'s `foodQuality`/`fishMovement`/`foodCapacity` buy buttons now read `` `${cost} 🏆` `` — verified live to show `2 🏆`, `1 🏆`, `1 🏆`. `fishMerging`'s own buy button (a one-time unlock, not a leveled purchase) was left as `` `Unlock — ${cost} 🏆` `` since "Buy Level X" was never its pattern in the first place.
- **A "poppy," non-flat visual pass across the HUD, chat/notification ticker, Shop panel, pause menu, and Tank Upgrades panel** (`style.css`): every one of these swapped its flat single-color background for a soft diagonal `linear-gradient`, gained a 2-3px white border, and picked up a layered `box-shadow` — an outer colored drop shadow plus an `inset` bright highlight along the top edge (and, on a few, a second faint inset shadow along the bottom) — reading as a raised, glossy card/pill instead of a flat tinted rectangle. Applied to: `#hud`, `#notification-latest`/`#notification-log` (the chat pill and its expanded log), `#shop-panel`/`#tank-panel` themselves, the `#shop-money`/`#shop-food`/`#shop-cleanliness`/`#tank-points-display` readout pills, `#shop-collapse-btn`/`#tank-collapse-btn`, `.tool-btn` (Food/Demolish), `#shop-preview`, `.tank-upgrade-card`/`.tank-upgrade-buy`, `#pause-menu`/`.pause-btn`/`#pause-resume-btn`. Two elements — `.tool-btn-build` and `.species-icon-btn` — read their actual fill from a per-instance `--tile-color`/`--species-color` CSS variable, so a background gradient would have fought that; those two instead got the same glossy read purely from layered `inset` box-shadows (a bright highlight near the top, a soft dark shadow near the bottom) stacked on top of whatever solid color the variable supplies, with no gradient involved. `.mound-throw-btn`/`#mound-menu`/`#lab-menu` (the Mound and Science Lab popups) were deliberately left untouched — not named in the request, so left alone rather than restyled speculatively.
- **Every scrollbar in the game now uses the same pink/rounded custom style, not the browser default:** `#notification-log` already had this from an earlier pass; `#shop-panel` and `#tank-panel` (both `overflow-y: auto`, previously plain default scrollbars) picked up the identical `::-webkit-scrollbar`/`scrollbar-width`/`scrollbar-color` rules.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested, including 4 consecutive Mound cracks via the debug `N` cheat): screenshots confirmed the gradient/glossy HUD pill, notification pill, shop panel, tank upgrade cards/buttons, and pause menu all rendering with visible highlight/shadow depth; the shop's species grid showed exactly the 6 base species in their correct new colors (teal Suckerfish, yellow Electric Eel, pinkish-orange Blimpfish, purple Octopus); each utility species' shop preview canvas showed its correct new shape (flat/lumpy finned Suckerfish, finless undulating flat Eel, finless head-on-top tentacled Octopus) matching the live tank rendering; a frozen-sim splice test confirmed hybrid color-blending and shape inheritance; the tile cheat-cycle and build palette both confirmed no Ramp tile exists anywhere in the game any more.

**Science becomes a real physical resource, Processor/Auto-Feeder each grow Electric and Advanced tiers with a real power-demand system and a HUD readout, utility fish now grow up through the same 3-stage ladder as the base feeders with growth-scaled behavior, Rudimentary Fan moved off the free tease onto a new paid "Tier 1.75," Gene-Splicing dropped to 25 Science, a dedicated Merge tool replaces automatic combine/splice dragging, the mound's crack rendering bug got fixed, and the background music was reworked for a less staccato/grating, more melodic feel with real volume sliders** — the single largest batch this project has taken in one turn; this entry is the consolidated changelog.

- **Science (physical resource):** Science Octopus no longer adds an instant number to a permanent meta counter — `state.meta.scienceTotal` is gone entirely, replaced by `state.level.science`, a level-scoped banked resource exactly like money, credited only when a physical Science Bubble item (`Entities.js`'s `createScience`/`updateScience` — mass 9 in `ITEM_MASS_BY_TYPE`, 3x a coin's; radius slightly smaller than a bronze coin) is actually collected — click-banked (`tryBankScienceAt`) or pulled into a Processor's intake, exactly like a coin. Rendered as a "magical bubble" in `main.js`'s item loop: a purple-to-blue `ctx.createRadialGradient` fill plus a bright rim stroke and glossy highlight, a real gradient (not a `ctx.filter`, which stays the one actually expensive canvas op per the seaweed-blur lesson). The Octopus's own production is a real long brew cycle now (`Config.js`'s octopus `growthStages`, 70s baby/mid, 50s adult — "a full minute at base," refined by the later per-stage numbers), not a speed-scaled instant tick: `Entities.js`'s `updateFish` posts a small "+0.1 🔬" floating text (`SCIENCE_PROGRESS_TICKS` = 10) every time a tenth of the cycle passes — pure progress feedback, nothing banked yet — then spawns the real, physical, fall-and-be-collected bubble(s) once the full cycle completes.
- **Processor (renamed from Collector) and Auto-Feeder each gained an Electric and an Advanced tier** (`Config.js`'s `PROCESSOR_STATS`/`AUTO_FEEDER_STATS`, new tile ids `TILE_COLLECTOR_ELECTRIC`/`_ADVANCED`, `TILE_AUTO_FEEDER_ELECTRIC`/`_ADVANCED`), all three tiers of each stacked into one shop slot exactly like the 3 Fan tiers already did (`BUILDING_FAMILIES` gained `collector`/`auto_feeder` entries — the existing family-stacking UI code in `UI.js` needed zero changes, it was already fully generic). A Processor's per-item hold duration (previously one flat `COLLECTOR_PROCESS_DURATION_MS` for everything) is now resolved once at the moment processing starts (`Grid.js`'s `beginCollectorProcessing`, stored on the item as `collectorTargetMs`) from the exact numbers given — base 6s coin/20s Science/0 power, Electric 4s/15s/10mw, Advanced 3s/9s/20mw — and it now ONLY accepts coins and Science (Food/Waste landing on it just rest there, per the new spec being specifically about those two resources). Waste production moved from "one per item consumed" to a continuously-running background clock per tile (`data.wasteAccumMs`, only advancing while genuinely holding something) — base every 10s of active processing, Electric 12s, Advanced 15s — a real mechanic change per direct request ("produce 1 waste every N seconds it's processing," not per-item. The Auto-Feeder's per-load duration and the count of loads needed per Food output are now both tier-driven too (base 20s/3 loads, Electric 12s/3, Advanced 10s/2) via a new `data.wasteCount` counter that resets once Food actually dispenses; `Grid.js`'s new `renderAutoFeederDots` draws one small dot per required load directly on the tile, lighting up as each completes — per direct request ("dots that light up... as they process the input amount required for the output"). Every tier of both buildings shares its base tile's shape (square + bevel, the Processor's own center circle) but gets a small corner badge (`renderTierBadge` — a yellow ⚡ for Electric, a purple ✨ for Advanced) plus its own `BUILDING_TYPES` fill color, per direct request that each tier "look unique... but still identifiable as the same type of building." Electric Processor/Auto-Feeder are granted in the same `TIER_UNLOCKS[4]` crack as Electric Eel itself ("unlocked automatically when the electric eel is unlocked"); Advanced Processor/Auto-Feeder are bought once each in the Science Lab for 25 Science (`ADVANCED_PROCESSOR_COST`/`ADVANCED_AUTO_FEEDER_COST`, two new buttons in `UI.js`'s Lab popup, same one-time-unlock-then-placeable-for-money pattern Gene-Splicing itself already used), not tier-gated at all.
- **A real power-demand system, and an electricity HUD readout with a rolling graph popup:** `Grid.js`'s new `computeCurrentPowerDemand` sums every currently-drawing building live each call — a Fan draws its cost unconditionally while placed (existing precedent), a Processor/Auto-Feeder only while actively holding/processing something. `main.js`'s `update()` samples this plus `state.level.powerSupply` (still just an ever-growing capacity number, fed by Generator fish, same mechanic as before, now just also displayed as a "max") into `state.level.powerHistory` once per real sim-second, capped at 60 entries (`POWER_HISTORY_MAX`, a rolling one-minute window). `#hud-power`/`#shop-power` (new, hidden until Electric Eel is unlocked) show `⚡ current/max mw`, text only ever updating once a second since it just reads the history array's last entry — "updates every second," satisfied for free. Clicking `#hud-power` (only ever visible/clickable while the shop is closed, same as every other `#hud` child) toggles a small popup graph underneath it (`#hud-power-graph`, same dropdown-under-pill pattern `#notification-log` already used), a canvas line/area chart of demand (filled, orange) vs. supply (a grey line) over the last 60 samples, styled with the game's own poppy palette rather than a generic chart look.
- **Utility fish grow up now, through the same 3-stage feed-driven ladder the base feeders use** (`Config.js`'s suckerfish/electric_eel/octopus `growthStages`, `feedsRequired` 0/3/6 matching Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish's own pattern) — previously each spawned pre-grown at a single fixed "adult" stage. `FishRenderer.js`'s `drawFish` now only switches to a utility species' unique body shape (`drawEelBody`/`drawOctopusBody`/`drawSuckerfishBody`) at the fully-grown Adult stage; baby and mid both render via the same `drawStandardBody` every base feeder uses, just tinted the utility species' own `FISH_COLORS` entry — "make their baby and mid look like the [standard] fish, but with the utility fish colors." Only an Adult utility fish can be dragged as a Gene-Splicing source now (`Entities.js`'s `isSpliceSource` gained a stage check) — "only the adults can be used for hybridization."
- **Growth-stage-scaled behavior for all 3 utility species, per direct request, replacing the old flat/speed-scaled mechanics:** Suckerfish's `dropInterval` field is repurposed as its EAT COOLDOWN (a new `fish.eatCooldownRemainingMs`, ticked down every frame, gating the actual eat action in `updateFish`'s seek/eat branch — the fish still swims right up to a Waste item on cooldown so it doesn't look frozen, it just can't eat until the cooldown clears) — 35s baby/mid, 25s adult; `hungerRate` itself stays flat across stages, so starvation timing is unaffected, only how often it can actually eat. Electric Eel's power generation is fully distance-based now (a new `fish.distanceAccumPx`, accumulated every tick unconditionally from the fish's own real current speed, producing 1 MW every `stageDef.pixelsPerMW` pixels traveled — 10px baby/mid, 5px adult) — replaces the old timer-plus-speed-multiplier scheme entirely, and is a more literal reading of "speed-based production" than the indirect ratio ever was. Science Octopus's brew-cycle timing is covered above. (Volt-Topus, which carries both GENERATOR and RESEARCHER tags at once, still only ever produces Science, never Power — the branch order in `updateFish` deliberately checks `isPureResearcher` before `isPureGenerator` to preserve that original "one resource per fish" simplification, which a first pass at this reorder briefly broke and had to be caught before shipping.)
- **A dedicated Merge tool (🧤), replacing "any mousedown on an eligible fish always tries to combine/splice":** per direct request, `main.js`'s combine/splice mousedown handler now bails out immediately unless `state.ui.selectedTool === 'merge'` — a new tool string set by a new `#tool-merge-btn` in the shop's tool-bar (`UI.js`), right alongside Food/Demolish. The canvas cursor now changes to match the active tool too — a hammer for Demolish, a glove for Merge (`main.js`'s `emojiCursorCss`, a small inline-SVG data-URI cursor built from the emoji itself, no image asset) — per direct request ("change the cursor to a hammer" / "change the cursor to match").
- **Rudimentary Fan moved off the free Tier 1.5 tease onto its own paid "Tier 1.75"** — per direct request ("the base fan doesn't unlock anymore... add in another Tier that costs 500 gold... and just unlocks the rudimentary fan"). The tease (`MOUND_TEASE_COST`, $150) is a pure no-op joke again; a new second step, gated by a new `state.level.fanUnlockPurchased` flag, costs `FAN_UNLOCK_COST` ($500) and grants only the Fan, still without advancing `state.level.tier` — see `Mound.js`'s 3-branch `getMoundNextCost`/`crackMound`. The debug `N` cheat now sets both `moundTeased` and `fanUnlockPurchased` before cracking, so it still always advances a real tier rather than spending the press on either paid step.
- **Gene-Splicing cost cut from 100 to 25** (`GENE_SPLICING_COST`), and checked against the new `state.level.science` instead of the retired `state.meta.scienceTotal`.
- **A real bug fix: the Mound's cracks reading as "shifted"** — `Mound.js`'s crack-drawing loop only ever wrapped the dome's own texture fill in `ctx.clip()` to its silhouette, not the crack/branch strokes drawn right after — a branch's jittered endpoint could poke straight through the dome's tapering top edge into the water above it (confirmed via a zoomed screenshot: a stray line clearly escaping the dome's outline). Fixed by moving `ctx.restore()` to after the whole crack loop instead of right after the texture fill, so the clip covers everything. Also tightened the per-point jitter (±8px → ±5px, since several same-sign random points in a row could bow a crack visibly toward one side even though each crack's own base position is centered/evenly spread), fixed the branch's `sin()` angle always coming out positive (every branch always forked the same direction, right, regardless of which crack it was — now a real coin flip), and re-centered the crack x-distribution to spread evenly across a 35%-65% band well clear of the dome's tapering edges (was 50%-90%, which crept close enough to the right edge at higher tiers to read as "everything bunched toward one side"). Verified via a before/after screenshot at Tier 4 (3 cracks) — the fixed version shows all 3 cracks evenly spread and fully contained within the dome's silhouette, no stray lines escaping the edge.
- **Background music reworked for "less staccato and grating, more melodic/upbeat/adventure," plus real volume sliders** (`Sound.js`): the lead voice switched from a buzzy `'triangle'` wave with a short 0.05s release and 82%-of-note-length coverage (the audible gap between almost every note IS what staccato sounds like) to a warmer `'sine'` wave with a 0.22s release and 97% coverage, so consecutive notes now overlap (legato) instead of clicking on/off. The melody itself was rewritten with a rising 3-note opening flourish, longer sustained notes at each phrase's peak, and a falling resolve at the end, still built entirely from the same C major pentatonic scale (no bad-sounding interval possible regardless of order). Tempo dropped from 132 to 112 BPM for an unhurried "adventure" feel, and the bass switched from a thin `'square'` blip to a `'triangle'` note paired with a soft sustained octave-up pad for a fuller low end. `setMusicVolume`/`setSfxVolume`/`getMusicVolume`/`getSfxVolume` are new exports (0-1, applied live to the existing `musicGain`/`sfxGain` nodes, stored separately so a slider drag before the AudioContext even exists yet still takes effect once it's created) — wired to two new `<input type="range">` sliders in the pause menu's Settings panel, replacing the old "more settings coming soon" placeholder.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested): the build palette showed exactly 1 slot (Platform) before any Fan-related crack, and exactly 4 (Platform, Fan family, Processor family, Auto-Feeder family) after unlocking everything; clicking each family's slot showed the correct tier-specific stats block (confirmed Advanced Processor's "Coin: 3s / Science: 9s / Waste: every 15s" and Advanced Auto-Feeder's "Waste: 10s per load / Needs: 2 loads" matching the exact requested numbers); dragging two Adult Guppies onto each other with the Merge tool NOT selected produced no combine, and the identical drag with it selected (and Fish Merging already unlocked) correctly combined them; the Demolish/Merge tool buttons each produced a distinct custom cursor, Food correctly fell back to the default crosshair; freshly-spawned baby Suckerfish/Eel/Octopus all rendered as plain colored ovals (the shared standard body), not their unique adult shapes; a grown Electric Eel accumulated real power (0 → 369mw supply over a 60-simulated-second run at 10x speed) confirming distance-based generation fires even before the fish has anywhere to swim to; the electricity HUD appeared, updated, and its click-to-open graph popup rendered a live rising supply line; a grown Science Octopus posted visible "+0.1 🔬" progress bubbles mid-cycle and produced exactly 1 physical Science item (alongside 2 poop-timer Waste items) after its 50-second adult brew cycle completed, with its adult "head on top" shape clearly rendering throughout.

**The Mound's tier sequence lost its one wasted step, the Turbo Fan got rebalanced into a real hover-suspension tool with matching power costs, Gene-Splicing moved off the Mound onto its own Tank Upgrade, UI chrome gained sound effects, the background music got reworked a second time for real bounce/fullness, and the Food tool got its own cursor** — a second large batch, all per direct request; this entry is the consolidated changelog.

- **The Mound's tier sequence dropped its one wasted step:** the old real Tier 1->2 crack ($1000) granted nothing but the tier-flip itself — neither Economy Fish Combining's mechanics nor Fish Merging has ever actually been gated by tier in code, so that crack existed purely to exist. Removed entirely; what used to be the Tier 2->3 crack's grant (Processor + Suckerfish) now happens at the real Tier 1->2 crack instead, and the Auto-Feeder split out into its own new paid "Tier 2.5" sub-step (`AUTO_FEEDER_UNLOCK_COST`, $5000 — same pattern as the existing Tier 1.75 Fan step, a new `state.level.autoFeederUnlockPurchased` flag checked/set in `Mound.js`'s 5-branch `getMoundNextCost`/`crackMound`). `MOUND_MAX_TIER` dropped from 5 to 4 and `TIER_UNLOCKS`/`MOUND_CRACK_COST` renumbered accordingly (old Tier 4's Electric content is new Tier 3; old Tier 5's shatter/Octopus/hybrids is new Tier 4) — final sequence: 1 (start) -> 1.5 tease ($150, nothing) -> 1.75 ($500, Rudimentary Fan) -> real 1->2 crack ($1000, Processor + Suckerfish) -> 2.5 ($5000, Auto-Feeder) -> real 2->3 crack ($25000, Electric Eel/Fan/Processor/Auto-Feeder) -> real 3->4 crack ($50000, Mound shatters into the Science Lab, Science Octopus + every hybrid + Turbo Fan). The debug `N` cheat now also forces `autoFeederUnlockPurchased` alongside the two existing flags, so it still always advances a real tier.
- **Gene-Splicing moved from a Science Lab purchase to a Tank Upgrade**, per direct request ("unlocked through the tank upgrades for 5 tank points, instead of unlocked through mound tiers") — no longer requires reaching the Mound's max tier at all. `UI.js`'s Lab popup lost its `#lab-research-btn` entirely (Advanced Processor/Auto-Feeder purchases stay there, still Science-gated and still requiring the shattered Mound); a new "🧪 Gene-Splicing" card in the Tank Upgrades panel (mirroring Fish Merging's existing one-time-unlock pattern exactly) spends `GENE_SPLICING_TANK_POINT_COST` (5 Tank Points) and pushes the same `GENE_SPLICING_TECH_ID` into `state.meta.techUnlocked` that `Entities.js`'s `isSpliceSource` already reads — no change needed to the splice-gating logic itself, only where the unlock is purchased.
- **The Turbo (Advanced) Fan rebalanced into a real hover-suspension tool, per direct request** ("same range, but less powerful... coins should be closer to the 40% mark... waste and food float at the top"): reverse-engineered from the physics — at equilibrium a suspended item's weight exactly balances the Fan's linearly-decaying force, so `hoverFraction = 1 - (mass * GRAVITY) / maxForce`. Solving for a coin (mass 3) to hover at 40% of range gives `maxForce = 440` — and that same 440 also lands Waste (mass 1) at almost exactly 80% (a strong signal 440 was the intended number, not a coincidence); Food (mass 0.3, much lighter) ends up near 94%, closest to the water's surface, consistent with "food floats near the top" even without landing on the identical percentage a single force value can't give two different masses at once. `FAN_T4_MAX_FORCE` cut from 1100 to 440 (range unchanged at 640px); `FAN_T3_MAX_FORCE` (Electric/mid Fan) cut from 520 to 350 so it stays clearly weaker than the now-gentler Turbo Fan instead of accidentally becoming the strongest tier. Power costs set to the exact requested flat numbers: Rudimentary 0mw (unchanged), Electric 1mw (was 5), Turbo 3mw (was 14) — all still drawn unconditionally while placed, same not-yet-power-gated precedent as before. Verified live: dropping Food into a freshly-placed Turbo Fan's cone (aimed straight up via the debug `T` cheat) settled it into a stable hover near the top of the visible cone wedge, and the electricity HUD read exactly `3/0 mw` with only that one Fan placed.
- **Sound effects added for UI chrome** (`Sound.js`'s new `playPanelOpen`/`playPanelClose`, a soft rising/falling two-note sine blip): wired into the Shop panel toggle, Tank Upgrades panel toggle, the pause menu's open/close (both the Escape-key toggle and the Resume-button/backdrop-click path), the pause menu's Settings/Back sub-tab switches, and the electricity HUD's click-to-open graph popup — open plays the rising version, close/back plays the falling one, on every path uniformly.
- **Background music reworked a second time** — the previous pass fixed "staccato/grating" (legato articulation, a warm sine lead) but ended up too slow/sparse to read as "cute and upbeat... a hero on a new adventure" (Stardew Valley's Summer theme, Yoshi's Island, and SMW's Special World music were the named references). This pass keeps the legato fix but: tempo back up to 128 BPM; the melody rewritten with dense paired-eighth "hop" phrasing across three answering call-and-response phrases (each a step higher than the last) before a confident low resolve, still pure C major pentatonic; the bass now genuinely walks (8 moving root-sixth-fifth hits across the loop instead of 4 static ones) for real fullness; and a new quiet rhythmic "bounce" pulse (a tiny soft square-wave tick on the off-beat of every bass hit) layered underneath for the skipping, forward-hopping feel those reference tracks share.
- **The Food tool gets its own cursor** — a plain colored dot matching `FOOD_COLOR` (the same color the shop's Food icon and every dropped pellet already use), via a new `circleCursorCss` helper in `main.js` alongside the existing `emojiCursorCss` one Demolish/Merge already used, added to `CURSOR_BY_TOOL`.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested): stepping through 4 debug `N` presses (with enough granted money) landed exactly at the new max Tier 4, showing all 6 base species unlocked and all 4 build-family slots present; the Tank Upgrades panel listed a new "🧪 Gene-Splicing" card alongside the existing four, and `#lab-research-btn` was confirmed fully absent from the DOM while the Lab's two Advanced-building buttons remained; a Turbo Fan's cone visibly suspended a dropped Food pellet near the top of its range rather than launching it away or failing to catch it, with the HUD correctly reading `3/0 mw`; every newly-wired sound call site (pause open/close, Settings/Back tab switches, Resume, the electricity HUD toggle, both volume sliders) was exercised with zero exceptions.

**The Mound shrank into a short on-ramp and the Science Lab became the game's real end-goal tech tree** — per direct request ("fundamentally shift from the mound being the end all game goal to science being the end all game goal"), landing right after the batch above in the same session.

- **Mound sequence reshuffled again, one tier shorter:** `MOUND_MAX_TIER` dropped from 4 to 3. Final sequence: 1 (start) -> 1.5 tease ($150, nothing) -> 1.75 ($500, Rudimentary Fan) -> real 1->2 crack ($1000, Processor + **Science Octopus** — moved here from the old Tier 5) -> 2.5 (`AUTO_FEEDER_UNLOCK_COST` cut to $2500, Auto-Feeder only) -> real 2->3 crack ($5000, cut from $25000 — the Mound shatters completely into the Science Lab, granting nothing else directly). Suckerfish and Electric Eel — previously Mound-tier unlocks — are gone from `TIER_UNLOCKS` entirely; they, and every Electric/Advanced building, moved into the Lab below. `Mound.js`'s `TIER_CRACK_MESSAGES` rewritten to match (Tier 2's line now mentions Octopus, not Suckerfish; the old Tier 4 shatter line is now Tier 3's).
- **The Science Lab rebuilt from a flat 2-button list into a real branching dependency tree** (`Config.js`'s new `SCIENCE_LAB_UPGRADES` table, 8 nodes: `eel`, `suckerfish`, `electric_fan`, `electric_collector`, `electric_auto_feeder`, `advanced_fan`, `advanced_collector`, `advanced_auto_feeder`) — per direct request ("should look like a web of unlocks branching from the unlocks that are barring them before"). Every node has a `requires` array of prerequisite node ids and a `grants: { species, buildings }` shape identical to a `TIER_UNLOCKS` entry; a new `state.meta.labUpgradesPurchased` array (permanent, like `speciesUnlocked`/`buildingsUnlocked`) is the single source of truth for "is this node bought," checked uniformly regardless of whether a node grants a species or a building. `eel` and `suckerfish` are both root nodes (no prerequisites); `electric_fan`/`electric_collector` each require only `eel`; **`electric_auto_feeder` requires BOTH `eel` and `suckerfish`** — per direct request ("make sure the two branch together so it's obviously that both the eel and suckerfish are requirements"), rendered as two separate connector lines converging on the same node rather than a single merged edge. Each Electric building's Advanced counterpart requires that exact Electric building (`advanced_fan` <- `electric_fan`, etc.) — "the electric buildings bar the advanced versions." Every node costs BOTH Science Bubbles and gold at once (a first for this game's economy), at the exact requested numbers: Eel 10🔬/$1000, Suckerfish 15🔬/$1000, Electric Fan 20🔬/$2500, Electric Auto-Feeder 30🔬/$5000, Electric Processor 50🔬/$5000, Advanced Fan 100🔬/$15000, Advanced Auto-Feeder 150🔬/$15000, Advanced Processor 250🔬/$25000.
- **The Lab popup itself moved from a small Mound-anchored flyout to a centered modal** (`#lab-overlay`/`#lab-modal`, the same dimmed-backdrop pattern `#pause-overlay` already uses) — a real node-link tree needs consistent screen space regardless of camera position, which the old anchor-tracking flyout (sized for 1-2 buttons) never had room for. `UI.js`'s `buildLabTree` computes each node's dependency DEPTH (one more than the deepest of its own prerequisites, memoized since several nodes share a prerequisite) and lays out one CSS column per depth; `drawLabTreeConnectors` draws a bezier curve per prerequisite edge on a `<canvas>` layered under the node buttons (`pointer-events: none`, so it never intercepts clicks), reading each button's live `getBoundingClientRect()` — solid green for an already-purchased prerequisite's edge, faint dashed grey for a still-locked one. `buyLabUpgrade` is one generic data-driven purchase function (checks `labUpgradesPurchased` for the id itself and every entry in `requires`, checks both costs, then applies `grants` the same way `Mound.js`'s `crackMound` already does) replacing the two bespoke button handlers the old flat list needed.
- **Gene-Splicing's Tank Upgrade purchase now also unlocks every hybrid species row** into `state.meta.speciesUnlocked` (looped from `SPECIES_LIST` by `.parents` presence) — not strictly required for the splice interaction itself (`isSpliceSource`/`canSpliceFish` only ever check the utility PARENT's own unlock status, never the resulting hybrid's), but keeps `state.meta` consistent with what a Mound crack used to grant directly, now that hybrids have no Mound-tier or Lab-node of their own.
- **A new debug convenience:** the `M` cheat key now also grants 500 Science (`CHEAT_SCIENCE_GRANT_AMOUNT`) alongside its existing money/Tank Points grant, so testing the Lab's tech tree doesn't require grinding a real Octopus brew-and-collect cycle first.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): stepping through the Mound via the debug `N` cheat landed at the new max Tier 3 with exactly `guppy/dartfin/blimpfish/octopus` unlocked (no Suckerfish/Eel, confirming they no longer come from Mound tiers at all); clicking the Science Lab's seabed footprint opened the new tree modal showing all 8 nodes correctly laid out in 3 columns (Eel/Suckerfish at depth 0, the three Electric buildings at depth 1, the three Advanced buildings at depth 2) with Electric Auto-Feeder's two dashed incoming edges from both roots clearly visible; buying Eel and Suckerfish flipped both to a green "Unlocked ✓" state with solid green edges, simultaneously un-locking all three Electric-tier nodes (their cost/afford text replacing "Locked") while the Advanced tier stayed locked; the shop's species grid picked up Suckerfish and Electric Eel immediately after purchase with no panel rebuild needed beyond the existing `refreshShopPanel` call.

**Food/Demolish/Merge moved to a fixed always-on-screen bottom tool-bar with number-key hotkeys, a new scrollable "visual buffer" strip below the seabed houses it, insufficient-funds attempts now shake the money readout, an unread notification bounces itself every few seconds as a reminder, and coins got easier to click again** — five more direct requests landed together.

- **Food/Demolish/Merge relocated out of the shop into a new fixed `#bottom-tool-bar`**, centered at the bottom of the viewport and visible regardless of whether the shop/Tank Upgrades/pause menu is open — per direct request ("put them in the bottom middle of the screen so they are always there"). `index.html`'s `#tool-bar` (inside the shop) now holds only `#build-tool-grid` (the building palette, unaffected — buildings stay in the shop); the three tool buttons live in the new fixed bar instead, still driven by the exact same `state.ui.selectedTool` values and click handlers as before, just wired through a new small exported `UI.js` helper (`selectTool(state, tool)`) shared between the buttons' own click handlers and the new hotkeys. New number-key hotkeys — **1** (Food), **2** (Demolish), **3** (Merge) — call the same `selectTool` from `main.js`'s keydown handler. The old always-visible `#tool-tooltip` line (dynamically showing "Food $3" / the Demolish/Merge description) is gone entirely — per direct request ("remove any text from the shop for the tools, and move those to a tool hovertip"), each button's description now lives on its own native `title` attribute (a real hover tooltip), with the hotkey number appended to each (e.g. "Food — $3 (1)").
- **A new scrollable "visual buffer" strip below the real seabed**, per direct request, purely so the fixed bottom tool-bar always has real background behind it even when the camera's scrolled all the way down, instead of running out into nothing. `Config.js`'s new `CAMERA_BOTTOM_BUFFER_PX` (220) extends `Engine.js`'s `updateCamera` vertical clamp past the world's real bottom edge (`WORLD_H`) — deliberately NOT extra tile rows (`state.level.grid` stays exactly `WORLD_TILES_H` rows), so nothing can ever be built there; `canPlaceTile` already rejects any row past the real grid with no code change needed. `Grid.js`'s `renderSeabedGrid` already filled the seabed color all the way to the bottom of the canvas regardless of true world bounds, so the buffer strip "looks the same as the rest of the city background" for free; the only new render step is `renderCameraBottomBuffer`, a black `ctx.createLinearGradient` fading in exactly across the buffer's own world-Y span (`WORLD_H` to `WORLD_H + CAMERA_BOTTOM_BUFFER_PX`) — per direct request, deliberately NOT bleeding up into the real seabed above it. This required a small restructure of `renderSeabedGrid`: the base fill/texture/gradient now run unconditionally (previously an early `return` on "every tile row is off-screen" — true whenever the camera's scrolled past the world's bottom — skipped them entirely, along with `renderFanIndicators`, which also now runs unconditionally for the same reason a Fan's cone can extend past its own culled tile).
- **A failed shop purchase now shakes the money readout red** (`UI.js`'s new `flashMoneyInsufficient`, reusing the existing `flash-spend` red-shake animation) instead of silently doing nothing — per direct request. Wired into every purchase-attempt call site in `main.js`'s click handler and `updateBuildDrag`: Food (`trySpawnFood` returning `'no_money'`), a fish (`trySpawnPurchasedFish` returning `'no_money'`), and a building (checked via `canPlaceTile`'s `'cannot afford'` reason, both for a Fan's two-click aim-then-place flow and ordinary drag-placement) — a Mound/Lab/Tank-Upgrade purchase didn't need this, since those buttons are already `disabled` when unaffordable and so never generate a click to react to in the first place. Verified live: draining starting money down to $2 (below Food's $3 cost) and clicking again left the balance completely unchanged while the money HUD still visibly shook — confirmed distinct from the pre-existing "any money change shakes/flashes" behavior, which never fires here since nothing was actually spent.
- **An unread chat notification now bounces itself as a reminder**, per direct request — `UI.js`'s new `scheduleNotificationReminder`, a self-rescheduling `setTimeout` chain (same shape as the existing `scheduleSheen`) that re-triggers the pill's `.bounce-play` animation every random 3-6 seconds for as long as a new message has arrived but the log has never been expanded to read it. A new `notificationUnread` flag is set `true` the moment a genuinely new notification arrives (alongside the existing arrival bounce+shimmer) and set `false` the instant the player actually expands the log (not merely opens-then-immediately-closes-without-reading, and not re-armed by closing it again) — a later message re-arms it even if an earlier one was already read.
- **Coin click radius bumped again, 40% → 60% bigger than the coin itself** (`COIN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER`, `Config.js`) — per direct request, "so coins are even easier to click." Purely a hit-test radius change; `tryBankCoinAt` still only ever banks the first match per click, so overlapping bigger radii still can't double-bank one click.

**A third starting Food pellet, the waste floor's dashed line replaced with a real rocky shelf, and a full-roster 20%-slower-hunger/10%-less-frequent-production pass** — three more direct requests landed together.

- **`FOOD_MAX_ON_SCREEN_BASE` raised from 2 to 3** — per direct request ("start with 3 food pellets that can be placed instead of 2"). Verified live: 5 rapid Food-tool clicks placed exactly 3 pellets (HUD reading `🍖 3/3`), the same tight-cap-then-refuse behavior as before just one pellet more generous.
- **The dashed yellow line at `WASTE_FLOOR_Y` replaced with a real rocky shelf visual** (`Grid.js`'s `renderWasteFloorBreak`) — per direct request ("so it makes more sense why waste accumulate there"). A fixed jagged top edge (`ROCK_SHELF_POINTS`, world-space `(x, yOffset)` pairs spanning the full world width) is generated once at module load — not reseeded per frame, so it never jitters while panning — then rendered each frame as a shaded polygon: a real `ctx.createLinearGradient` fill (tan lip fading to a dark base) plus two cheap offset strokes standing in for a sunlit highlight ridge and an underside shadow, same "no `ctx.filter`" discipline as every other decorative render in this file. Reads as a physical rock ledge Waste is piling up against instead of an abstract dashed marker.
- **Every fish species gets 20% slower hunger accumulation, and every FEEDER-producing species' coin-drop interval (plus the flat fish-poop timer) got stretched 10% longer** — per direct request ("all the fish get hungier 20% slower... produce money and waste 10% less frequently"), applied as a straight `hungerRate *= 0.8` / `dropInterval /= 0.9` pass across all 18 `SPECIES` rows (base feeders, the 3 pure utility species, and all 9 Gene-Splicing hybrids) plus `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` (25000 -> 27778ms). Deliberately NOT touching: the Suckerfish's `dropInterval` (an eat cooldown, not production), the Science Octopus/Scrub-Topus/Volt-Topus's `dropInterval` (a science brew cycle) or the Electric Eel/Scrub-Eel's `pixelsPerMW` (distance-based power, no interval to stretch) — the request named "money and waste" specifically, and hunger-rate was applied uniformly since it named "all the fish."
- **A pre-existing bug caught and fixed while touching this code: SCAVENGER+FEEDER hybrids (Scrub Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish) were never dropping coins at all.** An earlier eat-cooldown rework had gated the passive coin-drop branch (and the eat-cooldown machinery itself) behind a bare `isScavenger` check, which — unlike intended — also caught these three hybrids (SCAVENGER + FEEDER at once), silently zeroing out their `dropInterval` field's actual use. Fixed by introducing `isPureScavenger = isScavenger && !def.behavior.includes('FEEDER')` in `Entities.js`'s `updateFish`, used in place of the bare `isScavenger` check at the three sites where it actually matters: the eat-cooldown gate, the `eatCooldownRemainingMs` assignment (only a pure Scavenger's `dropInterval` gets repurposed as a cooldown), and the passive coin-drop branch's `else if`. A pure Scavenger (Suckerfish itself) is unaffected; a SCAVENGER+FEEDER hybrid still eats Waste with no cooldown, same target-seeking as before, but now actually drops coins on its own (now-rescaled) `dropInterval` again.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): a screenshot confirmed the new rocky shelf rendering as a shaded jagged ledge (visibly distinct from the old dashed line) fading correctly into the black camera-buffer gradient below it; the Food HUD/pellet count confirmed the 2->3 cap change live.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested): the bottom tool-bar stayed visible and clickable with the shop both open and closed, and was confirmed fully absent from inside `#shop-panel`; pressing 1/2/3 correctly set `Selected tool` to `food`/`demolish`/`merge` each time, matching clicking the buttons directly; each button's native `title` showed the right cost/description plus its hotkey number; panning the camera all the way down with Arrow Down showed the seabed texture continuing seamlessly into the new buffer strip before fading to solid black, with the fixed tool-bar sitting naturally within that black area exactly as intended (confirmed by a screenshot).

**The Rocky Shelf became a real physics barrier for every item type (not just Waste), Coins and Science Bubbles each got their own active-drop cap with Coin/Science HUD readouts and blocked-production feedback, and the seabed gained 2 more real buildable rows without changing the tank's visible height** — a consolidated batch, all per direct request ("update the tank physics, coin/science caps, and HUD indicators based on our new tank floor design").

- **World reshuffled to fit 4 buildable rows below the shelf instead of 2, without changing the tank's visible silhouette or the combined tank+buffer scroll depth:** `WORLD_TILES_H` raised 45 → 47 (`WORLD_H` 1440px → 1504px) and `CAMERA_BOTTOM_BUFFER_PX` cut 220px → 156px (exactly 2 tiles) — the sum of the two (the camera's real max scroll depth) is unchanged at 1660px either way, per direct request to "keep the height of the tank and visual buffer the same." The renamed `ROCK_SHELF_Y` (formerly `WASTE_FLOOR_Y`) moved from `WORLD_H - 2*TILE_SIZE` to `WORLD_H - 4*TILE_SIZE`, which — because `WORLD_H` grew by exactly those same 2 tiles — lands at the *identical absolute pixel height* it always rendered at (1376px), so the shelf and everything above it don't visibly shift at all; the 2 new tiles purely add real seabed rows underneath it (2 → 4 buildable rows below the shelf) rather than dead buffer padding, which per direct request also fixes the buffer "being a little too tall."
- **The Rocky Shelf is now a hard barrier for every item type, not just Waste:** `Grid.js`'s `sweepVertical` — previously gated `item.type === 'waste'` — now stops ANY item (coin, Science Bubble, Food, Waste) that falls into `ROCK_SHELF_Y` with nothing built to catch it first, per direct request ("all drops... stop on it and don't pass to the bottom of the tank"). Practically, this means `stepItemOnGrid`'s `'lost'` status (falling off the bottom of the world with no payout — see "Items can't stack, and can fall off the bottom") is no longer reachable through normal gravity for anything; it's kept in place purely as a defensive fallback. The rock-shelf render function was renamed `renderRockShelf` (from `renderWasteFloorBreak`) to match, with no visual change of its own.
- **Coin Cap:** a new active-drop ceiling on Coin items — `state.level.upgrades.coinCapLevel` indexes into `COIN_CAP_BY_LEVEL` (`10 → 25 → 50 → 100 → 250 → 500`, the exact requested progression), upgraded exclusively through a new "🪙 Coin Capacity" Tank Upgrade card (`COIN_CAP_UPGRADE_COSTS`, 5 levels of Tank Points — same `createUpgradeCard` DOM/refresh pattern Food Capacity already used, just an absolute-value table instead of a base+increment formula since the steps aren't evenly spaced). `Entities.js`'s `countTankItemsByType(state, 'coin')` counts EVERY coin anywhere in `state.level.items` (seabed city included — unlike Food's own cap, there's no "only while reachable" carve-out, since the point is "how many drops exist unbanked in the world" full stop), checked by `updateFish` right before a coin would spawn.
- **Science Cap:** the same mechanic for Science Bubble items (`SCIENCE_CAP_BY_LEVEL`, `5 → 10 → 20 → 30 → 40 → 50`), but upgraded exclusively through a new "🫧 Bubble Capacity" card in the Science Lab modal (`SCIENCE_CAP_UPGRADE_SCIENCE_COSTS`/`_GOLD_COSTS`, 5 levels costing both Science AND gold like every other Lab node) instead of a Tank Upgrade, per direct request. Shown as its own small leveled card sitting above the branching tree (`#lab-capacity-wrap`, built once by `buildLabCapacityCard` alongside `buildLabTree`, refreshed every frame the modal's open from inside `refreshLabTree`) rather than a node inside `SCIENCE_LAB_UPGRADES` — a capacity bump doesn't gate any species/building unlock, so it has no natural place in that dependency web.
- **Capped-state feedback:** when a fish's drop cycle completes while its resource is already at its active cap, `Entities.js`'s new `triggerProductionBlocked` fires instead of spawning anything — a muted "🫧" floating indicator above the fish's head (the same `floatingTexts`/`createPickupText` particle system every other in-tank readout uses, just in a new desaturated `PRODUCTION_BLOCKED_COLOR` instead of a normal gain color) plus a new `Sound.js` SFX, `playProductionBlocked()` (a dull descending 2-note triangle-wave "thud," deliberately the one SFX in the game meant to read as a non-event rather than a reward). A blocked Science brew is an all-or-nothing check up front (if the tank's already full, the whole cycle's blocked with one feedback event) but a Science brew that's only PARTIALLY blocked (the batch's `dropValue` would overflow the remaining room) still spawns as many bubbles as fit, silently, with no separate feedback for that partial case. Per direct request, only a blocked COIN also arms the HUD's "shake red" cue — `state.ui.coinCapFlashPending`, a plain cross-module flag (Entities.js has no reason to import UI.js just for this) read and cleared by `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` on its very next frame, which calls the same `playFlash(..., 'flash-spend')` the money-insufficient shake already uses, targeted at the Coin HUD readout instead.
- **New HUD readouts:** `#hud-coin-cap`/`#shop-coin-cap` (`🪙 current/max`, always visible, mirroring Food's own dual HUD-and-shop-panel copy) and `#hud-science-cap`/`#shop-science-cap` (`🔬 current/max`, hidden until the Science Octopus is unlocked — same "hidden until relevant" precedent `#hud-power` already uses for the Electric Eel).
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested): a screenshot confirmed the rocky shelf renders at the same visual position as before with visibly more (4, not 2) real seabed rows beneath it and a shorter black buffer strip; placing a building via the debug `T` cheat in the new deep rows succeeded with no errors; spawning 15 grown Guppies and running the sim at 10x for 15+ seconds with the default Coin Cap (10) produced an item count that plateaued rather than growing unbounded, with the Coin HUD holding steady at exactly `🪙 10/10`; a targeted poll confirmed the Coin HUD's `flash-spend` "shake red" class actually fires once the cap is hit and a fish's drop is blocked; buying a level of the new Coin Capacity Tank Upgrade card live-updated its description text and immediately raised the HUD's denominator (`0/10` → `0/25`); opening the Science Lab (after fully cracking the Mound) showed the new Bubble Capacity card above the tree showing its dual Science+gold cost (`10 🔬 · $500`), and buying a level correctly incremented it.

**The Rocky Shelf reworked into a flat-topped geological boundary with the jaggedness moved to its underside, and everything below it repainted as a visually distinct "underground" mini-biome** — per direct request, landing right after the batch above.

- **The shelf's flat/jagged edges swapped** (`Grid.js`'s `renderRockShelf`): the TOP edge is now a flat line at `ROCK_SHELF_Y` with a solid fill plus a thin lighter highlight strip — deliberately the same "flat fill + thin highlight strip" treatment `renderSeabedGrid` already uses for the tank/city surface line at `SEABED_ROW_START`, per direct request to make the shelf read "closer to the separation between the tank and the city." The jaggedness that used to be on the top edge moved to the BOTTOM instead — `ROCK_SHELF_POINTS`' per-x jitter now offsets the underside of the rock body, so the shelf reads as a flat ledge whose rocky underside hangs down irregularly into the space below it, rather than a jagged outcrop poking up. That hanging underside is now "the part extending the 3D look," per direct request — the flat top no longer has any 3D/depth treatment of its own, only the boundary-line highlight.
- **A new "underground" biome fill below the shelf** (`Grid.js`'s `getUndergroundTexturePattern`, `renderSeabedGrid`): everything from the shelf's flat top downward is repainted with a darker, cooler base fill (`#352a1f`) plus its own speckle texture — generated with the exact same technique as the existing city speckle pattern (`getCityTexturePattern`), just recolored with a few pale blue-grey mineral flecks mixed into the dark speckle instead of plain black/white, so it reads as "mostly like the city biome but a deeper rock version of it," per direct request, rather than an unrelated pattern. Painted as a plain rect from `ROCK_SHELF_Y` down (simple and cheap), with `renderRockShelf`'s own jagged underside drawn on top of that boundary afterward — the rock visibly overlaps into the underground fill rather than the fill starting at a hard, flat edge, reinforcing "the shelf's rock hangs down into the cave beneath it." The 4 real seabed rows below the shelf (see the batch above) now visibly sit inside this darker underground fill rather than looking like a continuation of the surface city.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): a screenshot confirmed the flat top edge with its highlight strip reading as a clean boundary line, the jagged underside now hanging down into a visibly darker, cooler-toned underground area distinct from the warm brown city dirt above it, and the whole thing rendering correctly at every camera position from the top of the seabed down to the max scroll depth.

**Fish Merging cost cut, Gene-Splicing rebuilt from a single Tank Upgrade flag into its own branching Science Lab hybrid tree gating each of the 12 hybrids individually, and Bubble Capacity split out of its leveled card into 5 chained tree nodes** — per direct request, landing right after the batch above.

- **Fish Merging cut from 10 to 5 Tank Points** (`FISH_MERGING_UNLOCK_COST`) — no other change to how it's bought or what it gates.
- **Gene-Splicing moved off its standalone Tank Upgrade card entirely, into a new sub-tree inside `SCIENCE_LAB_UPGRADES`** (`Config.js`): a root node `gene_splicing` (10 🔬/$1000, `requires: ['eel', 'suckerfish']`, grants nothing by itself — per direct request, "just a pre-requisite to unlocking each hybrid fish") gates 3 "track" nodes, one per hybrid combination type — `suckerfish_hybrids`, `electric_hybrids`, `science_hybrids` (10 🔬/$1000 each, also granting nothing on their own, "just gives access to those hybrid tracks") — grouped by the exact same `scrub_*`/`volt_*`/`scholar_*` id-prefix convention the species rows already use. Beneath each track sit that type's individual hybrid nodes (12 total: 5 `scrub_*`, 4 `volt_*`, 3 `scholar_*`), each a flat 25 🔬/$5000 and granting exactly its own species into `speciesUnlocked` — mirroring how `eel`/`suckerfish`/`octopus` already grant themselves. Requiring `eel`+`suckerfish` only at the root (not repeated on all 12 leaf nodes) still transitively gates every hybrid purchase behind both utility species, per direct request ("make the purchase of the suckerfish and eel as requirements for all the hybrid purchases") — once `gene_splicing` is bought, both are already guaranteed owned.
- **Science Hybrids is additionally gated behind at least one Bubble Capacity purchase**, per direct request: its `requires` list is `['gene_splicing', 'science_cap_1']` — requiring `science_cap_1` specifically (the first node of the new capacity chain below) already means "at least one," since `science_cap_2`+ each require the previous node in that same sequence, so owning any later one implies owning the first.
- **Each hybrid combination is now its own individual unlock check, not a blanket flag** (`Entities.js`): `isSpliceSource` is a coarse pre-check now (`state.meta.labUpgradesPurchased.includes(GENE_SPLICING_LAB_ID)`, a new Config.js constant replacing the retired `GENE_SPLICING_TECH_ID`/`state.meta.techUnlocked`, both removed entirely) — necessary but not sufficient, since it fires on mousedown before any target is even known. `canSpliceFish` does the fine-grained check once both fish are known: after resolving `hybridId` via the existing `getHybridSpeciesId`, it now also requires `state.meta.speciesUnlocked.includes(hybridId)` — the exact same array each hybrid's own Lab node purchase pushes into, so dragging Suckerfish onto an Adult Guppy only succeeds once `scrub_guppy` specifically has been bought, even if every other hybrid (or the whole rest of the tree) is unlocked.
- **Bubble Capacity split from one leveled card into 5 separate chained tree nodes**, per direct request ("change the max science upgrades so each one is a separate icon... instead of 5 times on the same icon") — `UI.js`'s standalone `#lab-capacity-wrap` card (`buildLabCapacityCard`/`refreshLabCapacityCard`, plus its `describeScienceCapacityLevel` helper) is gone entirely, replaced by 5 new `SCIENCE_LAB_UPGRADES` nodes (`science_cap_1`..`_5`, named "Bubble Cap 10" through "Bubble Cap 50", each requiring the previous) using the exact same `SCIENCE_CAP_UPGRADE_SCIENCE_COSTS`/`_GOLD_COSTS` values as before — same progression, different presentation. `grants` gained a new field, `scienceCapLevel` (a plain `+1`, since each node is a discrete one-time step) — `UI.js`'s generic `buyLabUpgrade` applies it the same way it already applies `grants.species`/`grants.buildings`, alongside them rather than as a special case.
- **The debug `U` cheat still means "everything," including the new nodes:** `Object.keys(SCIENCE_LAB_UPGRADES)` already covers all 17 new nodes automatically, but marking a `science_cap_*` node "purchased" that way doesn't itself apply its `scienceCapLevel` grant (that side effect only lives in `buyLabUpgrade`, which the cheat bypasses) — `main.js`'s `U` handler now also sets `state.level.upgrades.scienceCapLevel` to its max directly so the cheat's "everything unlocked" promise holds for the Bubble Cap chain too, not just its tree buttons.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): stepped through the full dependency chain — `gene_splicing` stayed locked until both `eel` and `suckerfish` were bought, then became purchasable; `suckerfish_hybrids`/`electric_hybrids` became purchasable immediately after, while `science_hybrids` stayed locked until `science_cap_1` was also bought; every individual hybrid node correctly showed `25 🔬 · $5000` once (and only once) its track was unlocked. Confirmed the per-hybrid gate live in the tank: after unlocking the full path to `scrub_guppy` only (leaving `scrub_dartfin` unbought), dragging an Adult Suckerfish onto an Adult Guppy successfully spliced (entity count 2→1), while the identical drag onto an Adult Dartfin was silently refused (entity count unchanged at 3) — confirming the gate is genuinely per-hybrid, not per-track. Buying `science_cap_1` through `_5` in sequence walked the Science HUD's cap denominator through `10→20→30→40→50` exactly. The `U` cheat, checked on a fresh reload, showed every lab node (old and new) as purchased and the Science cap HUD reading `.../50`.

**Shop/Tank Upgrades panels widened 10% with a growable stats box, a real bug fix for the Rudimentary Fan never actually unlocking via the debug cheat, and the Science Lab tree switched from scroll to click-and-drag panning** — three more direct requests landed together.

- **`#shop-panel`/`#tank-panel` widened 260px → 286px** (10%, per direct request "so fit more info for the building stats"), and `#shop-preview`'s own box changed from a hard `height: 180px` to `min-height: 180px` — per direct request ("make sure the shop window fits all the stats for the building"). Previously a building with a tall stats block (the Processor family's 4 lines, in particular) could overflow past the fixed-height preview box; now the box grows to fit whatever's actually inside it, still floored at the old 180px for anything shorter so the icon grid below doesn't jump around for the common case.
- **Real bug fix: the debug `N` cheat was silently eating the Rudimentary Fan (and Auto-Feeder) grant every time it ran**, never actually broken in real play. `main.js`'s `N` handler used to pre-set `moundTeased`/`fanUnlockPurchased`/`autoFeederUnlockPurchased` to `true` and call `crackMound()` once — but `crackMound`'s own grant branches are each gated on the matching flag still being `false` (`!state.level.fanUnlockPurchased`, etc.), so forcing them `true` first made every one of those branches skip itself, and `crackMound` fell straight through to a bare tier increment with nothing granted. Verified via the real (non-cheat) Mound-click UI path that the Fan grants correctly there — the bug was specifically in the cheat's shortcut, which is presumably how this was being tested and read as "never unlocks." Fixed by having `N` call the REAL `crackMound()` repeatedly (topping up money before each call so affordability is never the blocker) until the tier genuinely advances, walking through the tease/Fan-grant/Auto-Feeder-grant sub-steps for real instead of pre-declaring them done — verified live that a single `N` press from a fresh load now correctly lands at Tier 2 with the Rudimentary Fan AND Processor present in the build palette, and a second press lands at Tier 3 with the Auto-Feeder too.
