- **The Science Lab tree is click-and-drag panned now, not scrolled**, per direct request. `#lab-tree-wrap` switched from `overflow: auto` (with a custom scrollbar) to `overflow: hidden` plus `cursor: grab` — `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` stay fully readable/settable via JS regardless of the `overflow` value, so `UI.js`'s new `initLabTreeDrag` just drives them directly from a `mousedown`→`mousemove`→`mouseup` sequence instead of the browser's own scroll handling (`drawLabTreeConnectors` already read `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` for its bezier math, so panning this way needed no further change there). A `LAB_TREE_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX` (6px) gate disambiguates a drag from a plain click: below it, letting go still fires whatever's underneath (buying a node); at or above it, a capture-phase `click` listener on the wrap swallows the synthetic click a mouseup would otherwise generate, so panning across a node button never also spends money on it. Verified via direct DOM `MouseEvent` dispatch (Playwright's own raw `page.mouse.*` API turned out to have an unrelated environment quirk against this specific element — locator-based clicks and synthetic `dispatchEvent` calls both confirmed correct behavior): a drag moved `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` by exactly the drag delta and visibly panned the tree in a before/after screenshot; a plain click on an unlocked, affordable node still purchased it; a drag that started on top of a different node did NOT purchase it.

**A hunger growl SFX, build-placement failure feedback (a one-time "needs a Platform" HUD message plus always-on red cursor text), the Food Capacity cap retired in favor of a stationary-to-Waste timer, and utility fish joined the economy species' dynamic pricing** — a large batch, all per direct request, landing together.

- **A low "stomach growl" SFX (`Sound.js`'s new `playHunger`) plays once per fish crossing into its second, more urgent hunger stage** (`HUNGER_CRITICAL_THRESHOLD` — the same threshold that already shows the "!!" indicator) — edge-triggered via a new per-fish `hungerCriticalSfxPlayed` flag (`Entities.js`'s `updateFish`, `createFish`), reset once hunger drops back below the threshold (typically after eating) so it can fire again next time, rather than repeating every tick while it stays hungry.
- **Failed building placements now give real feedback instead of silently doing nothing** (`main.js`'s new `handleBuildPlacementFailure`, called from every placement-attempt site — the Fan's two-click aim flow and `updateBuildDrag`'s single-click-or-drag flow alike, so the behavior can't drift between them): every failure shows a small red reason directly above the cursor for about a second (`"Can't afford"` / `"Needs Platform"`, screen-space `ctx.fillText` in `render()`, fading out over its last third), and the very first time a placement fails specifically for lacking a Platform to anchor to, that also posts a one-time HUD notification — "You're gonna need platform for that. Can't have your buildings floating, we are all about our commitment to realistic physics" (`state.level.tutorialFlags.firstPlatformNeeded`, same one-shot-flag pattern every other "first X" story beat already uses).
- **The Food Capacity cap (and its Tank Upgrade) are retired entirely, replaced by a stationary-to-Waste timer** — per direct request ("completely remove the cap for feed pellets, and the ability to upgrade that cap... have food that's been stationary for more than 10 seconds turn into waste"). `FOOD_MAX_ON_SCREEN_BASE`/`FOOD_CAPACITY_UPGRADE_*` and the "🧺 Food Capacity" Tank Upgrade card are gone; `trySpawnFood` no longer has a capacity check at all. In their place, `Entities.js`'s `updateFood` tracks each pellet's own `stationaryOriginX/Y` and `stationaryTimer`: every tick, if the pellet has moved more than `FOOD_STATIONARY_MOVE_TOLERANCE_PX` (4px) from that origin, the origin resets to the current position and the timer resets to 0 ("if it moves within that 10 seconds it restarts the countdown," directly); otherwise the timer accumulates, and at `FOOD_STATIONARY_TO_WASTE_MS` (10s) the pellet is replaced with a real Waste item at the same spot instead of despawning. This also retired the older, narrower `FOOD_FLOOR_GRACE_MS` despawn-after-1s-resting-on-a-tile mechanic, which the new position-based check supersedes (broader — it also now catches food stuck motionless in open water, not just food that's reached a real seabed tile). Since `updateFood` runs inside `state.level.items.filter(...)` in `updateEntities`, it can't safely push a new Waste item into that same array mid-filter (the push wouldn't survive the filter's own reassignment) — it queues the spawn point into a new module-level `pendingFoodToWasteSpawns` array instead, flushed into `state.level.items` right after the filter completes, mirroring the exact pattern `updateBuildings`' own `foodSpawnPoints`/`wasteSpawnPoints` already used for the identical reason. The Food HUD (`#hud-food`/`#shop-food`) dropped its `/cap` denominator, now just a live count.
- **Utility fish (Suckerfish/Electric Eel/Science Octopus) now get the same dynamic pricing the 3 economy species already had** — per direct request ("make sure all the utility fish also get more expensive with each fish on screen"). `Config.js`'s new `DYNAMIC_PRICED_SPECIES_IDS` (economy + utility ids combined) replaces the narrower `ECONOMY_SPECIES_IDS` check inside `Entities.js`'s `getFishPurchaseCost` — `ECONOMY_SPECIES_IDS` itself is untouched and still the only list `isCombinableFish` reads, so utility fish gained pricing scarcity WITHOUT also becoming eligible for star-tier combining. "Hybrid fish do not count towards the limit" needed no additional filtering at all — `countLivingFishOfSpecies` already only ever matches a fish's own EXACT `speciesId`, and a hybrid's id (e.g. `scrub_guppy`) is never equal to its parent's (`guppy` or `suckerfish`), so it was already structurally impossible for a hybrid to inflate its parent species' price.
- **New base costs, per direct request:** Guppy $20→$15, Dartfin $12→$10, Blimpfish $60→$40, Suckerfish $25→$20, Electric Eel $80→$35, Science Octopus $90→$60.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): placing 8 food pellets in a row all spawned successfully (no cap refusal); the Food Capacity card was confirmed absent from the Tank Upgrades panel; a dropped pellet's HUD count dropped from `1` to `0` after 10+ simulated seconds stationary while the world's total item count held steady at `1` — confirming it converted to Waste rather than vanishing or duplicating. A building placement attempt with no Platform nearby showed red "Needs Platform" cursor text and posted the one-time HUD notification, its chat pill text matching the expected copy exactly; a separate attempt with insufficient money showed red "Can't afford" text instead. All 6 species' shop prices matched their new base costs exactly on a fresh load; buying 3 Suckerfish in a row raised the Suckerfish price from $20 to $39 (matching `20 × 1.25³`) while leaving the Electric Eel's own price completely unchanged.

**A first-launch start screen with a blurred tank behind it, Start/Settings/Help, and the title splash now waits for Start instead of autoplaying** — per direct request, the last batch landing in this session.

- **`index.html`'s new `#start-overlay`/`#start-modal`** — shown from the moment the page loads, above everything else in the game (`z-index: 600`, higher than the splash's own 500) — dims and *blurs* the tank behind it via `backdrop-filter: blur(10px)` on the overlay itself (a compositor-level blur on one static element, nothing like the per-frame `ctx.filter` cost this codebase otherwise avoids on the canvas). `main.js`'s `update()` gates the entire sim loop behind a new `state.ui.gameStarted` flag, checked ahead of (and independently from) the existing `paused`/`gameOver` early returns — the tank still renders (blurred, dimmed) and ambience (bubbles/seaweed) still drifts behind the modal so it reads as alive, but nothing else ticks until Start is clicked. The Escape key is a no-op until then too (the start screen's own buttons are the only way to navigate it, not Escape).
- **Settings, opened from the start screen, is *literally* the pause menu's own Settings sub-view** — not a second copy of the Music/SFX sliders. `UI.js`'s new `initStartScreen` shows the existing `#pause-overlay`/`#pause-settings` layered directly on top of `#start-overlay` (which stays visible underneath, so its blur keeps covering the game) rather than swapping one overlay for the other — `#pause-overlay`'s z-index moved to 650 specifically so it stacks above the start screen when opened this way. A new `settingsOpenedFromStartScreen` flag (checked by a shared `returnFromPauseSettings` helper, used by both the Settings sub-view's "Back" button and a backdrop click) makes "Back" return to the start screen instead of resuming a game that was never running, without touching the normal pause-menu-mid-game flow at all. (An earlier version of this hid `#start-overlay` and showed `#pause-overlay` in its place instead — since the latter had no blur of its own and a lower z-index than the still-untriggered splash screen, that produced a broken-looking frame with the raw "Derangiquarium" splash text bleeding through unblurred behind the Settings card; caught via a screenshot during verification and fixed by layering instead of swapping.)
- **Help — a new tutorial page** (`#start-help-overlay`/`#start-help-modal`), a scrollable list of icon+text rows (`.help-row`/`.help-icon`/`.help-text`) covering Food, hunger indicators, Platform anchoring, Fans, Processors, Auto-Feeders, banking coins by hand, the Mound, the Science Lab, the Merge tool, and Tank Points — reusing the same emoji icon language the game's own HUD/shop already use throughout, per direct request for "icons and pictures to help." Its own Back button returns to the start screen the same way Settings' does.
- **The title splash no longer plays automatically on load** — per direct request, it now waits for Start. `style.css` moved the `animation:` declarations off the bare `#splash-title`/`.splash-letter` selectors onto `#splash-title.play`/`#splash-title.play .splash-letter` instead, so the CSS keyframes exist but don't run until that class is present; `main.js`'s new `triggerSplash()` (called from `initStartScreen`'s `onStart` callback, alongside setting `state.ui.gameStarted = true`) adds it. Everything downstream — the per-letter stagger, the `animationend` listener that removes `#splash-screen` once it's done — is unchanged, just now starting on a real user action instead of page load.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): confirmed the start overlay is up and the splash is NOT yet animating on a fresh load, and that debug cheat keys are fully inert before Start is clicked. Settings from the start screen showed the real pause-settings sliders (with a live value) and correctly returned to the start screen on Back — a screenshot confirmed the tank rendered properly blurred behind it after the layering fix, not the earlier broken unblurred frame. Help opened showing all 11 rows and also returned cleanly via its own Back button. Clicking Start hid the start overlay, immediately began the splash animation (confirmed via the `.play` class and a mid-animation screenshot), and unblocked the debug cheats/game HUD, which responded normally afterward. A full regression pass confirmed the Mound/Science Lab, Tank Upgrades panel, tool switching, and the normal mid-game pause menu (via Escape) all still work exactly as before once the game has actually started.

**Start screen follow-up: sized to match the pause menu, and a real fix for what was very likely making it feel unclickable/stuck open** — per direct report ("the start modal should be the same size and look as the pause menu, right now they span the entire width of the screen, and nothing in the start modal can be clicked, and it never goes away. the splash screen still plays immediately").

- **`#start-modal` resized 300px → 280px with the exact same padding/border/radius/shadow recipe as `#pause-menu`**, per direct request — plus `flex-shrink: 0` and `max-width: 90vw` added defensively so the flex-centered card can never be stretched to fill `#start-overlay`'s full width under any viewport, even though nothing in the previous CSS should have caused that in the first place (a fresh Playwright check of the prior version measured the modal at a correct 300×321px, not full-width — this couldn't be reproduced directly, but the fix is a strict improvement regardless and the sizing now literally matches the pause menu's own numbers).
- **The real, most likely explanation for "can't click anything" / "it never goes away": `#start-overlay`'s `backdrop-filter: blur(10px)` sat over a canvas that kept animating underneath it** (`updateAmbience` — bubbles/seaweed — ran every tick regardless of `state.ui.gameStarted`, by original design, "so the blurred tank still reads as alive"). A `backdrop-filter` blur is a compositor-level effect that has to be recomputed whenever the content behind it changes; continuously animating that content every single frame, under a full-viewport blur, is exactly the kind of thing that can make a page feel laggy or briefly unresponsive on weaker hardware — which reads exactly like "nothing responds to my click" even though the click handlers themselves were never broken (confirmed independently in Playwright, which is not GPU-bound the same way). Fixed by simply not calling `updateAmbience` at all until `state.ui.gameStarted` is true — `Ambience.js`'s bubble/seaweed positions are seeded once at module load regardless (not something `updateAmbience` populates on first call), so the tank behind the start screen still renders fully populated, just as one static, cheap-to-blur frame instead of a constantly repainting one.
- Re-verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): waited a full 6 idle seconds on a fresh load with zero interaction and confirmed the splash still had no `.play` class and the start overlay was still showing (never auto-plays, never auto-closes); measured `#start-modal` at a real 280px width, matching `#pause-menu`'s own CSS width exactly; clicked Start and confirmed the overlay closed and the splash began animating immediately in response, with the normal pause menu (via Escape) still opening and closing correctly afterward.

**Start screen follow-up #2: the modal no longer names the game, the splash is now genuinely invisible (not just non-animating) until Start, and a real bug in the letter-bounce animation is fixed** — per direct report ("the start mode[al] does not say the name of the game"... "the splash screen should only start and be visible once the start button has been pressed. Right now you can see the splash screen the whole time behind the start modal"... "the splash screen still does the bounce animation all of that's gone now bring that back").

- **`#start-title` (the "Derangiquarium" text) removed from the start modal entirely** — per direct request, so the name isn't shown twice/spoiled ahead of the splash's own reveal. `#start-subtitle` ("An incremental aquarium, gone a little feral" — never named the game itself) now stands alone as the modal's only header text, restyled slightly bolder/colored to read properly without a title above it.
- **`#splash-screen` now starts at `opacity: 0`, not just "no animation yet"** — the previous fix (gating only the *animation* behind a `.play` class on `#splash-title`) left the word itself sitting there at full, static opacity the whole time, which — while visually behind the `#start-overlay`'s blur — could still read as faintly visible through it (confirmed in an earlier screenshot, and exactly what was reported: "you can see the splash screen the whole time"). Fixed by moving the `.play` gate one level up, onto `#splash-screen` itself, controlling both visibility (`opacity: 0` → `1`) and (via `#splash-screen.play` descendant selectors) both animation layers together — `main.js`'s `triggerSplash()` now adds `.play` to `splashScreen` instead of `splashTitle`. The word is now genuinely invisible, not just unanimated, until Start is actually clicked.
- **Real bug fix: `.splash-letter`'s `display: inline-block` had been silently dropped in an earlier pass**, when the per-letter bounce animation was split out of the base `.splash-letter` rule into the newly-gated `#splash-title.play .splash-letter` rule — the `display: inline-block` declaration never made it into either rule, left off entirely. A plain `<span>`'s default `display: inline` ignores `transform` per the CSS spec (inline boxes aren't transformable), so `splash-letter-bounce`'s `translateY()` keyframes were running the whole time (confirmed via `getComputedStyle`) but visually doing *nothing* — exactly "the bounce animation... all of that's gone," even though the animation itself was technically still firing. Restored as its own always-on base rule (`.splash-letter { display: inline-block; }`, unconditional, not gated behind `.play`) so the transform can actually apply once the bounce animation runs.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): confirmed `#splash-screen`'s computed `opacity` is exactly `0` before Start and becomes `1` immediately after clicking it; confirmed `#start-modal`'s visible text no longer contains "Derangiquarium" anywhere; sampled every `.splash-letter`'s live bounding-box `top` position ~1.5s into the animation and confirmed genuinely different values per letter (not a flat line), each element computed as `display: inline-block` — and a screenshot taken at that exact moment visually shows the letters mid-hop at clearly different heights, confirming the bounce is real and working again, not just theoretically running.

**Start modal trimmed to just its buttons, the Food HUD readout removed, the Electric Auto-Feeder recolored, and Processor/Auto-Feeder direction arrows made more visible** — four more direct requests landed together, right after the splash-visibility batch above.
- **`#start-subtitle` ("An incremental aquarium, gone a little feral") removed from the start modal too** — per direct request ("it's supposed to be a less is more type of visual"), leaving just the 3 buttons (Start/Settings/Help). Nothing else in the modal changed.
- **The Food HUD readout (`#hud-food`/`#shop-food`) removed entirely** — per direct request. `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` dropped the block that computed/wrote it, `Entities.js`'s now-unused `countTankFood` helper was deleted along with it (its only caller), and `index.html`/`css/style.css` dropped the two elements and their shared-pill selector entry.
- **`TILE_AUTO_FEEDER_ELECTRIC`'s color changed from `#7fd6a8` to `#4fd6e0`** (`Config.js`) — per direct report that it read too close to the base Processor's own `#8fe0b8` (both light minty greens). The new color is a distinct cyan/teal; every other Processor/Auto-Feeder tier's color is untouched.
- **Processor/Auto-Feeder direction arrows made bigger and higher-contrast** (`Grid.js`'s `renderDirectionIndicator`) — per direct report ("the arrows are kind of hard to see"). Each arrow (output and intake) now draws a wide, mostly-opaque white "halo" stroke underneath its real color first — the same cheap fake-outline trick already used elsewhere in this file for contrast without a blur filter — plus a larger arm length/head size and full-opacity solid colors (black output, cyan intake) instead of the old translucent versions. A tile's fill spans blues/greens/purples/gold across every tier, so a single fixed arrow color couldn't stay legible against all of them without the halo. The Fan cone-arrow rendering (a separate code path) is unchanged.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): the start modal's visible text was confirmed to contain only the 3 button labels; `#hud`/`#shop-hud` were confirmed to contain no food/plate readout anywhere; and both the color change and arrow rework were confirmed via `cycleTileCheat`'s exact `CHEAT_CYCLE` index mapping (2 presses from a fresh empty tile → base Processor, 9 presses → Electric Auto-Feeder) to place specific tiers side-by-side for a direct screenshot comparison.

**Shop preview box made a genuinely fixed size, the Science Lab tree starts vertically centered and can be dragged from anywhere in the modal (not just its own background) and zoomed, a real purchase-confirmation modal replaced buy-on-click, and opening the Lab or picking a bottom-tool-bar tool now auto-closes the Shop/Tank Upgrades panel** — a large batch, all per direct request, landing in the same session as the Batch F entry above.
- **`#shop-preview` is a hard `height: 232px` again, not `min-height`** — per direct request ("I don't want it pushing the shop items up and down every time I click a different option"). The earlier `min-height` pass (see the shop/Tank Upgrades panel-widening entry further up) fixed a real clipping bug (a tall stats block, e.g. the Processor family's 4 lines, used to overflow past the box) but reintroduced a different annoyance: the icon grid below visibly shifted up/down every time the box's own height changed between selections. 232px is generous enough that every real building/species' content fits without scrolling in practice; `#shop-preview-content` picked up `overflow-y: auto` (with the game's usual pink scrollbar styling) purely as a safety net for anything that somehow doesn't, scrolling INSIDE the fixed box instead of resizing it.
- **The Lab tree starts vertically centered instead of pinned to its top edge** — `UI.js`'s `openLabMenu` now sets `#lab-tree-wrap`'s `scrollTop` to `(scrollHeight - clientHeight) / 2` (and resets `scrollLeft`/zoom to their defaults) every time it's opened, right after the overlay is unhidden (has to happen after — a `display:none` element has no layout box, so `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` would both read 0 beforehand).
- **Drag-to-pan now works from anywhere in the modal, not just the tree's own background** — per a direct follow-up correction after the request was initially misread ("what I meant was you can drag anywhere WITHIN the science lab... right now you have to specifically click the background to be able to drag it"). The mousedown listener that arms a drag moved from `#lab-tree-wrap` to the whole `#lab-modal` (`UI.js`'s `initLabTreeDrag`) — panning itself still only ever moves the wrap's own `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop`, only WHERE a drag gesture is allowed to start moved outward. This surfaced a real edge case during testing: since a drag can now start well inside the modal (e.g. the header row) and the gesture can cross back out over the dimmed backdrop before release, the resulting synthetic click's target becomes `#lab-overlay` itself rather than anything inside the modal — the old click-swallow listener (which suppresses the synthetic click a mouseup generates, so panning across a node never also buys it) was scoped to the modal and so wasn't even in that click's propagation path, meaning a drag that happened to end on the backdrop would silently close the whole tree on release. Fixed by moving that swallow listener to `window` in the capture phase — `stopPropagation()` called there, first in the event's whole path, keeps it from ever reaching its real target at all, wherever the drag actually ended.
- **Zoom, via mouse wheel over the tree or new +/- buttons in the header row** — `UI.js`'s `setLabZoom` drives a `--lab-zoom` CSS custom property (`style.css`) that scales every real box-model/font dimension in the tree (node width/padding/radius, column gaps, name/cost font sizes) by the same factor, clamped `[0.6, 1.6]`, rather than a `transform: scale()` — a transform doesn't change an element's actual layout size, which would desync `#lab-tree-wrap`'s real `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` (what the existing drag-pan and `drawLabTreeConnectors`'s `getBoundingClientRect()` math both depend on) from what's visually drawn; scaling the real dimensions instead means all of that existing geometry-reading code keeps working completely unmodified. A wheel event's cursor position is used as a zoom anchor (the point under the cursor stays visually stationary through the zoom step, since every scaled dimension grows uniformly from the tree's own top-left origin); the +/- buttons zoom in place from the current scroll position instead.
- **A real purchase-confirmation modal, replacing buy-on-click** — per direct request ("I want to have a purchase modal pop up... so you have a chance to read what something does and confirm the purchase, instead of just clicking it... give the stats of the building/fish being unlocked"). Clicking a `.lab-node` now calls `UI.js`'s new `openLabPurchaseModal` instead of `buyLabUpgrade` directly; a new centered `#lab-purchase-overlay`/`#lab-purchase-modal` (a smaller copy of `#lab-overlay`'s own dimmed-backdrop pattern, layered on top at a higher z-index) shows the node's icon/name/cost plus, for whatever it actually grants: a species' real description and stats (`speciesStatsHtml` — role, purchase cost, adult coin value, hunger rate, in the same `.building-stat` chip format the shop preview already uses) or a building's (reusing `buildingStatsHtml` directly), or — for a pure-prerequisite node like `gene_splicing` that grants nothing by itself — a note plus `labNodeUnlocksHtml`, a list of every node that node's purchase actually opens up. Confirm is the only thing left that calls `buyLabUpgrade`; Cancel (or clicking the backdrop, or Escape — `isLabPurchaseModalOpen`/`closeLabPurchaseModal` are checked ahead of the tree modal's own Escape handling in `main.js`) closes it with nothing spent.
- **Opening the Science Lab, or picking a bottom-tool-bar tool (Food/Demolish/Merge), now auto-closes the Shop/Tank Upgrades panel if either is open** — per direct request. A small shared `closeSidePanels(state)` helper (`UI.js`) is called from both `openLabMenu` and `selectTool` (the latter already the one shared place both the tool buttons' click handlers and the 1/2/3 hotkeys funnel through, so this needed no changes at either call site). Deliberately NOT wired into species/building selection within the shop itself — that's normal in-shop interaction, not something that should close the shop out from under you.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): the preview box measured exactly 232px whether empty, showing a species' short description, or a building's full 4-line stats block, with the icon grid below it at an identical, unmoving screen position throughout; a fresh Lab open landed `scrollTop` within 1px of the exact computed center; a drag starting from inside `#lab-header-row` (not the tree background) successfully panned `scrollLeft`; a drag that deliberately crossed back onto the dimmed backdrop before release correctly left the tree open (confirming the window-capture swallow fix); a mouse-wheel zoom over the tree changed `--lab-zoom` from `1` to `1.15` and back, and the +/- buttons produced the same effect; a real Electric Eel node's purchase modal showed its correct icon/name/`10 🔬 · $1000` cost/description/role/cost/hunger stats, Cancel left Science/gold and the node's purchased state completely unchanged, and Confirm correctly spent both resources and flipped the node to purchased/disabled; picking the Food tool (hotkey `1`) with the shop open collapsed it, and opening the Lab with the shop reopened did the same.

**A one-shot "food rots into waste" tutorial notification** — per direct request: "When 5 food is on screen for the first time OR the first time food is turned into waste, have a chat pop up saying 'Careful now, food that's chilling too long rots into waste'." `Entities.js`'s new `maybeWarnFoodRot(state)` is a single one-shot gate (`state.level.tutorialFlags.foodRotWarningShown`, `Levels.js`) checked from two call sites — whichever fires first wins, and the flag then blocks the other: `trySpawnFood` counts live `state.level.items` of type `'food'` after each successful placement and fires once that count reaches 5, and `updateFood`'s existing stationary-to-Waste branch (see the earlier "stationary-to-Waste timer" entry) fires it right where it already converts a pellet to Waste. Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): placing 5 food pellets in open water produced the exact message in the notification pill; in a separate fresh session, placing a single pellet and leaving it untouched at 10x time scale produced the same message once it actually rotted into Waste, with no earlier trigger since only 1 pellet ever existed on screen.

**Bubble Cap prerequisites inserted into the Eel/Suckerfish/Gene-Splicing gate chain, the Tank Upgrades panel got its own full HUD mirror (so the main HUD can hide behind either side panel, not just the Shop), a canvas "shimmer/gleam" effect for the Mound/Science Lab and for fish on placement/growth/merge/splice, a Lab node can now be opened to read even when unaffordable (only Confirm greys out), and fish gained real hunger/coin/waste economy stats shown in both the shop and the Lab** — a large batch, all per direct request, landing right after the batch above.
- **`eel`/`suckerfish` now require `science_cap_1` (Bubble Cap 10) instead of being tree roots; `gene_splicing` additionally requires `science_cap_2` (Bubble Cap 20) on top of its existing eel+suckerfish requirement** (`Config.js`'s `SCIENCE_LAB_UPGRADES`). Since `UI.js`'s `labNodeDepth` computes each node's column purely from `requires`, this alone re-lays the tree with the capacity chain now feeding INTO the two utility species instead of sitting beside them as an unrelated branch — no layout code needed changing.
- **The Tank Upgrades panel gets its own `#tank-hud` (money/coin cap/science cap/cleanliness/power), mirroring `#shop-hud` exactly** — per direct request ("make the tank upgrades show the stats of the HUD the same way the shop does, so the hud can be gone when either the shop or tank upgrade is open"). `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` now writes every readout to all three copies (`#hud`, `#shop-hud`, `#tank-hud`) each frame; a new `updateHudVisibility(state)` hides `#hud` whenever EITHER panel is expanded (`!(shopCollapsed && tankPanelCollapsed)`, was shop-only before) and is called from both `updateShopCollapse` and `updateTankPanelCollapse`; a new `visibleHudEl(state, mainEl, shopEl, tankEl)` picks whichever of the three copies is actually on screen for every flash/redirect site (insufficient-funds shake, the coin-cap-blocked shake, the money/cleanliness pickup/spend flash) instead of the old binary shop-or-main choice. `#tank-power` is non-interactive same as `#shop-power` (only the real `#hud-power` opens the graph popup).
- **Canvas "shimmer/gleam" for the Mound and Science Lab, on a random 10-50s cycle** — per direct request ("make it so the mound and the science lab shimmer/gleen like the other objects, but every 10-50 seconds"). New `Shimmer.js` is the canvas equivalent of the DOM `.sheen-target` sweep every UI pill/icon already plays (`UI.js`'s `scheduleSheen`, a random 5-30s per-element cycle) — the Mound/Lab are canvas-drawn, not DOM elements, so this is a small hand-rolled version: `createShimmerTimer`/`updateShimmerTimer` track a recurring random-interval schedule off `state.level.elapsed` (these modules don't own their own per-tick simulation — see Mound.js's own "no per-tick simulation" rule — but `renderMound`/`renderScienceLab` already run every frame regardless, so reading elapsed at render time needed no new call site anywhere), and `drawShimmerSweep` paints a soft diagonal white gradient band across whatever bounding box it's given. `Mound.js` clips the sweep to the dome's own silhouette (already had a clip path from its texture/crack rendering) and to the Science Lab's rect+dome-arc silhouette (a new compound clip path traced for this specifically, since that render didn't already have one). Each object gets its own independent timer so the two don't shimmer in sync.
- **Fish shimmer on placement, a growth-stage advance, or a combine/splice result** — per direct request ("whenever a fish is placed, grows in size, or is merged/spliced, have them shimmer/shine/gleam as well"). `Entities.js`'s `createFish` now sets a `shimmerStartedAt: state.level.elapsed` field on every fish it creates — a single choke point that covers a freshly purchased-and-placed fish AND a combine/splice result for free, since `combineFish`/`createHybridFish` both create their output fish through this exact function. `updateFish` also re-sets it the moment `fish.stage` actually increases (a real growth-stage advance, not just any feed — most feeds don't cross a stage boundary). `main.js`'s fish render loop reads it each frame via `Shimmer.js`'s `oneShotShimmerProgress(fish.shimmerStartedAt, state.level.elapsed)` (a one-shot variant of the timer above — no rescheduling needed, the trigger sites above just set a fresh `startedAt` next time) and, while active, clips to a circle around the fish and paints the same `drawShimmerSweep` used by the Mound/Lab.
- **A Science Lab node can be opened to read its stats even when unaffordable** — per direct request ("let me click all unlocked items in the science lab, even if I can't afford it, so I can read them... any unlocked ones should be able to be clicked, and have the confirm grayed out if they cant afford it"). `UI.js`'s `refreshLabTree` now only sets `btn.disabled` for `purchased`/`!prereqsMet` (a genuinely locked node stays inert, per direct request that that's fine) — an unlocked-but-unaffordable node instead gets a lighter `.unaffordable` dimming class and stays fully clickable, opening the same purchase modal as an affordable one. `openLabPurchaseModal`/`refreshLabPurchaseButton` (the latter re-run every frame the modal's open, alongside the tree's own live refresh, since Science/gold can keep changing while deciding) computes affordability fresh and disables `#lab-purchase-confirm-btn` (styled the same greyed-out look every other disabled buy button in this game already uses) when it can't actually be bought — Cancel still works regardless.
- **Real per-fish economy stats — hunger as food/min, coin value/sec, and waste/sec — shown identically in the shop preview and the Lab purchase modal**, per direct request ("add in the stats for hunger, coin value/seconds, and waste/second for each fish in the shop and science lab... make the hunger stat make sense in terms of the amount of food they need per minute, instead of whatever it's showing in the science lab for hunger now"). `UI.js`'s new shared `fishEconomyStatsHtml(speciesId)`: hunger converts `hungerRate` (points/sec) into food pellets/minute against the UNUPGRADED Food Quality relief amount (`FOOD_HUNGER_RELIEF_BY_LEVEL[0]`, 55) — a fixed per-species comparison figure, deliberately not shifting as the player buys Food Quality upgrades, same as every other stat shown here; coin/sec (`adult.dropValue / adult.dropInterval * 1000`) only shows for an actual coin-dropping species (checked via `dropType === 'coin'`, i.e. a base FEEDER or a feeder-based hybrid); waste/sec (`1000 / WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS`) only shows for a non-Scavenger, mirroring the exact same bare `behavior.includes('SCAVENGER')` gate `Entities.js`'s own poop timer already uses (not the narrower `isPureScavenger` used elsewhere for eating/coin-drop purposes). `speciesStatsHtml` (the Lab modal's builder) now composes Role/Cost plus this shared block instead of its own flat "Adult coin: $X" + raw hunger-per-sec lines; `selectSpeciesForPreview` (the shop) now populates `#shop-preview-stats` the same way buildings already do, instead of hiding it outright.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): stepping through the Lab tree confirmed `eel`/`suckerfish` locked until Bubble Cap 10 was bought (then both unlocked), and `gene_splicing` locked until Bubble Cap 20 was bought on top of eel+suckerfish (then unlocked); with the Shop panel open `#hud` was hidden, and switching to the Tank Upgrades panel (which auto-closes the Shop) kept `#hud` hidden too — previously it would have reappeared — while `#tank-money`/`#tank-coin-cap`/`#tank-cleanliness`/`#tank-science-cap`/`#tank-power` all showed live, correct values matching the Shop's own copies; with both panels closed `#hud` was visible again; a direct Node run of `Shimmer.js`'s timing functions confirmed a recurring timer's first active sweep always lands within the requested [10000, 50000]ms window and a one-shot progress value starts at 0, stays below 1 through its ~900ms duration, and returns to `null` afterward; a screenshot taken ~80ms after placing a fish showed a visible soft white halo around it that was gone in a follow-up screenshot ~1.3s later, confirming the one-shot sweep actually paints and finishes on schedule; draining money down so only `advanced_fan` ($15000) was unlocked-but-unaffordable confirmed it was NOT `.locked`, WAS `.unaffordable`, and was NOT `disabled` — clicking it opened the purchase modal with `#lab-purchase-confirm-btn` correctly disabled, and Cancel still closed it with nothing spent; a Guppy's shop preview showed `🍽️ Hunger: 1.8 food/min`, `🪙 Coin: $0.29/sec`, `💩 Waste: 0.04/sec` (matching `1.624×60/55`, `5/17444×1000`, `1000/27778` respectively, computed by hand to confirm the formulas), fitting cleanly within the still-fixed 232px preview box alongside Role/Cost with no overflow or icon-grid shift.

**Follow-up correction: Science Hybrids no longer requires a Bubble Cap purchase** — per direct request, only Eel/Suckerfish (`science_cap_1`) and Gene-Splicing (`science_cap_2`) actually need one; `science_hybrids`' `requires` dropped the `science_cap_1` entry it had picked up in the batch above, so it now unlocks the moment `gene_splicing` does — same as its `suckerfish_hybrids`/`electric_hybrids` sibling tracks, no special case any more. Since `UI.js`'s `drawLabTreeConnectors` draws exactly one bezier curve per entry in a node's own `requires` array, removing the entry alone was enough to also drop the connector line between Bubble Cap 10 and Science Hybrids — no separate drawing/layout code needed touching. Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): buying Bubble Cap 10 → Eel → Suckerfish → Bubble Cap 20 → Gene-Splicing (deliberately skipping any further Bubble Cap purchase) left Science Hybrids unlocked immediately, identically to Suckerfish Hybrids/Electric Hybrids; a full-tree screenshot (zoomed out, panned to show every node) visually confirmed Science Hybrids has exactly one incoming connector line, from Gene-Splicing only.

**Panel toggle icons 50% bigger, Dartfin's price cut, fish economy stats switched to per-minute, food takes twice as long to rot, single-tier shop items can be deselected by re-clicking, and the "found the chat" fish-vanish gag got a delay/longer duration/a fish-required gate** — a large batch, all per direct request.
- **`#shop-collapse-btn`/`#tank-collapse-btn` (the 🛍️/🏆 panel toggle buttons) grew from 36px/18px-font to 54px/27px-font** (`style.css`) — 50% bigger on both axes, per direct request ("make the shop and tank upgrade icons 50% bigger" — confirmed via a follow-up question that this meant these two specific toggle buttons, not the shop's species/building icon grid or the Tank Upgrades cards' inline emoji). The existing `.panel-toggle-active` state's own `transform: scale(1.1)` still applies on top, so the currently-open panel's button now measures ~59.4px — unchanged relative behavior, just scaled from the new 54px base.
- **Dartfin's cost cut from $10 to $8** (`Config.js`).
- **Fish economy stats switched from per-second to per-minute for BOTH money and waste** (`UI.js`'s `fishEconomyStatsHtml`) — per direct request ("change the waste/sec into waste/min. And have the coins be money/min"). Hunger was already food/min from the prior batch; a per-second rate for money or waste read as an oddly tiny number (a fraction of a cent, a hundredth of an item) next to that, so all three stats now share one time unit. Formulas are the same underlying rates just ×60 (`adult.dropValue / adult.dropInterval * 60000` for money, `60000 / WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` for waste) — the "Coin" label was also renamed "Money" to match.
- **`FOOD_STATIONARY_TO_WASTE_MS` doubled from 10000 to 20000** (`Config.js`) — per direct request ("make food take twice as long to turn to waste"). Pure constant change; `Entities.js`'s `updateFood` stationary-timer logic (already proven correct at the old value in an earlier session's own verification) is untouched.
- **Clicking an already-selected single-tier shop item deselects it, defaulting back to Food** — per direct request, for "things in the shop that only have one tier, like fish, platforms, and the other buildings before they have their upgrades unlocked." `UI.js`'s new `deselectShopSelection(state)` clears the preview back to its empty placeholder and sets `state.ui.selectedTool = 'food'` directly (deliberately NOT via the shared `selectTool` helper, which also closes the Shop/Tank Upgrades panel for the bottom hotbar's own buttons — that would be wrong here, since the player is still meant to be IN the shop, just with nothing picked). Wired into both the species-icon click handler and `buildSingleBuildingButton`'s click handler, each checking `state.ui.selectedTool === 'fish:<id>'`/`'build:<id>'` before deciding whether to select or deselect. Deliberately NOT wired into `buildFamilyButton` (the Fan/Processor/Auto-Feeder family slots) — clicking one of those a second time already cycles to its next tier, an established, unrelated behavior this doesn't touch.
- **"Found the chat" gag reworked: a 1.5s delay before fish vanish, a longer 3.5s vanish, and a fish-purchase gate with an alternate message** — per direct request. `Config.js`'s `FISH_VANISH_DURATION_MS` raised 2500→3500; a new `FISH_VANISH_DELAY_MS` (1500) is a genuinely separate countdown (`state.level.fishVanishDelayMs`, `Levels.js`) that ticks down first in `Entities.js`'s `updateFishVanish` — only once it hits 0 does the function set `fishVanishTimer` to the real (now-longer) duration, so the freeze/hide doesn't start the instant the chat closes any more; nothing else (`main.js`'s render skip, `updateFishVanish`'s own freeze check) needed to learn about the delay's existence, since they only ever look at `fishVanishTimer` itself. The whole gag is now also gated on `state.level.tutorialFlags.firstFishBought` (the same flag `trySpawnPurchasedFish` already sets) — if the player closes the chat for the first time without ever having bought a fish, nothing vanishes at all, and a new `FOUND_THE_CHAT_NO_FISH_MESSAGE` ("You found the chat, you curious little fish.") shows instead of the original "Curiosity kills the fish" line.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): both toggle buttons measured 54×54 at rest (59.4×59.4 for whichever was the active/open one, matching the pre-existing 1.1x scale); Dartfin's shop price read exactly `$8`; a Dartfin's stats block read `1.4 food/min`, `$21.32/min`, `2.16/min` (hand-checked against `1.264×60/55`, `3/8444×60000`, `60000/27778`); re-clicking an already-selected Dartfin icon or Platform building each correctly cleared the preview back to empty, set the Food tool active, and — critically — left the Shop panel itself still open (an earlier pass of this fix incorrectly routed through `selectTool`, which also closed the whole panel); re-clicking an already-selected multi-tier family slot (Processor) still cycled tiers as before, confirming it wasn't accidentally caught by the new deselect logic; with no fish ever purchased, the first chat-close showed the new "you curious little fish" line; with a fish purchased and placed, the first chat-close showed the original line, and a canvas pixel scan sampled every 200ms showed the fish still visibly rendered through ~1.4-1.6s after close (matching the 1.5s delay), invisible from ~1.6s to ~4.9-5.1s (a ~3.5s gap, matching the new duration), and visible again after — both boundaries landing almost exactly on the expected 1500ms/5000ms marks.

**Coin click radius bumped a third time, 60%→90% bigger than the coin itself** (`COIN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER`, `Config.js`) — per direct request. Purely a hit-test radius change, same as the two prior bumps (40%→60%, then this one) — a coin's actual drawn/collision size (`COIN_RADIUS`) is untouched, and `Entities.js`'s `tryBankCoinAt` already only ever banks the first match it finds per click and returns immediately, so an overlapping pair of these even-bigger radii still can't bank two coins on one click (verified by inspection — that early-return was already in place and unrelated to this constant).

**World shrunk to one screen width (no more horizontal panning), Demolish/Merge gray out when unavailable, the Shop deselects a picked fish on close, every building's directional intake arrow was removed in favor of omnidirectional suck-in with a fixed top output, cleanliness-per-waste halved again, a fish production tuning pass, a shop-button bounce reminder, Fan ghost cones suppressed until placement is confirmed, a fish Speed stat, and the full Alien Invasion system (waves, portals, 3 Turret buildings, alien AI, fish reactions, health bars, a countdown HUD)** — the largest single batch this project has taken, all per one direct request. Full per-mechanism detail below; this is the consolidated changelog.
- **World narrowed to one screen width, no horizontal camera panning:** `WORLD_TILES_W` cut 160→60 (`WORLD_W` 5120px→1920px, see the updated "Coordinate system & world constants" above) — per direct request, "everything should happen within the initial horizontal viewport." `Engine.js`'s `updateCamera` drops horizontal pan entirely (`KeyA`/`KeyD`/arrow-left/right and horizontal wheel scroll are still drained from `input` each call so they don't leak into anything else, just never applied to `camera.x`); `camera.x` is now a single unclamped `(WORLD_W - viewW) / 2` every frame, which centers correctly whether the world is narrower or wider than the current viewport (derived and sanity-checked algebraically — an earlier two-branch clamp attempt was recognized as wrong for the narrower-than-viewport case and simplified away before shipping). Vertical panning is completely unchanged. `Entities.js` gained `clampItemToWorldWalls(item)`, called after every open-water horizontal integration step (Food/Coin/Science/Waste) — the world's left/right edges now act as hard walls (zeroing outward `vx` and clamping position) so nothing can drift off the sides the way items used to be able to drift out of the old 5x-wider world. `Ambience.js`'s `BUBBLE_COUNT`/`SEAWEED_COUNT` were both cut by the same ~0.375 ratio the world itself shrank by (45→18, 48→18) so background density-per-px-of-width reads the same as before instead of suddenly cramming the original counts into a much narrower column.
- **Demolish tool grays out with no buildings placed; Merge tool grays out until merging or hybrids are unlocked** — per direct request. `Grid.js` gained `hasAnyBuildingPlaced(state)` (scans every seabed grid cell for a non-empty tile); `UI.js` gained `isDemolishToolAvailable(state)` (wraps it) and `isMergeToolAvailable(state)` (`state.level.upgrades.fishMergingUnlocked || labUpgradesPurchased.includes(GENE_SPLICING_LAB_ID)`). `selectTool` now refuses to select either tool while unavailable; `updateToolbar` (called every frame from `updateHUD`, not just on selection) computes both availability flags first, sets `.disabled` on the two bottom-tool-bar buttons, and auto-falls the selected tool back to Food if it just became unavailable — all before the `.selected` class pass, so a mid-game state change (e.g. demolishing your last building) can't leave a disabled-but-still-marked-selected button. `.tool-btn:disabled` (`style.css`) dims/desaturates and drops the pointer cursor.
- **Closing the Shop while a fish is selected deselects it back to Food** — per direct request. `UI.js`'s `updateShopCollapse` now checks `state.ui.shopCollapsed && state.ui.selectedTool.startsWith('fish:')` at its very start and calls the existing `deselectShopSelection` (added in the prior batch) if so — reused as-is, no new deselect logic needed.
- **All building intake arrows removed — the Collector, Auto-Feeder, and (new) Turrets now suck in any eligible item that touches them, from any side, instead of requiring a specific approach angle; Collector/Auto-Feeder output defaults to straight up.** Per direct request, "the arrows on the buildings are impossible to see and plan around... let's remove the arrows and the need for a specific input side." `Grid.js`'s `isOnIntakeSide` (an angle/dot-product check against the tile's stored aim direction) is gone entirely, replaced by a plain `isNearBuildingCenter(centerX, centerY, itemX, itemY, radius)` proximity check, shared by the Collector/Auto-Feeder/Turret intake scans in `updateBuildings`. The Auto-Feeder's output point is now a fixed offset straight up from its own center (`centerY - TILE_SIZE * AUTO_FEEDER_PORT_OFFSET_FRACTION`) rather than derived from a per-instance aim angle; `rotateBuilding` (the `R` hotkey) is deleted outright, since there's no longer any angle to rotate on a Collector/Auto-Feeder. `renderDirectionIndicator` is now Fan-only — a Fan still needs a real aim direction (it's a directional force emitter, untouched by this change) and keeps its arrow+cone; the Collector/Auto-Feeder's old two-arrow indicator and the `drawArrowhead` helper it used are deleted. `placeTile`'s Collector/Auto-Feeder branches no longer store an `angle` field at all. This directly enabled the Turret family below to reuse the exact same intake pattern with no new mechanism needed.
- **Cleanliness-per-waste-event halved again, 0.5%→0.25%** (`CLEANLINESS_PER_WASTE_EVENT`, `Config.js`) — per direct request, continuing the same slow-burn direction the constant's prior 4%→0.5% cut already established.
- **Fish production tuning pass, three numbers at once:** every `SPECIES` row's `hungerRate` ×0.75 (25% slower — "get hungrier 25% slower") and every FEEDER-producing row's `dropInterval` (money) ÷0.9 (10% slower — "produce money... 10% slower"), both across all 18 rows (base feeders, 3 utility species, 9 hybrids) per direct request; `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` ÷0.7 (≈30% slower, 27778→39683ms — "produce... waste 30% slower").
- **A pre-existing bug caught and fixed while doing this pass:** `UI.js`'s `fishEconomyStatsHtml` gated the Money stat display on `s.dropType === 'coin'`, which only matches the 3 base feeders — every FEEDER-tagged hybrid (whose `dropType` field actually describes its *secondary* resource, e.g. `'waste_cleared'` for Scrub Guppy) was silently missing its Money stat in the shop/Lab preview even though it genuinely earns coins via the same `dropInterval` mechanism. Root-caused by re-reading `Entities.js`'s `updateFish`, where the real money-producing branch resolves to `behavior.includes('FEEDER')` — fixed the UI check to match.
- **The Shop toggle button bounces until it's opened for the first time** — per direct request, "at the beginning, before the shop has been opened, have it bounce until it's opened for the first time." `Levels.js` gained `tutorialFlags.firstShopOpened` (false at level start); `UI.js`'s new `scheduleShopButtonReminder(state)` is a self-terminating `setTimeout` chain (same shape as the existing `scheduleNotificationReminder`) that replays the shop toggle button's `.bounce-play` animation every random 3-6s until the flag flips true, checked both before firing and before rescheduling so it stops on its own; called once from `main.js`'s `initStartScreen` callback (alongside `triggerSplash()`), right when the player actually starts the game. `toggleShopCollapse` sets the flag the moment the shop is opened for the first time.
- **A Fan's ghost cone no longer shows during the pre-placement hover phase, only once its cell is actually armed (click 1)** — per direct request, "it's visually confusing to have the cone moving around while trying to choose the fan location." `Grid.js`'s `renderBuildGhost` gained a `showCone = true` param, narrowing when `renderDirectionIndicator` actually draws anything; `main.js`'s plain-hover build-ghost render branch (before `isFanAimingActive()` is even true) now explicitly passes `false`. The two-click aim flow itself (click 1 arms the cell, click 2 confirms the angle) is completely unchanged — only the hover-before-click-1 cone visibility changed.
- **Fish Speed stat added to the shop preview and Lab purchase modal** — per direct request. `UI.js`'s shared `fishEconomyStatsHtml(speciesId)` gained a `🏊 Speed: <b>${Math.round(s.swimSpeed * FISH_SPEED_MULTIPLIER)}px/s</b>` line, shown for every species (unlike the Money/Waste stats, which are behavior-gated) since every fish swims.
- **The Alien Invasion system — the big one.** Per direct request: aliens spawn in waves every 3-5 minutes (`ALIEN_WAVE_INTERVAL_MIN_MS`/`_MAX_MS`), starting at 2-3 aliens with 20-30 HP each and ramping linearly over `ALIEN_WAVE_DIFFICULTY_RAMP_WAVES` (10) waves up to 10-15 aliens with 60-100 HP, then plateauing. Two chat-log warnings fire at 60s and 30s before a wave (`ALIEN_WARNING_MS_1`/`_2`, `ALIEN_WARNING_MESSAGE_1`/`_2`); the final 10 seconds (`ALIEN_COUNTDOWN_START_MS`) hand off to a real on-screen countdown banner (`#alien-countdown`, top-center, pulsing) instead of more chat spam — `UI.js`'s `updateAlienCountdown`, called every frame from `updateHUD`, shows/hides it and ceils the remaining `alienNextWaveAtMs - elapsed` gap into a whole-seconds readout.
  - **Module split, chosen deliberately to avoid a circular import:** `Systems.js` (whose own header comment had reserved "wave timers" since Phase 1) owns wave *timing* — `updateAlienWaves`, called every tick from the existing `updateStoryTriggers` — deciding WHEN a wave starts, the difficulty-ramp math, the two warning notifications, and pushing plain `{ x, y, hp, openAtMs, spawned, spawnedAtMs }` portal records into `state.level.alienPortals` (`spawnAlienWave`, staggering each portal's `openAtMs` by `ALIEN_PORTAL_STAGGER_MS`). `Entities.js` (whose own original header already documented itself as owning "Fish, `Alien`, Food, Item") owns the actual entity: `createAlien`/`updateAlien` (AI, poop, hp-based removal), and a new `updateAlienPortals`, called from `updateEntities`, which is the one place a due portal actually becomes a real `createAlien(...)` entity — pushed into `state.level.entities` once `elapsed >= portal.openAtMs + ALIEN_PORTAL_OPEN_MS`, with the portal itself cleaned up `ALIEN_PORTAL_CLOSE_MS` after that. This split means Systems.js never needs to import Entities.js's `createAlien` at all — it only ever writes plain data.
  - **Alien AI, "kinda dumb" predator/prey by design:** `updateAlien`'s wander cycle (`ALIEN_WANDER_INTERVAL_MIN_S`/`_MAX_S`, same shape as `Entities.js`'s existing fish `wander()`) picks a new heading each cycle — with `ALIEN_CHASE_CHANCE` (50%) odds of looking for the nearest fish within `ALIEN_AWARENESS_RADIUS` (260px) and biasing toward it by a randomized angle offset (never a dead-on lock), otherwise a fully random heading. Fish got the mirror-image behavior: `wander()` gained an optional `nearbyAlien` param — with `ALIEN_FLEE_CHANCE` (65%) odds of biasing away from it instead, same randomized-offset, never-strict-retreat shape. An alien can't swim into the seabed city any more than a fish can (`SEABED_FLOOR_Y` clamp, same rule, same reasoning — Fans are the intended way to reach anything down there). Every `ALIEN_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` (1s) a living alien spawns a Waste item at its own position and costs `CLEANLINESS_PER_WASTE_EVENT`, mirroring a fish's own poop timer exactly.
  - **Fish reactions:** a fish within `ALIEN_INCOME_BLOCK_RADIUS` (90px) of a living alien produces no coin at all — checked in `updateFish`'s coin-drop branch only (waste/science/power production are untouched, per direct request's specific wording, "stop... producing money"); the drop-timer cycle still resets rather than holding at the threshold, so a coin doesn't instantly fire the moment the alien wanders off. `fish.alienNearby` (recomputed every tick) drives a continuous gray tint; a separate timed tint (`fish.capBlockedTintRemainingMs`, counting down from `FISH_BLOCKED_TINT_MS` = 1s) fires the same visual the moment a coin drop is blocked by the Coin Cap instead, reusing `triggerProductionBlocked`. `FishRenderer.js`'s `drawFish` gained a `grayed` (0-1) param, blended in RGB space via a new `mixRgb` helper (kept separate from the existing hex-string `mixColor` specifically so layering a second tint on top of the sickness tint's own `rgb(...)` output never gets re-parsed as if it were a hex color) toward a flat `ALIEN_BLOCKED_GRAY`; `main.js`'s fish-render call computes `grayed = (fish.alienNearby || fish.capBlockedTintRemainingMs > 0) ? 0.55 : 0`.
  - **Three Turret buildings — Waste, Electric, Advanced — reusing the exact family-stacking shop UI the Fan/Processor/Auto-Feeder families already have.** `Config.js`'s `TURRET_STATS` gives exact per-tier fire rate/damage/power cost (Waste: 1.5/sec, 4 dmg, free ammo from Waste — `WASTE_TURRET_SHOTS_PER_WASTE` = 10 shots per Waste item consumed, `WASTE_TURRET_MAX_WASTE` = 5 held, rendered as ammo dots via `renderTurretAmmoDots`, mirroring the Auto-Feeder's own load-dots; Electric: 2/sec, 6 dmg, 2mw/shot; Advanced: 3/sec, 8 dmg, 3mw/shot) at the exact numbers requested. The Waste Turret is `unlockedByDefault: true`, available from the very start alongside Platform per direct request ("give the waste turret at the very beginning of the game with the platforms"); Electric/Advanced Turret are new Science Lab tree nodes (`electric_turret` requires `eel`, `advanced_turret` requires `electric_turret`) — "restricted behind the same walls as the collectors and auto-feeders" (Platform-anchoring, same as every other building — no special-casing needed, `canPlaceTile`'s `isAnchored` check already applies uniformly). `Grid.js`'s `updateBuildings` gained a Turret branch (inserted carefully between the Collector's own `continue` and the Auto-Feeder's early-exit check — an early draft nearly placed it AFTER that early-exit, which would have made it completely unreachable, caught and fixed before shipping): each tick it tops up Waste-Turret ammo from any Waste within `TURRET_INTAKE_RADIUS` (omnidirectional, same `isNearBuildingCenter` intake pattern as the Collector/Auto-Feeder above), then — cooldown permitting and ammo/power-agnostic for Electric/Advanced (same "tracked but not yet gated on availability" precedent every other Electric building has always had) — finds the nearest living alien within `TURRET_RANGE` (480px, all 3 tiers) and applies `turretStats.damage` directly to `alien.hp`. Turret firing is deferred-removal, same pattern the Processor's own consumption already established: `Grid.js` mutates `alien.hp` mid-tick (this runs before the entities filter loop), and the actual hp≤0 removal happens naturally in `updateAlien`'s own `return false`, later in the same tick.
  - **Alien portals — animated open/hold/close, staggered per alien within a wave.** Purely a `main.js` render concern (`state.level.alienPortals` is plain data, not entities) — a radial-gradient purple burst that ramps in over `ALIEN_PORTAL_OPEN_MS` (900ms), then back out over `ALIEN_PORTAL_CLOSE_MS` (700ms) once its alien has actually spawned, sized by `ALIEN_PORTAL_RADIUS` (26px).
  - **Alien rendering + health bars:** a flat dark-purple (`ALIEN_COLOR`) circle with two small red "eyes" so it doesn't read as a plain blob, plus a black-backed red health bar (`ALIEN_HEALTH_BAR_WIDTH`/`_HEIGHT`) above its head, filled by `alien.hp / alien.maxHp`. Rendered in `main.js`'s `render()` as its own pass, right after the fish loop — which itself needed a one-line fix (`if (fish.type !== 'fish') continue;`) since `state.level.entities` now holds aliens too and the fish loop previously assumed every entity in that array was a fish (`SPECIES[fish.speciesId]` would otherwise throw on an alien).
  - **Click damage:** clicking a living alien (within `ALIEN_RADIUS` of the click point) does `ALIEN_CLICK_DAMAGE` (1) — checked first in `main.js`'s canvas click handler, ahead of every tool-specific branch, same "always works regardless of selected tool" precedent coin-banking already has.
  - **A one-time combat tip** (`ALIEN_FIRST_WAVE_TIP_MESSAGE`) posts to the notification ticker the moment the very first wave's portals are actually created (`Systems.js`'s `spawnAlienWave`, gated on a new `tutorialFlags.firstAlienWaveTipShown`), explaining click-damage and turrets before the player has to guess.
  - **A new debug cheat, `Y`:** forces the next alien wave to start immediately (`state.level.alienNextWaveAtMs = state.level.elapsed`) — for testing the whole wave/portal/alien lifecycle without waiting out a real 3-5 minute gap, same "skip the grind" precedent `N`/`M`/`U` already established.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): world-width camera centering held `camera.x` completely immovable against ArrowLeft/KeyD/horizontal-wheel-scroll while vertical panning still worked, with a screenshot confirming sane ambience density and normal build/fish placement; Demolish/Merge correctly grayed out with nothing built/nothing merge-unlocked and un-grayed the instant that changed; force-cracking the Mound to Tier 3 and placing a Platform + an (unlocked-via-cheat) Advanced Turret produced zero errors, and the debug overlay confirmed `Tile under cursor: turret_advanced`; the `Y` cheat immediately produced a wave (`Entities` count rising from 0 to 2, then to 3 as a third portal's stagger delay elapsed — confirmed as correct staggered-spawn timing, not a bug, by working through the exact `ALIEN_PORTAL_STAGGER_MS`/`ALIEN_PORTAL_OPEN_MS` math against the observed timestamps), with the first-wave tip notification visibly posted and a screenshot showing 3 correctly-rendered aliens (dark purple, red eyes, health bars) alongside functioning bubbles/seaweed/HUD; a second run spawned 8 grown Guppies near a forced wave and a screenshot clearly showed the 2 guppies nearest the alien rendering visibly grayer/duller than the 6 further away, confirming the proximity-based income-block gray tint; waste items and a falling cleanliness percentage (100%→98%→99% across runs) confirmed alien poop is real; 35 clicks scattered across the water column while aliens were alive produced zero errors (click-damage hit-testing, and fell through harmlessly to other click handlers on a miss); a placed turret coexisting with active aliens for several seconds produced no crashes or NaN state. Not separately verified live: a turret actually landing a kill (aliens have 20-30 HP and the test run's short window/scattered clicks didn't converge fast enough to confirm a kill+removal in this pass) — the removal path itself (`updateAlien`'s `hp <= 0` → `return false` from the entities filter) is the same well-established pattern every other entity/item removal in this codebase already uses, and turret damage application was confirmed wired (no errors from `Grid.js` mutating `alien.hp` every tick a turret was in range).

**Real bug fix: a runaway alien population was silently tanking the framerate over long sessions.** Reported directly ("after a while the framerates dropped to unplayable levels") the session right after the Alien Invasion system above shipped. Root cause: waves had no ceiling on total simultaneously-alive aliens — an un-cleared wave just stacked on top of the next one — and every living alien poops a Waste item every second forever, with `Grid.js`'s `resolveItemCollisions` comparing every item against every other item each tick (O(n²), ×4 sub-passes, 60/sec); a neglected tank's item count climbing into the hundreds is exactly the kind of cost that goes from invisible to unplayable. Fixed with a new `ALIEN_MAX_ALIVE` (20, `Config.js`) — `Systems.js`'s `spawnAlienWave` now shrinks (or skips) a wave's spawn count to whatever room is left under the cap (counting both living aliens and any of the wave's own not-yet-opened portals, so a burst can't sneak past it), rather than always spawning its full rolled amount. Verified live: forcing 8 back-to-back waves with zero kills (previously capable of producing 100+ aliens) held the entity count exactly at 20 with FPS steady around 56.

**A big follow-up batch — alien waste halved, aliens get a hit-flash/bounce/death animation, the Coin/Science Cap HUD readouts pulse and shake, a Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish economy rebalance, and the background music rewritten a third time** — all per direct request, landing together.
- **`ALIEN_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` halved 1000→2000ms** — "make the aliens drop waste every 2 seconds instead of every second." Also a welcome side effect on top of the `ALIEN_MAX_ALIVE` fix above: it halves the population cap's own worst-case waste-production rate.
- **Aliens flash red and "bounce" when hit, and play a real death animation when killed** — per direct request. `Entities.js`'s `createAlien` gained `hitFlashMs` (0 by default), set to `ALIEN_HIT_FLASH_MS` (220ms) at both places damage is ever applied — `Grid.js`'s Turret branch and `main.js`'s click-damage handler — and ticked back down to 0 every frame in `updateAlien`. `main.js`'s alien render reads it back to (a) blend the body color from `ALIEN_COLOR` toward `ALIEN_HIT_FLASH_COLOR` in RGB space (`lerpRgbToString`, a new small helper — `ALIEN_COLOR` is parsed to an `{r,g,b}` triple once at module scope, `ALIEN_COLOR_RGB`) proportional to how much flash time is left, and (b) apply a scale-punch "bounce" (`1 + ALIEN_HIT_BOUNCE_SCALE * sin(progress * π)`, peaking at the flash's midpoint and settling back to 1x) to the body/eyes only — the health bar stays anchored off the alien's real, unscaled radius so it doesn't jitter. Death gets its own decoupled visual: the instant `updateAlien` finds `hp <= 0`, it pushes a `{ x, y, age: 0 }` record into a new `state.level.alienDeathEffects` array (seeded in `Levels.js`, aged/culled by a new `updateAlienDeathEffects` alongside the alien's own removal from `state.level.entities`) — `main.js` renders each one as an expanding/fading ring plus a 6-point radial particle burst over `ALIEN_DEATH_EFFECT_DURATION_MS` (500ms), fully independent of the (already-removed) alien entity, same "detached particle" pattern `state.level.floatingTexts` already uses for pickup text.
- **The Coin Cap and Science Cap HUD readouts now pulse red continuously at or above 80% of their cap, and shake red every time the live count goes up** — per direct request. `Config.js`'s new `CAP_WARNING_THRESHOLD_FRACTION` (0.8) is checked live in `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` against each readout's own `count/max`, toggling a new `.cap-warning` class (`style.css`, a simple `@keyframes cap-warning-pulse` looping the text color between the pill's default and red); the "shake on increase" reuses the exact same one-shot `.flash-spend` red-shake every other HUD readout already has, triggered by comparing the live count against a new `lastCoinCapCount`/`lastScienceCapCount` (module-level, same pattern as the existing `lastMoney`/`lastCleanliness`). Since both classes can land on the same element at once (near the cap AND the count just rose), a `.cap-warning.flash-spend` compound rule explicitly lists both animations together — without it, plain CSS cascade order would let the continuous pulse's `animation` declaration silently override the one-shot shake's for the brief window they overlap; with it, the one-shot shake plays in full and the still-infinite pulse naturally resumes once it finishes. The pre-existing "blocked by the cap" shake (`state.ui.coinCapFlashPending`) is untouched — a blocked drop never actually raises the count, so it can't double up with the new increase-triggered trigger.
- **Guppy gives 25% more gold; Dartfin produces waste 10% slower than Guppy; Blimpfish produces waste 5% faster** — per direct request ("make the guppies give 25% more gold, so they give more money than the dartfin... dartfin produce waste 10% slower than the guppies, and the blimpfish produce waste 5% faster"). Guppy's `dropValue` (all 3 growth stages, previously a flat 5) is now a flat 6.25 (`5 * 1.25`) — the fractional value is intentional and correct, since the drop-time formula already wraps every drop in `Math.ceil(...)` regardless of stage. Waste production, unlike money, was a single flat `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` shared by every species with no per-species hook — `SPECIES` rows gained an optional `wastePoopIntervalMultiplier` (Guppy omits it, defaulting to 1 via `|| 1` at the read site in `Entities.js`'s `updateFish`; Dartfin gets `1/0.9` — "10% slower" using this codebase's existing "÷(1-x)" convention for that exact phrase; Blimpfish gets `0.95` — "5% faster," the multiply side of the same convention), applied as `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS * (def.wastePoopIntervalMultiplier || 1)` in the fish-poop timer.
- **Background music rewritten a third time, per direct request ("I hate it... more melodic, upbeat and adventurous feeling, with more substance, and no random clicks in the song").** The "random clicks" were almost certainly the previous version's own rhythmic "bounce" pulse — a bare, fast-attack square-wave tick, literally described in that version's own code comment as exactly that — which is gone entirely this time, not just quieted; nothing in the new track is a bare percussive blip, only tuned notes on smooth 'sine'/'triangle' oscillators (a square wave's sharp edges at a fast attack are what actually read as "clicky"). The real substance upgrade: previous passes were melody-plus-a-single-bass-note built entirely off one pentatonic scale (so nothing could ever clash, since notes were chosen loosely rather than against real harmony); this version is hand-composed (never randomized) over an actual **I-V-vi-IV chord progression** (C-G-Am-F, repeated twice across an 8-bar loop — one of the most common "hopeful/adventurous" progressions in game and pop music) using the FULL C major scale rather than just its pentatonic subset, so the melody can leap along real chord tones for a genuine fanfare contour (bar 1 arpeggiates straight up the C major triad; bar 7 climbs to the loop's highest note, A5, right before the final turnaround). Three independent voices play every bar: the lead melody, a moving "oom-pah" bass (root on beat 1, fifth on beat 3 — real movement, not a static drone), and a soft sustained pad holding the FULL triad (root+third+fifth, three simultaneous oscillators) underneath, which is what actually makes a chord change audible as a harmony shift rather than just the bass note moving. Tempo nudged to 132 BPM. Verified the hand-written melody's math directly in Node (all 8 bars sum to exactly 4 beats each, 32 total, and every scale-degree index used by both the melody and the 4 chord definitions falls within the real 15-note scale array — no silent `undefined`-frequency notes) before ever loading it in a browser; live in Playwright, the loop was run through 2 full boundaries (~29s) with zero console errors, confirming the rescheduling handoff between loops doesn't glitch.

**Guppy bumped again, a real shop-stats bug fixed, the first alien warning goes quiet after wave 1, the Shop/Tank Upgrades panels became half-height fly-out modals flanking the bottom tool-bar with the HUD promoted to a single always-visible copy, and turrets were reworked from a short-range hitscan into a global-range homing-projectile weapon** — a large batch, all per direct request/report.
- **Guppy's coin value bumped another 20%, on top of the earlier 25%** — "make the guppies produce 20% more valuable coins still." `dropValue` (all 3 growth stages) goes from 6.25 → 7.5 (`5 × 1.25 × 1.2`); Guppy's effective income now clearly beats Dartfin's at every stage (adult: $23.22/min vs Dartfin's $19.19/min, confirmed live).
- **Real bug fix: the shop's Waste/min stat showed the exact same number for every species**, ignoring the per-species `wastePoopIntervalMultiplier` added in the previous batch (Dartfin 10% slower, Blimpfish 5% faster than Guppy) — `UI.js`'s `fishEconomyStatsHtml` was still reading the flat global `WASTE_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` directly instead of scaling it by `s.wastePoopIntervalMultiplier || 1`, the same formula `Entities.js`'s actual poop timer already used correctly; only this display-side stat had drifted out of sync. Verified live: Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish now show 1.51/1.36/1.59 waste-per-min respectively, matching the intended ±10%/5% deltas exactly.
- **The first-ever "Something's stirring out past the reef..." alien warning no longer repeats on every wave** — per direct request ("it doesn't need to say that every time"). `Systems.js`'s `updateAlienWaves` still flips the per-wave `alienWarning1Shown` flag at the 60s mark every wave (unchanged — it's what stops the check from re-firing every tick within one wave cycle), but the actual `pushNotification` call is now additionally gated on a new one-time `tutorialFlags.firstAlienWarning1Shown` (`Levels.js`), so only the very first wave, ever, actually posts that line; every wave after goes quiet at that mark. The second warning ("Uh oh, I'm reading movement...") and the countdown banner are untouched — still fire every wave.
- **Shop and Tank Upgrades are now fly-out modals half the height of before, anchored to their own toggle icon, which moved to flank the bottom tool-bar** (Shop on the left, Tank Upgrades on the right) instead of stacking top-right — per direct request. `index.html`'s `#panel-toggle-cluster` is gone; `#shop-collapse-btn`/`#tank-collapse-btn` now live inside a new `#bottom-bar-row` alongside `#bottom-tool-bar` (Food/Demolish/Merge), which itself is no longer independently `position:fixed` — just a flex child of the row now. `#shop-panel`/`#tank-panel` swapped their old `top:14;right:14;bottom:14` full-height docking for `height: calc((100vh - 28px) / 2)` (exactly half the old slot) anchored above their own icon (`left:14`/`right:14`, `bottom:96px` to clear the toolbar row) with a `transform-origin` pinned to the corner nearest that icon — same scale+opacity "grow from/shrink into an anchor point" mechanic `Mound.js`'s own popup (`#mound-menu`) already established, replacing the old `width:0` collapse. Deliberately NOT given a dimmed/click-catching backdrop the way the Mound/Lab popups have — unlike those rare, deliberate visits, the Shop stays open WHILE actively playing (banking coins, watching fish) far more often, so a full-screen click-eater would break that; closing still only happens via the toggle button, its hotkey, or opening the other panel, unchanged. No JS changes were needed for the open/close mechanism itself — it's still the same `classList.toggle('collapsed', ...)` UI.js already had, just riding new CSS underneath it.
- **The Shop/Tank Upgrades panels no longer carry their own duplicate HUD (`#shop-hud`/`#tank-hud` and every `#shop-money`/`#tank-coin-cap`/etc. readout) — `#hud` is the one copy now, and it's never hidden any more, top-right, at all times, panels open or not.** Per direct request ("remove the hud from the shop and tank upgrades, and keep it in the top right of the screen always"). `UI.js`'s `updateHudVisibility`/`stripHudFlashClasses` and the `visibleHudEl` redirect helper are gone entirely — every flash/update call (`flashMoneyInsufficient`, the money/cleanliness/coin-cap/science-cap pickup/spend flashes) now targets `els.money`/`els.cleanliness`/`els.coinCap`/`els.scienceCap`/`els.power` directly, no more "pick whichever copy is visible" branching needed since there's only one. `#hud` moved from `top:14;right:100px` (the old offset that cleared the top-right toggle-button cluster) to a plain `top:14;right:14` now that the toggle buttons live at the bottom instead.
- **Turrets reworked from a short-range instant-hit weapon into a global-range homing-projectile weapon, fixing a real report** ("the waste turrets don't currently shoot at the aliens unless they are super close, and then there's no visual bullet"). Root cause: the old `TURRET_RANGE` (480px) was a hard cutoff on the auto-target search, but a seabed-anchored turret can easily be 800+ px of pure vertical distance from an alien loitering up in the water column (`SEABED_FLOOR_Y` to `FISH_MIN_Y` alone spans more than that) — the turret was working exactly as coded, the range itself was just too small relative to the tank, and there was never any projectile to render, just an instant, invisible hp deduction. Per direct request ("make it so turrets have global range, but have projectiles they shoot that have to collide with the aliens before it does damage (it should never miss)"): `TURRET_RANGE` is deleted outright — `Grid.js`'s target search in `updateBuildings` now has no distance cutoff at all, always finds the nearest living alien anywhere in the level. Firing no longer mutates `alien.hp` directly; it pushes `{ x, y, targetId, damage }` into a new `turretShots` array, returned alongside the existing `foodSpawnPoints`/`wasteSpawnPoints` (the same "Grid.js hands back data, the real owner constructs it" split already used for Food/Waste). `Entities.js`'s new `createTurretProjectile`/`updateTurretProjectiles` turn each shot into a real, independently-ticked projectile that HOMES on its target's live position every tick (`TURRET_PROJECTILE_SPEED`, 900px/sec) — guaranteeing it "never misses" even if the alien moves, since it keeps closing distance to wherever the target actually is rather than flying a fixed straight line — and only applies damage (plus the hit-flash) once it actually arrives (within `TURRET_PROJECTILE_HIT_RADIUS`, 14px); if the target's already dead by then (a second turret or a click got there first), the shot just fizzles with no damage. `main.js` renders each one as a bright yellow bolt (`TURRET_PROJECTILE_COLOR`) with a short fading motion trail back toward its origin. The turret's own building-stats display (`UI.js`) now reads "📏 Range: Global" instead of a px figure.
- **A living alien's clickable radius is now 50% larger than its drawn radius** — per direct request ("so they are easier to click"), the exact same "hit-test bigger than the visual" pattern `COIN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER` already established for coins. New `ALIEN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER` (1.5); `main.js`'s click-damage hit-test now checks `ALIEN_RADIUS * ALIEN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER` instead of the bare radius — purely a hit-test change, the drawn/collision size is untouched.
