- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): the shop/tank toggle buttons confirmed flanking the tool-bar in screen-space (shop.x < toolbar.x < tank.x); the Shop panel's live bounding box measured exactly 461px tall (`(950-28)/2`, matching the test viewport) positioned bottom-left with its bottom edge clearing the tool-bar row, and `#shop-hud`/`#tank-hud` confirmed absent from the DOM entirely; the HUD stayed visible and correctly positioned with either panel open (screenshots of both states); an Advanced Turret placed on a platform strip, with two forced-wave aliens spawned far above it in open water, was confirmed firing continuously via multiple simultaneous yellow projectile bolts visibly in flight toward them in a screenshot, and both aliens were dead (`Entities: 0`) within 4 seconds — a global-range engagement that would have been completely unreachable under the old 480px cutoff. One real testing lesson from this pass, worth keeping: the auto-fit camera zoom is NOT exactly 1x, so a fixed screen-pixel offset (e.g. "32px = one tile") doesn't reliably land in an adjacent grid column — placement tests should drag across a wide screen-pixel range (or retry at several candidate offsets) rather than assuming a specific px-per-tile ratio.

**Follow-up correction: the Shop/Tank Upgrades fly-out panels now genuinely anchor to their own toggle icon instead of the screen edge, Shop grew another 10% taller, and a real scrollbar-clipping bug got fixed** — per direct report/request, landing right after the batch above.
- **Panels now position relative to their real button, not a hardcoded screen-edge offset.** The previous pass's `#shop-panel { left: 14px }` / `#tank-panel { right: 14px }` (`position: fixed`) was only correct because it happened to match where the toggle buttons used to live (a top-right stacked cluster, pinned to the screen edge) — once those buttons moved into the CENTERED `#bottom-bar-row` for the earlier "move Shop/Tank to flank the tool-bar" request, the panels stayed pinned to the literal screen edge while their buttons sat well inland, so a panel no longer opened anywhere near its own icon. Fixed properly this time by making the panel a true positioned CHILD of the same element the button lives in, not a sibling with an assumed offset: `index.html` gained `#shop-anchor`/`#tank-anchor` (`position: relative` wrappers, each holding its toggle button AND — physically relocated in the markup — its panel), and `#shop-panel`/`#tank-panel` switched from `position: fixed` to `position: absolute; bottom: calc(100% + 10px); left: 0` (or `right: 0` for tank) — which tracks the button's actual rendered position automatically, with zero JS needed, and stays correct regardless of how the row centers itself at any viewport width.
- **Shop panel height +10% on top of the earlier half-height** — "made the shop 10% taller." `#shop-panel`'s height is now `calc((100vh - 28px) / 2 * 1.1)`; `#tank-panel` stays at the plain half-height, unchanged (only Shop was named in the request).
- **Real bug fix: the scrollbar's top and bottom were visually clipped by the panel's own rounded corners.** Root cause: `#shop-panel`/`#tank-panel` had `overflow-y: auto` AND `border-radius: 26px` on the very same element — a browser clips ALL overflow content (the scrollbar included) to an element's own rounded-corner shape, so the scrollbar's track/thumb got cut off right where the curve intrudes near the very top and bottom edges. Fixed by moving the actual scrolling to a new INNER wrapper (`#shop-panel-scroll`/`#tank-panel-scroll`, `position: absolute; inset: 14px` — the same 14px the old padding used, so the visible content layout is pixel-identical to before) that has no border-radius of its own — a plain rectangle safely inset within the curve, so its scrollbar can never be clipped by it. This also satisfies the "make the scrollbar shorter" half of the same report for free: the inner box's height is naturally the panel's own height minus 28px (14px top + 14px bottom inset), so the scrollbar stops well short of the card's real top/bottom edges instead of running the full height. `#shop-panel`/`#tank-panel` themselves dropped `overflow`/`padding`/the scrollbar-color rules entirely (moved onto the new inner wrapper) and kept only the outer visual styling (background/border/radius/shadow/position/size/transition).
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): the Shop panel's live bounding box now shares its exact left edge with the Shop button's left edge (both `x: 578`), and its height measured `507.09px` (`461 × 1.1`, matching the +10% exactly); the Tank panel's right edge exactly matched the Tank button's right edge; a direct DOM check confirmed `#tank-panel-scroll` sits inset 16px from `#tank-panel`'s own box on all four sides (14px CSS inset + the panel's 2px border), carries `border-radius: 0px` (vs. the outer panel's `26px`), and genuinely overflows (`scrollHeight: 887` vs `clientHeight: 429`) — confirming the scrollbar now belongs to a plain, un-rounded, inset rectangle that structurally can't be clipped by the outer curve. Repeated shop/tank open-close cycling (hotkeys and buttons, several times each) plus a fish purchase all continued to work with zero console errors, confirming the markup relocation didn't break anything relying on those element IDs.

**The upper/lower city boundary softened, the bottom-buffer fade became a hard break, and the Coin Cap got a real "coin on fire, disintegrating" effect plus a one-time 80%-full chat warning** — a smaller visual/feedback batch, all per direct request.
- **The Rocky Shelf reads as a much gentler boundary now** — "not quite so dramatic between the upper city and lower city sections." `Grid.js`'s `ROCK_SHELF_JAG_PX` (the jagged underside's random undulation) was halved, 14→7; the shelf's own body gradient (`#8a7256`→`#6b5a42`→`#3c3226`, a stark light-tan-to-near-black plunge) softened to `#77644c`→`#5f5140`→`#463a2c`, sitting closer to both the city fill above and the underground fill below rather than jumping hard between them; its top-edge highlight strip dimmed (`#a68a68`→`#8f7a5e`) and thinned (4px→3px zoom-scaled); its underside shadow stroke lightened (`rgba(20,14,10,0.55)`→`0.32`) and thinned. The underground biome's own base fill lightened too (`#352a1f`→`#3d3122`), still visibly darker/cooler than the city fill above it, just a smaller jump. Purely a palette/amplitude pass — the geometry/rendering technique (fixed jittered points generated once at module load, a flat top + jagged bottom, no `ctx.filter`) is unchanged.
- **The camera bottom-buffer strip (pure background behind the fixed tool-bar) no longer fades to black — it's a hard break now.** Per direct request ("the gradient to black that currently transitions into the toolbar at the bottom should be changed to a hard break from the bottom tank to the toolbar"). `Grid.js`'s `renderCameraBottomBuffer` dropped its `createLinearGradient` entirely in favor of a flat `#000000` fill across the same buffer span — the underground/city fill directly above it now stops dead at `WORLD_H` and solid black starts immediately, rather than gradually darkening across the buffer's own height.
- **A blocked coin drop now shows a genuine "coin on fire, disintegrating" effect instead of the plain "🫧" bubble** — per direct request; a blocked SCIENCE brew still uses the original bubble (only "the fish can't spawn coins" was named). `triggerProductionBlocked` (`Entities.js`) branches on `resource`: `'coin'` pushes a `{ x, y, age: 0 }` record into a new `state.level.coinBlockedEffects` array (seeded in `Levels.js`, aged/culled by a new `updateCoinBlockedEffects` — same "detached particle, independent of the fish" pattern `alienDeathEffects` already established) instead of the old floatingText push. `main.js` renders each one over `COIN_BLOCKED_EFFECT_DURATION_MS` (800ms, `Config.js`): a shrinking gold coin disc, 2-3 flickering flame licks fanned above it (radial gradients, no `ctx.filter`, animated via `Math.sin(age * freq)` for a genuine flicker rather than a static glow), and a handful of dark ember/ash flecks drifting up and outward from the coin as `t` advances — reading as the coin visibly burning down and crumbling apart, all fading together as it goes.
- **A one-time chat notification the first time the Coin Cap reaches 80% full** — per direct request ("make it more obvious when the coin limit is reached... add in a chat hud the first time the coin max gets to 80%"). `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` (which already computes the live count/max ratio for the existing `.cap-warning` pulse) now also checks a new one-time `tutorialFlags.firstCoinCapWarningShown` (`Levels.js`) the moment that ratio first crosses `CAP_WARNING_THRESHOLD_FRACTION`, pushing `` `Pick up those coins, you can only have ${coinCapMax} on screen at once` `` — the live cap value interpolated in directly, so the number stays correct even after a Coin Capacity Tank Upgrade raises it — onto the notification ticker via the same inline push-then-cap pattern every other notification writer in this codebase already uses.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): a screenshot panned to the bottom of the tank showed the Rocky Shelf as a noticeably gentler, softer-colored undulating line (vs. the old sharp jagged/high-contrast version) and the camera buffer strip cutting to flat solid black immediately below it with no visible fade; forcing 12 grown fish to overflow a 10-coin cap produced the exact expected notification text — `"Pick up those coins, you can only have 10 on screen at once"` — the moment the HUD's `#hud-coin-cap` picked up its `cap-warning` class, and continued running (Items: 22, cleanliness dropping from waste) with zero errors, confirming `triggerProductionBlocked`'s new coin-vs-science branch and the new effect array's push/age/cull/render cycle all execute cleanly under sustained real load. The fire-coin effect's own render code wasn't separately caught mid-animation in a screenshot (an 800ms window is hard to time deterministically against a headless run) — accepted on code-review confidence instead, since it reuses the exact same canvas techniques (radial gradients, fixed-angle particle bursts, `worldToScreen`-based positioning) already proven correct and error-free by the alien death-burst effect earlier in this doc.

**A scroll-down hint for new players, the Shop bounces twice as often, and the bottom-buffer strip shrunk and got a real polish pass with a new boundary line** — a smaller batch, all per direct request.
- **A row of bouncing down-arrows nudges the player to pan down, 10 seconds into a fresh game, until they actually do** — "after 10 seconds, if the player hasn't scrolled down yet, have arrows show up along the bottom of the screen and bounce until the player scrolls at least one scroll downward." New `#scroll-hint` (`index.html`, 5 `▼` spans) sits just above `#bottom-bar-row`, `pointer-events: none` so it can never intercept a click, each arrow bouncing on a staggered delay for a small rolling-wave feel. `UI.js`'s new `updateScrollHint` (called every frame from `updateHUD`) does double duty: it detects "has the player scrolled down at all" by checking `state.camera.y > 0` — a reliable one-line signal since nothing but deliberate vertical-pan input ever moves `camera.y` away from the `0` `loadLevel` seeds it at (confirmed by re-reading Engine.js's `updateCamera`), so no need to hook into Engine.js's own input handling, which deliberately has no knowledge of tutorial state — and once true, latches it into a new one-time `tutorialFlags.hasScrolledDown` (`Levels.js`); the hint shows whenever `state.level.elapsed >= 10000` and that flag is still false.
- **The Shop toggle button now bounces twice as often before it's first opened** — per direct request. `UI.js`'s `SHOP_BUTTON_REMINDER_MIN_MS`/`_MAX_MS` (the random-interval `setTimeout` chain `scheduleShopButtonReminder` already used) halved from 3000/6000 to 1500/3000.
- **The bottom-buffer strip (background behind the fixed tool-bar) shrunk and got a real polish pass, plus a new flat boundary line marking the city's actual bottom edge.** Per direct request: "shrink the size of the black toolbar section at the bottom so it's just slightly bigger than the toolbar. It's too tall right now," "add in a rock line to the bottom of the city like the transition from the tank to the city (not like [the jagged Rocky Shelf])," and "rework the black toolbar to look like a polished toolbar area thats not a flat solid color... match the aesthetic of the rest of the game." `Config.js`'s `CAMERA_BOTTOM_BUFFER_PX` cut 156→100 — this time NOT compensated by moving tiles into `WORLD_TILES_H` (unlike an earlier cut, which preserved total scroll depth on purpose) since this pass is purely "make the buffer shorter," not "reclaim buildable space" — 100px comfortably clears `#bottom-bar-row`'s own ~86px on-screen footprint with a small margin, matching "just slightly bigger than the toolbar." `Grid.js`'s `renderCameraBottomBuffer` gained a flat `#4a3d2e` highlight strip exactly at `WORLD_H` (the same "solid fill + thin lighter strip" boundary language `renderSeabedGrid`'s own `SEABED_ROW_START` line already uses — flat, deliberately NOT jagged like the Rocky Shelf, per the explicit "not like" in the request) and its own fill changed from a flat `#000000` rectangle to a real dark linear gradient (`#251d15` → `#0a0806`) plus the same speckle texture (`getUndergroundTexturePattern`, reused) every other seabed/underground fill in this file already carries — still a hard, un-blended seam at the boundary (the new highlight strip IS that seam), just a properly polished fill within the buffer's own span instead of a flat, textureless black rectangle.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): `#scroll-hint` stayed hidden through the first ~10 seconds of a fresh game, became visible (class toggled, screenshot confirming 5 bouncing white arrows clearly readable above the tool-bar) once `state.level.elapsed` crossed 10000ms with no scrolling, and hid itself immediately on the very next frame after a single `ArrowDown` press — then stayed hidden through further waiting, confirming the one-time latch. A zoomed screenshot of the tank's true bottom (camera panned to its new, shorter max scroll depth) showed the Rocky Shelf's softened underside, the underground fill, a visible boundary, and a distinctly darker, genuinely textured/gradient buffer fill below it — not a flat black block — confirming the rework rendered correctly.

**A gentler hunger SFX, a real "cap is full" bounce distinct from the blocked-production shake, and a genuinely more polished bottom-buffer strip** — a follow-up batch, all per direct report/request.
- **The hunger "!!"-stage SFX swapped from a harsh sawtooth growl to a soft triangle-wave murmur** — per direct report ("too aggressive of a sound when the fish is starving and I'm always looking around for what I did wrong"). `Sound.js`'s `playHunger` kept its same two-note descending shape but switched `'sawtooth'` (a buzzy, harmonic-rich waveform) to `'triangle'` (smooth, no buzz) and dropped the gain (0.1/0.09 → 0.06/0.05) — a fish getting hungry is routine, not a crisis, and should read that way; `playFishDeath` (starvation itself) is still the one that should sound like something actually went wrong.
- **The Coin/Science Cap HUD readouts now bounce like a real notification badge once they're genuinely FULL (100%), not just past the 80% warning threshold** — per direct request ("instead of just pulsing red, have it bounce as well, so it looks like a notification... keep this visually distinct from when it shakes red when the fish can't produce more coin/science"). `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` now also toggles a new `.cap-full` class the moment `count >= max` (alongside the existing `.cap-warning` at 80%+); `style.css`'s new `cap-full-bounce` keyframe is a pure `transform: scale()` "pop" — deliberately never touching translate/rotate, since `flash-spend`'s shake already owns that combination for a completely different signal (a blocked production attempt just happened, not "the cap is full" itself). All three classes (`cap-warning`, `cap-full`, `flash-spend`) can land on the same element at once — a full cap being repeatedly re-blocked — so a three-way compound selector explicitly lists all three animations together, same "list every active animation explicitly so cascade order can't silently drop one" fix the two-way version already established; confirmed live that this exact three-class combination fires correctly during real gameplay.
- **The bottom-buffer strip actually reads as polished now, not "mostly a flat black area"** — per direct follow-up report that the previous pass's plain 2-stop gradient still wasn't enough. `Config.js`'s `CAMERA_BOTTOM_BUFFER_PX` bumped 100→105 (+5%, "make the toolbar area at the bottom 5% taller"). `Grid.js`'s `renderCameraBottomBuffer` gained real visual structure on top of the existing hard-edge boundary line: a richer 3-stop gradient (a genuine visible mid-tone band, not just a top-to-bottom fade), a row of evenly-spaced machined rivets just below the boundary line — deliberately regular/evenly-spaced rather than randomly jittered like the organic Rocky Shelf, so it reads as a deliberately engineered panel — each with its own tiny lit/shadowed bevel, a soft warm radial glow centered horizontally (suggesting ambient light bleeding up from the toolbar's own equipment, and giving the DOM toolbar's warm pink palette something to visually connect to instead of sitting on a cold black rectangle), and a second, fainter seam line partway down for structure beyond a single gradient.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): forcing a coin swarm to overflow a 10-coin cap produced the live class string `cap-warning cap-full flash-spend` together on `#hud-coin-cap` at `10/10` with no errors, confirming the three-way composition actually fires during real gameplay (the 80%-only intermediate state wasn't separately caught by this particular test's polling interval, but the threshold logic itself is a plain, directly-readable `>= 0.8` vs `>= 1.0` comparison, not something that needed a live catch to trust); a zoomed screenshot at the tank's new max scroll depth clearly showed the row of small dark rivet dots along the boundary line and a warm amber glow radiating from behind the bottom tool-bar, a clear step up from the previous flat/plain-gradient version.

**Money moved back into the Shop panel inline with its title, Tank Points did the same, Fish Merging moved to the top of Tank Upgrades, Build/Demolish tools default to Food while the cursor is up in open water, a cinematic first-alien intro (forced single alien, full-screen spotlight, forced pause until clicked), the alien got a real body/health-bar rendering pass, and a real performance bug (unbounded alien-poop Waste growth) got fixed** — a large batch, all per direct request.
- **`#shop-money`/repositioned `#tank-points-display`:** a live money readout is back in the Shop panel — per direct request "the only thing from the hud" it should carry — set inline with the "Shop" title via `#shop-header-row`'s existing `justify-content: space-between` (now shared by `#tank-header-row` too). `UI.js`'s `updateHUD` writes the same money text to it every frame, the one exception to "the main `#hud` is the only copy of everything" (see that section's own comment). `#tank-points-display` moved out of its old standalone position above the upgrade list into `#tank-header-row` alongside "Tank Upgrades," inline with the title the same way.
- **Fish Merging moved to the top of the Tank Upgrades list** — per direct request ("put the Gene splicing at the top of the tank upgrades"); clarified via a direct question that Gene-Splicing itself no longer lives in this panel (it moved into the Science Lab tree in an earlier batch), so Fish Merging — the card actually in this panel that unlocks the same Merge tool — is what moved. `UI.js`'s `buildTankPanel` reorders its final `append(...)` call accordingly.
- **Build/Demolish tools default to Food the instant the cursor is in open water, without ever touching `state.ui.selectedTool` itself** — per direct request, and per a specific correction that this was asked for once already and missed: "if I go to buy a platform, I can click to place the platform, move my cursor up to the upper tank, and immediately click to place food... and then immediately buy another platform... without having to click the platform again." `main.js`'s new `effectiveToolAt(worldY)` returns `'food'` whenever the raw selected tool is `build:*`/`demolish` AND `worldY < SEABED_FLOOR_Y`, else passes the raw tool through unchanged — Fish/Merge tools are untouched (open water is where they're legitimately used). Every consumer that used to read `state.ui.selectedTool` directly for tool-branching now reads this instead: the click handler's build/demolish/food branches, `updateBuildDrag` (which now just bails out early in open water, deferring to the click handler's Food-default path), `updateCanvasCursor`'s icon lookup, and `render()`'s ghost-preview branch. Because the underlying `selectedTool` never actually changes, a building stays armed the whole time — no reselecting needed when the cursor drops back to the seabed. `isFanAimingActive()`'s self-healing Fan-aim-confirm check is deliberately evaluated BEFORE this override in the click handler, so confirming an already-armed Fan's upward aim in open water (completely normal) doesn't get misread as a Food placement.
- **A cinematic first-alien intro:** the very first alien wave, ever, force-spawns exactly one alien (`Systems.js`'s `spawnAlienWave`, special-cased on `state.level.alienWavesSpawned === 0`) instead of the normal ramped roll. The instant it spawns (`Entities.js`'s `updateAlienPortals`, gated by a new one-time `tutorialFlags.firstAlienIntroShown`), `state.level.firstAlienIntroActive` is set and `state.level.firstAlienIntroTargetId` records which alien — `main.js`'s `update()` freezes the ENTIRE sim while this is true (added to the existing `!gameStarted`/`paused`/`gameOver` freeze-check chain), the keydown handler swallows every hotkey including Escape, and `render()` draws a full-screen dimmed overlay with a soft-edged circular hole punched around the alien (`ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'destination-out'` with a radial gradient) plus "An alien! Click it to fight back." text. The click handler's very first check (ahead of even the fish-drag suppression) intercepts every click while this is active: a miss does nothing at all, a hit within `ALIEN_RADIUS * ALIEN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER` (reusing the exact same, already-proven hit-test the normal alien-click-damage handler uses) applies `ALIEN_CLICK_DAMAGE` and clears the flag, resuming the game.
- **Alien body/health-bar rework, "not flat," matching the fish's own rendering style:** `main.js`'s new `drawAlienBody(ctx, x, y, radius, facing, color)` replaces the old flat-circle-plus-eyes draw with an oval body, a trailing tail fin, 3 dorsal spikes, a darker-underside/glossy-highlight shading pass (the same "pop more, less flat" treatment `FishRenderer.js` already uses), a dark rim stroke, and two red "menacing" eyes — oriented by a `facing` param derived from `alien.vx >= 0 ? 1 : -1`, so it visibly faces the direction it's swimming. The health bar switched from two flat rectangles to `ctx.roundRect()`-based pills — a dark track with a soft white border, filled by a `#ff9a8a`→`#e0392b` gradient sized to `hpFrac` — anchored off the alien's UNSCALED base radius (not the hit-flash bounce-scaled one) so it doesn't jitter mid-bounce. The existing hit-flash/bounce logic (color lerp toward `ALIEN_HIT_FLASH_COLOR`, a sine-based scale punch) is untouched, just now applied to the new body-draw call instead of the old inline one.
- **Real performance bug fixed: the game dropping to ~1 FPS once aliens had been alive a while.** Root-caused (not guessed) to aliens pooping a Waste item every `ALIEN_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` forever with no cap, combined with up to `ALIEN_MAX_ALIVE` (20) aliens alive at once and `Grid.js`'s `resolveItemCollisions` being O(n²) per tick (a documented, pre-existing cost) — a neglected tank's total item count could grow unbounded. Fixed the same way `ALIEN_MAX_ALIVE` itself was fixed: a new `Config.js` constant, `WASTE_MAX_ON_SCREEN` (200) — generous enough that hitting it already represents a badly-neglected tank, and deliberately silent/backstage (no HUD feedback, unlike the player-facing Coin/Science caps, since Waste piling up is documented elsewhere as intentional design pressure, not a new mechanic). `Entities.js`'s new `canSpawnMoreWaste(state)` helper gates all 4 Waste-creation call sites (alien poop, fish poop, the Collector/Processor byproduct loop, and the Food-to-Waste rot loop), each item checked individually (not once before a batch) so a burst still respects the cap precisely.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): a comprehensive test covering the full "place a Platform, move to open water, click places Food with the tool still armed, move back, place another Platform with no reselection needed" flow passed every assertion; the first-alien intro's spotlight rendering, forced single-alien wave, and full input-suppression (a miss does nothing, Escape is blocked, the pause menu doesn't open) were all confirmed, and a precise click on the intro alien (using a temporary debug-overlay line exposing its exact world position, added for this one verification pass and reverted immediately after) correctly ended the intro — time scale resumed, the pause menu became reachable again, and the alien was damaged, not killed, all with zero console errors; the reworked alien body/health-bar and the Waste-cap fix were confirmed via later screenshots/live testing in the very next batch below, since a slow/stuck rapid-click stress test interrupted this pass before that verification completed here.

**A circular pause-menu button (replacing the Escape hotkey), Escape now cancels an armed build/demolish/merge tool instead, a "Click to purchase / (Esc) to cancel" legend, a full guided-tutorial engine (game start, first Tank Point, post-first-alien-kill) built on a real CSS clip-path spotlight, Coin Capacity moved to 2nd in Tank Upgrades with a 1-Tank-Point first level, Waste sized off Food/aliens poop half as often, and a click-and-drag Waste mechanic in the city** — the largest batch this project has taken outside the alien-invasion/tutorial-overhaul sessions, all per one direct request; this entry is the consolidated changelog.
- **Pause menu: a circular `#pause-toggle-btn` (☰), top-right below `#hud`, styled identically to `#shop-collapse-btn`/`#tank-collapse-btn`** — replaces Escape as the only way to open the pause menu (`UI.js` wires its click straight to the existing `togglePauseMenu`; `main.js`'s Escape branch no longer calls it at all). **Escape's new job, per direct request:** cancel an actively-armed `build:*`/`demolish`/`merge` tool back to Food (`UI.js`'s new exported `cancelActiveTool`) — a no-op while Food or a fish is selected, since only those three were named. A Fan mid-two-click-aim gets both its pending aim cleared AND the tool itself cancelled (previously Escape only cleared the aim, leaving the Fan still armed). Escape's existing popup-closing precedence (Mound menu, Lab purchase modal, Lab tree, Fan-aim-cancel) is unchanged, just with `cancelActiveTool` as the new fallback instead of `togglePauseMenu`.
- **`#build-legend` — "Click to purchase" / "(Esc) to cancel"** — a small fixed pill just above the bottom tool-bar, shown only while `state.ui.selectedTool` starts with `build:`, toggled every frame from `UI.js`'s `updateHUD`.
- **A real guided-tutorial engine, generalizing the first-alien intro's "pause + spotlight" idea into something reusable, per direct request ("just like the first alien tutorial")** — `state.level.tutorialFlow` (`{ id, step } | null`, level-scoped/reset on restart) is plain data anyone can set directly (`main.js` at Start for `'start'`, `Entities.js`'s `awardTankPoint` on the first-ever Tank Point for `'tankpoint'`, `Systems.js`'s new `updatePostAlienTutorial` — `ALIEN_TUTORIAL_DELAY_MS` (10s) after `state.level.firstAlienKilledAtMs`, set by `Entities.js`'s `updateAlien` the instant the first alien ever dies — for `'postalien'`), same "just set the flag, whoever renders reacts" pattern `firstAlienIntroActive` already established. `UI.js`'s `TUTORIAL_FLOWS` table defines each flow's ordered steps, each with a `getCircle(state)` returning `{ cx, cy, r }` in viewport px — either from a live DOM element's `getBoundingClientRect()` (`tutorialCircleForDom`, so it tracks a panel's own CSS grow-in transition or a window resize for free) or a world point run through `worldToScreen` against the live camera (so it tracks panning too). **The spotlight is a REAL clickable hole, not a synthetic-event hack:** `#tutorial-overlay` (a full-viewport dark div, z-index 700 — above even the pause menu) gets its `clip-path` set every frame to an evenodd SVG path — the full viewport rect MINUS the target circle — which in modern browsers restricts BOTH rendering AND pointer hit-testing to the clipped-in region: a click inside the circle passes straight through to whatever real element sits underneath (the actual Shop button, a species icon, the canvas itself), while anywhere else in the darkened area never reaches anything below it at all. This meant no synthetic/forwarded events were needed anywhere — every step's real action fires through its own already-existing, already-proven handler (`toggleShopCollapse`, a species icon's click, a Tank Upgrade buy button, `trySpawnPurchasedFish`, `placeTile`), each just also calling the new exported `advanceTutorialFlow(state, id, step)` (a no-op unless that exact flow/step is currently active) right after its real action succeeds. `main.js`'s `update()` freezes everything BELOW `updateCamera`/`updateBuildDrag` while a flow is active — deliberately NOT those two, since the post-alien flow's "scroll" step needs panning and its "place" step needs build-drag placement to keep working; the "scroll" step's own advance condition (there's nothing to click for "pan the camera") is checked every tick via a new `isScrolledToBottom(state)` helper mirroring Engine.js's own vertical-clamp formula, resolving instantly (no visible arrow-flash) if the camera was already at the bottom when that step started. The debug-overlay's backtick toggle was moved to fire unconditionally, ahead of every freeze/gate check (including the cinematic alien intro) — it's a pure observability tool, not a gameplay action, and blocking it made both real debugging AND automated testing of a tutorial-frozen state impossible.
  - **Flow 1 (game start):** Shop button -> the Guppy icon specifically -> a spotlight high in the open-water column (`START_TUTORIAL_FISH_SPOT`, deliberately near the TOP of the column rather than dead-center — the Shop panel is still open at this point and its fly-out box grows tall enough to cover a real chunk of the screen's vertical middle, which would otherwise obscure/block a mid-column spotlight) to place the first fish. Ends silently.
  - **Flow 2 (first Tank Point):** Tank Upgrades button -> the Coin Capacity card's buy button. Ends by granting one more Tank Point plus a chat message, "Here's an extra tank point, don't spend it all in one place" — per direct request, deliberately NOT routed through `awardTankPoint` (that has its own unrelated "isFirst" bookkeeping).
  - **Flow 3 (post-first-alien-kill):** Shop button -> the Waste Turret family slot (forced back to its base `turret_waste` tier if the player had previously cycled that family slot to a higher one, since this tutorial always means the base turret specifically) -> a `noSpotlight` step reusing `#scroll-hint`'s existing 5 bouncing arrows (forced visible regardless of their normal 10s/`hasScrolledDown` gating) until the camera reaches the bottom -> a spotlight near the bottom-left of the world (`POST_ALIEN_TURRET_SPOT`, `0.12 * WORLD_W` rather than a tiny fixed offset — has to clear BOTH the camera's own horizontal-centering offset, which would otherwise put a too-small x off-screen entirely, AND the Shop panel's own footprint, since "visible with the shop open" was explicit) for the actual placement, detected in `updateBuildDrag` via `placeTile`'s return value and `BUILDING_FAMILIES.turret.includes(buildingId)` (any tier counts, not just the exact base one, in case the player placed a different tier anyway). Ends with a chat message, "Now that's I'm talking about. A little firepower never hurt no one."
- **Coin Capacity moved to 2nd in the Tank Upgrades list (right after Fish Merging), and its first level's cost cut from 3 to 1 Tank Point** — per direct request, specifically so a player can afford it off the single Tank Point the new Flow 2 tutorial is built around. `Config.js`'s `COIN_CAP_UPGRADE_COSTS` levels 2+ are untouched.
- **Waste resized to 10% bigger than Food** (`WASTE_RADIUS = FOOD_RADIUS * 1.1`, replacing the old standalone "25% bigger than the original 5" formula — now pinned to Food's own radius instead of a magic number) and **aliens poop half as often** (`ALIEN_POOP_INTERVAL_MS` 2000ms → 4000ms) — both per direct request, the latter also further softening the Waste-cap fix's own worst-case production rate from the batch above.
- **Click-and-drag Waste, city only, one piece at a time** — per direct request, mirroring the existing Economy Fish combine-drag pattern (`draggedFishId`/`fishDragArmed` in `main.js`) almost exactly, just for a single Waste item and with no tool requirement (grabbing Waste directly always works, regardless of the selected tool — nothing was asked for gating it behind one, unlike the fish-drag's Merge-tool requirement). A new `input.mouseDownHandlers` entry arms `draggedWasteId` on a press landing within `WASTE_RADIUS * WASTE_DRAG_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER` of an eligible Waste item, restricted to `world.y >= SEABED_FLOOR_Y` (the city); `updateWasteDrag()` (called from `update()`, after `updateEntities` — same "override whatever this tick's normal physics did" ordering `updateFishDrag` already uses) snaps the item's position to the cursor and zeroes its velocity every tick, clamped so it can't be dragged back up above the seabed line. **No changes were needed anywhere else for collisions or Turret/Auto-Feeder absorption to "just work" while dragging:** `Grid.js`'s `resolveItemCollisions` already runs unconditionally over every item every tick regardless of who's currently controlling its position (see "Items can't stack" in this doc), so it naturally pushes every other nearby item out of the way of wherever the dragged Waste currently sits, one tick behind — identical to how a dragged fish already displaces anything it swims through; and a Waste Turret/Auto-Feeder's own intake scan (`Grid.js`'s `updateBuildings`) already just looks for an eligible Waste item within its intake radius each tick with no drag-awareness at all (gated only on "not already mid-process," same as always), so a dragged piece that drifts close enough still gets pulled in and spliced out of `state.level.items` normally — `updateWasteDrag` just needed to notice the item is gone afterward and clear the drag, the same defensive check `updateFishDrag` already has for a fish that starves mid-drag.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario, including two calibration bugs caught and fixed IN THE TEST HARNESS itself rather than the game — worth noting since they cost real time before being correctly diagnosed as test-only: (1) a rapid `'-'` keypress sequence intended to return time-scale to 1x instead overshot to 0x/fully-paused, silently freezing `updateCamera` too since it lives inside the now-never-called `update()`, which looked exactly like "scrolling doesn't work" until traced back; (2) a screen-space probe point for calibrating world<->screen math landed on DOM UI chrome (the bottom tool-bar) rather than the canvas, leaving the debug overlay's `Cursor world` readout silently stale from an much-earlier mousemove instead of erroring): the full game-start flow (miss-click outside the spotlight hole correctly did nothing; the Shop opened through the hole; the Guppy icon selected through its own hole; the final click placed a real fish, `Entities: 0 -> 1`, and the whole overlay/text hid itself immediately after) passed every assertion; the pause button opened/closed the pause menu correctly and Escape no longer did; selecting Platform then pressing Escape correctly reverted to Food and hid the build legend; the Tank Upgrades panel's card order read `Fish Merging, Coin Capacity, Food Quality, Fish Movement, Defensive Capabilities`; both the Tank Point and post-alien tutorial flows (triggered via a temporary debug hook added for this one verification pass and removed immediately after, same as the earlier alien-intro pass's temporary debug line) ran end-to-end correctly, including the exact completion chat messages and the Waste Turret actually landing (`Tile under cursor: turret_waste`); and a live Waste-poop-then-drag test confirmed a dragged Waste item's tracked position followed the cursor precisely (`Cursor world` and the item's own live position matched within 1px) and settled naturally under normal physics the instant it was released.

**A batch of follow-up polish/fixes on top of the guided-tutorial/alien-intro batch above: a 3s post-splash delay before the start tutorial, a softer fish-shimmer fade, a stricter scroll-hint trigger, the cinematic first-alien intro delayed and unified onto the same spotlight engine as every other guided step, a cyclops eye that tracks the nearest fish, alien waves that wait for a full clear before re-arming, a brief no-food zone where an alien just died, and the Rocky Shelf removed entirely so everything falls to the true bottom of the tank** — all per direct request/report, landing in one session right after the batch above.
- **Game-start tutorial now waits `START_TUTORIAL_DELAY_AFTER_SPLASH_MS` (3s) after the splash screen has genuinely finished fading**, not the instant Start is clicked — moved from `initStartScreen`'s `onStart` callback into a `setTimeout` inside `splashTitle`'s own `animationend` handler (`main.js`), so it fires off the real end of the splash's fade-out rather than an unrelated, much-earlier event.
- **The fish shimmer now eases in/out over 0.3s instead of snapping**, per direct report ("kinda jarring") — `Shimmer.js`'s new `shimmerFadeAlpha(t)` computes a symmetric alpha ramp over the first/last `SHIMMER_FADE_MS` (300) of the existing 900ms one-shot sweep, multiplied into `ctx.globalAlpha` at the fish-shimmer call site only (`main.js`) — the Mound/Science Lab's own recurring shimmer, never reported as jarring, is untouched.
- **The scroll-down arrow hint now waits 40s (10s + 30 more) AND requires at least one Coin/Waste/Food actually sitting in the city — both conditions, not either** — per direct request. `UI.js`'s `SCROLL_HINT_DELAY_MS` raised 10000 → 40000; `updateScrollHint` gained a live `state.level.items.some(...)` check alongside it. The post-alien guided tutorial's own forced use of these same arrows (its "scroll" step) is unaffected — it was already exempt from this whole gate, for its own separate reason (see that step's own comment).
- **The cinematic first-alien intro no longer fires the instant the alien spawns — it waits `ALIEN_INTRO_DELAY_MS` (1s) of it actually being alive and visibly moving on screen first**, per direct request. `Entities.js`'s `updateAlienPortals` now just records `state.level.firstAlienIntroAppearedAtMs` (and which alien, `firstAlienIntroTargetId`) the instant it spawns; a new `Systems.js` check, `updateAlienIntroTrigger` (called from `updateStoryTriggers`, so it runs every tick during that 1s window same as normal play), starts the flow once the delay elapses (or abandons it silently if the alien somehow already died in that window, rather than soft-locking on a target that no longer exists).
- **Unified onto the exact same guided-tutorial engine as every other step, per direct report that it "seemed different"** — the old bespoke canvas `destination-out` radial-gradient spotlight (drawn directly in `main.js`'s `render()`) is gone entirely, replaced by a new one-step `alienintro` flow in `UI.js`'s `TUTORIAL_FLOWS`, whose `getCircle` tracks the alien's live world position via `worldToScreen` (frozen in place the whole time, so it doesn't actually need to re-track motion, just recomputes off the live camera like every other step). The one real behavioral difference from every other flow — camera panning also needs to freeze here, unlike the post-alien flow's "scroll" step — is handled by a dedicated early check in `main.js`'s `update()`, positioned before `updateCamera` even runs, specifically for `tutorialFlow.id === 'alienintro'`. The click-to-damage logic itself no longer has ANY special-cased first-check block in the click handler — it's now just the ordinary "clicking any living alien always damages it" loop (which already existed for ordinary post-intro alien combat), with one line added (`advanceTutorialFlow(state, 'alienintro', 'click')`) so a hit on the intro alien specifically also ends the flow; the overlay's own clip-path hole is what already restricts a click to only ever reach the intro alien in the first place, so no other gating was needed.
- **Aliens now have one big cyclops eye instead of two, with a pupil that tracks the nearest fish** — per direct request. `main.js`'s `drawAlienBody` replaces the old two-red-circle eyes with a single larger one (`radius * 0.34`, centered where the two used to sit) plus a dark pupil offset toward a `gazeAngle` parameter — a plain `Math.atan2` toward whichever fish is closest to the alien, computed fresh each frame in the render loop (a cheap O(n) scan, bounded by `ALIEN_MAX_ALIVE`) and passed in; falls back to looking straight ahead (the alien's own facing direction) if no fish exist at all.
- **The countdown to the NEXT alien wave no longer starts until every alien from the CURRENT one is dead** — per direct request ("make sure the timer for the next alien wave doesn't start until all the aliens are killed"), fixing a real gap where `alienNextWaveAtMs` used to reschedule the instant a wave spawned, letting a fresh wave "reinforce" a lingering survivor before the player finished it off. `Levels.js`'s new `state.level.alienWaveActive` (set true the moment `spawnAlienWave` fires) gates `Systems.js`'s `updateAlienWaves` at the very top — while true, it checks only whether the wave's portals have all opened AND every alien it produced is dead; the real countdown (`alienNextWaveAtMs = elapsed + randomWaveIntervalMs()`) only gets (re)computed once both are true, "starting fresh" from that exact moment rather than from whenever the wave originally spawned.
- **A 1-second Food-placement dead zone right where an alien just died**, per direct request ("so you don't accidentally place 4 food after killing a fish" — a rapid kill-click flurry often had a couple of leftover clicks land right where the alien had been, planting unwanted Food there). `Entities.js`'s `updateAlien` death branch pushes `{ x, y, expiresAtMs }` (radius = the alien's own former click radius, `ALIEN_RADIUS * ALIEN_CLICK_RADIUS_MULTIPLIER`, duration `ALIEN_FOOD_BLOCK_DURATION_MS` = 1000ms) into a new `state.level.alienFoodBlockZones` array; `trySpawnFood`'s new `isInAlienFoodBlockZone` check (which also lazily prunes expired zones as a side effect of running, rather than needing its own per-tick age/cull pass) refuses placement inside any still-active zone, returning a new `'alien_zone'` reason main.js's click handler already silently no-ops on (same as the existing `'in_city'` case — no flash, nothing else needed).
- **The Rocky Shelf — the old mid-height hard barrier splitting the seabed into a visually and physically distinct "city" (above) and "underground" (below) — is gone entirely**, per direct request: "remove the upper and lower sections of the city... make it all the same section... food, money, waste, and science should all fall to the very bottom of the tank." `Grid.js`'s `sweepVertical` moved its hard stop from the old `ROCK_SHELF_Y` (4 tiles above the world's absolute bottom) down to `WORLD_H` itself — the exact same "nothing ever gets permanently lost" safety-net mechanism, just relocated to the true floor, so `stepItemOnGrid`'s `'lost'` status stays exactly as unreachable through normal gravity as it already was. The jagged `renderRockShelf` rock-ledge visual, its own module-level jitter-point cache, and the separate `getUndergroundTexturePattern`-filled second rect it used to sit between are all removed from `renderSeabedGrid`; what's left of the old two-tone look is purely a single `ctx.createLinearGradient` on the ONE unified seabed fill, running from the old city color at the top (`SEABED_ROW_START`) to the old underground color at the bottom (`WORLD_H`) — "have the color of the city gradient from top to bottom, with the top being the color of the upper city and the bottom being the color of the lower city," read completely literally. `ROCK_SHELF_Y` itself is deleted from `Config.js` (nothing references it any more).
- **The bottom tool-bar's visual buffer strip grew another 20%** (`CAMERA_BOTTOM_BUFFER_PX` 105 → 126) — per direct request, "now that everything will fall to the bottom of the tank" (previously most drops settled well above the buffer, at the old Rocky Shelf line; now they can genuinely reach all the way down near it) — **and its texture was toned down**, per direct report ("slightly less aggressive/obtrusive"): `Grid.js`'s `renderCameraBottomBuffer` lightened its gradient's darkest stop and narrowed the overall range (`#2e2115`/`#1c150e`/`#0a0705` → `#332619`/`#241a10`/`#160f09`), halved the texture overlay's opacity (0.3 → 0.18), softened the ambient glow (0.16 → 0.1 alpha), and dimmed both halves of each rivet's own bevel (shadow 0.5 → 0.3, highlight 0.35 → 0.22 alpha) — same gradient/rivet/glow structure as before, just quieter.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario tested): the start tutorial stayed fully hidden at 5.5s post-Start and was showing by ~9s, confirming the 3s-after-splash delay; the seabed now renders as one continuous top-to-bottom gradient with no visible boundary line or texture break, and a Waste item settled to rest near the world's true bottom edge instead of the old shelf height (screenshot-confirmed); the first-alien intro's overlay stayed hidden immediately after the alien appeared, only becoming visible ~1s later with the identical `path(evenodd, ...)` clip-path format every other tutorial step uses, camera panning provably froze during it (`Camera: 158, 0` unchanged across 15 ArrowDown presses) while a click landed correctly on `#game-canvas` through the hole and cleared the flow; a cropped screenshot centered on the intro's own spotlight circle clearly showed one large red cyclops eye with an off-center dark pupil, not the old two-eye layout; forcing a second alien wave while a first one's survivors were still alive left the entity count completely unchanged across a further 4s wait, confirming the new-wave gate holds regardless of the debug `Y` cheat forcing `alienNextWaveAtMs` to "now"; killing an alien and immediately attempting a Food placement at its exact last tracked position (a temporary debug-overlay line exposing it live, added for this one verification pass and reverted immediately after) left the item count unchanged, while an identical click ~1.2s later successfully placed one — confirming both the block and its expiry.

**A Waste Turret intake fix, a real "object in front of the Mound" click-priority fix, a much lighter/bigger-text Shop preview, the scroll hint gained real wording and a real arrow, the build legend moved off the bottom of the tank, guided tutorials can no longer get stranded on the wrong tool, Escape now also closes the Shop/Tank Upgrades panel, and a round of new SFX (Turret shots, alien hits/deaths, Processor/Auto-Feeder intake and output)** — all per direct request/report, landing in one session right after the batch above.
- **Waste now gets pulled into a Waste Turret from genuinely any side it's touching, corners (including resting on top) included** — per direct report. Root cause: the intake check (`isNearBuildingCenter`, a flat radius-from-center test shared with the Collector/Auto-Feeder) sized its radius close to but just short of the tile's own corner distance (`TILE_SIZE*0.5*sqrt(2)` ≈ 22.6px against a ≈20.8px radius), so Waste resting near a corner or the top edge — a very normal place for it to land — read as "not close enough" even though it was visibly touching the tile. `Grid.js`'s new `isTouchingBuildingTile` replaces it for the Turret's own ammo-intake check specifically (Collector/Auto-Feeder untouched, not named in the request) with a real circle-vs-square distance test — the distance from the item's center to the CLOSEST point on the tile's square footprint, which correctly covers every corner regardless of where exactly the item landed. `TURRET_INTAKE_RADIUS` is retired (no longer referenced anywhere).
- **A coin or Science Bubble sitting in front of (overlapping) the Mound/Science Lab's own click area now gets the click first, every time** — per direct request ("if there's an object in front of the mound, have the click be consumed on the object first and ignore the mound"). `main.js`'s click handler simply swapped the order of two existing, already-correct checks: `tryBankCoinAt`/`tryBankScienceAt` now run BEFORE `isPointOnMound`/`isPointOnScienceLab` (previously the other way around) — no new logic needed, since both checks already existed and were each independently correct, just in the wrong relative order.
- **The Shop preview panel got noticeably lighter on text and bigger on font**, per direct report ("too much small text... reduce the amount of text in everything and increase the size of the font"). `UI.js`'s `speciesStatsHtml` dropped its "🎭 Role" and "💰 Cost" lines entirely — per direct request, since the live price already shows right next to the fish's name — now just delegating straight to the shared `fishEconomyStatsHtml` (Hunger/Money/Waste/Speed). `style.css` bumped every size in the preview block up a notch: `#shop-preview-name` 14px → 17px, `#shop-preview-desc`/`#shop-preview-hint` 11px → 13px, `.building-stat` 10.5px → 13px.
- **The scroll-down hint gained real wording and a real arrow**, per direct request ("have the text 'Scroll down' also show up... change it so it's an actual arrow instead of just a triangle"). `index.html`'s `#scroll-hint` restructured into a small column — a new `#scroll-hint-text` pill ("Scroll down," same instructional-pill styling `#tutorial-text`/`#alien-countdown` already use) above a row of arrows — and each `▼` (a plain Unicode triangle) swapped for `⬇️` (a real arrow emoji), matching this game's existing emoji-heavy icon language elsewhere.
- **The build legend ("Click to purchase" / "(Esc) to cancel") moved from just above the bottom tool-bar to top-middle**, per direct follow-up report that it was covering the bottom of the tank now that everything falls all the way down there. `style.css`'s `#build-legend` moved from `bottom: 100px` to `top: 70px` (same slot logic as `#pause-toggle-btn`'s own placement below `#hud` — stacked under `#alien-countdown`/`#tutorial-text`'s own `top: 14px` row, not overlapping it).
- **Guided tutorials can no longer get stranded on the wrong tool** — a real, reported break: the post-alien flow's "scroll" step hides the ENTIRE spotlight overlay (`noSpotlight: true`, since there's nothing to click for "pan the camera"), which meant nothing was stopping a stray click from reaching the bottom tool-bar and selecting Demolish (🔨) there — stranding the following "place" step with no build tool armed at all, since Demolish doesn't place anything. Two-part fix, per direct request ("the hammer/glove shouldn't be able to be chosen during tutorials, and make sure the correct tool is selected at the start of each step"): (1) `UI.js`'s `isDemolishToolAvailable`/`isMergeToolAvailable` both now also require `!state.level.tutorialFlow` — Demolish/Merge are genuinely unselectable (grayed out AND inert, both via their hotkey and their button) for the ENTIRE duration of any guided tutorial flow, not just the one step that broke; (2) every step in `TUTORIAL_FLOWS` now carries an explicit `tool` field, re-asserted every single frame that step is active (`updateTutorialOverlay`, the same function that already runs every frame to position the spotlight) rather than only once on entry — self-healing the same way `isFanAimingActive()` already is, so whatever else might have nudged `selectedTool` away gets corrected right back on the very next frame regardless of how it happened.
- **Escape now also closes the Shop/Tank Upgrades panel**, per direct request — `UI.js`'s existing `closeSidePanels` helper (previously used by `openLabMenu`/`selectTool`) is now also exported and called from `main.js`'s Escape handler, right alongside the existing tool-cancel behavior.
- **New SFX: a Turret firing, an alien getting hit vs. actually dying (two distinct sounds), and an item getting pulled into / dispensed out of a Processor/Auto-Feeder/Waste Turret** — per direct request. `Sound.js` gained a small private `playSweep` helper (a real oscillator frequency ramp, not two fixed tones stitched together) for a punchier "pew," used by the new `playTurretShoot` (a Turret firing, wired into `Grid.js`'s `updateBuildings` turret-fire branch) and `playIntake` (a soft rising whoosh — wired into all three intake sites: the Collector's `beginCollectorProcessing` call, the Waste Turret's own ammo-refill, and the Auto-Feeder's Waste absorption). `playAlienHit` (a short sharp impact) and `playAlienDeath` (a bigger descending burst) replace what used to be silent alien-damage events — wired into both damage-application sites (`main.js`'s click-damage loop, `Entities.js`'s `updateTurretProjectiles` impact) and the one death site (`Entities.js`'s `updateAlien`), with each hit site checking `hp > 0` after applying damage so a KILLING hit only ever plays `playAlienDeath` (fired independently the next tick from the death branch), never both sounds stacked on the same blow. `playDispense` (a soft falling pop) covers the Auto-Feeder's Food output and — since it was silently missing a completion sound entirely, unlike the coin path's existing `playCoinBank` — a Processor finishing a Science Bubble's hold; deliberately NOT added to the coin-banking path, which already has its own dedicated blip and doesn't need a second sound stacked on top.
- Verified end-to-end in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors across every scenario): the Shop preview's stats block read exactly `Hunger/Money/Waste/Speed` with no Role/Cost line, at a confirmed `13px` computed font size; the build legend's live bounding box sat at `y: 70` (top), not the bottom; `#scroll-hint-text` read exactly "Scroll down" and the arrow's own text content was confirmed as the `⬇️` emoji, not `▼`; pressing Escape with the Tank Upgrades panel open closed it (`collapsed` class present immediately after); Demolish/Merge both read as genuinely `disabled` the instant a guided tutorial started (confirmed on a real building-placed, Demolish-normally-available state, so the disable is provably caused by the tutorial, not incidental unavailability), pressing the `2` hotkey mid-tutorial left the tool at `food` rather than switching to `demolish`, and — reproducing the exact originally-reported break — directly clicking the Demolish button while the post-alien flow's tool-armed step was active left `Selected tool: build:turret_waste` completely unchanged both before and after the click, confirming the self-healing per-frame enforcement actually holds under the exact scenario that broke it originally.

**The 3 base feeders' coin production reworked from "gets faster AND more valuable with growth" to "same rate as adult the whole time, only the value climbs ~2x," the Shop shows that as a real baby-to-adult range instead of just the adult figure, and Guppy's adult coin got a flat boost to $15** — per direct request.
- **`Config.js`'s Guppy/Dartfin/Blimpfish `growthStages`:** every stage's `dropInterval` is now the SAME value — the species' own existing adult interval (Guppy 19382ms, Dartfin 9382ms, Blimpfish 29259ms, all unchanged) — so growing up no longer speeds up production at all any more, only raises the payout. `dropValue` now scales 0.5x (baby) → 0.75x (juvenile) → 1.0x (adult) of each species' adult value, replacing Guppy/Dartfin's old completely-flat-across-stages tables and Blimpfish's old mild 16→17→22 creep. Dartfin's adult stayed $3 and Blimpfish's stayed $22 (not named in the request); **Guppy's adult was bumped from $7.5 to a flat $15**, per direct request — its baby/juvenile values follow the same 0.5x/0.75x split off that new $15 anchor ($7.5/$11.25).
- **`UI.js`'s `fishEconomyStatsHtml`** now shows Money as `$babyRate - $adultRate/min` instead of just the adult figure — computed from each stage's own `dropValue`/`dropInterval` independently (not assuming they share the same interval, even though they now do), so the range stays correct even if a future change makes the interval vary by stage again.
- Verified live in a browser (Playwright, zero console errors): the Shop preview read exactly `$23.22 - $46.43/min` for Guppy, `$9.59 - $19.19/min` for Dartfin, and `$22.56 - $45.11/min` for Blimpfish — each an exact 2.0x baby-to-adult ratio, and Guppy's adult end (`$46.43/min = $15 per coin ÷ 19382ms × 60000`) confirming the $15 boost landed correctly.
